denominator is the squared distance to the ENTIRE minimum set.

Accepted fact **04219554e41d9750** supplies the original benchmark
recurrences and the positive finite ray limit:

    Omega(a,b)=(a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2,
    G(a,b)=2a+b/2+sqrt(b^2+8ab)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,
    B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m -> Z_1 in (0,infinity).         (1.1)

The maxima include ALL integer splits. No seed hypothesis from that
fact is assumed. For the numerical constant in the deficit we prove a
new elementary scalar bound B_n<=(62/25)(n+1)^rho in Section 9,
where rho=log_2(lambda). It avoids using an unverified transcription
of saved interval endpoints and avoids evaluating the recurrence.

The complete earlier records d54d97705d464425, 63d6dc5d659fd7a4 and
a488f62fb6c05ea8 were read. They are awareness only. Sections 2--4
below rederive the body, smooth envelope and exact sorting law; the
earlier unverified projection law is not an accepted premise. The old
paired start and all first-update-core starts are excluded from the
present construction. We do not retune that paired family.

Primary orientation was Francis Bach, *Learning with Submodular
Functions: A Convex Optimization Perspective*, arXiv:1111.6453v2
(2013), https://arxiv.org/html/1111.6453v2 . The exact downloaded local
text at workers/max/downloads/bach_1111.6453v2.txt was available;
Proposition 3.2 and its greedy-support proof, and Appendix A.3's
ordered quadratic projection proof, were read. The relevant concepts
are the base polyhedron of a normalized submodular function and
decreasing Euclidean isotonic regression. Our rank function is NOT
nondecreasing, so positive-polymatroid assertions are not substituted.
All mathematical ingredients actually used are proved below.

Matlas returned 2106.13683 Proposition 4.1 as related orientation on
the proximal map of pairwise absolute differences. Attempts to open
its arXiv HTML v2 and v1 returned "Cache miss". No statement from that
paper is used. A separate Matlas query for the scalar power envelope
returned unrelated covariance/polynomial lemmas; none is imported.
This is a continuation of the same rank-body program, with the exact
local primary source read, not a new literature branch rejected
without examination.

## 2. The unchanged Euclidean body and its support

Let e be the all-ones vector, E_d={x:<e,x>=0}, and

    a_i=(d+1-2i)/d,             1<=i<=d.

If the two disjoint differences of sets A and B have sizes p and q,
expanding the cardinality quadratic gives

    F_d(A)+F_d(B)-F_d(A union B)-F_d(A intersection B)=2pq/d>=0.

Also F_d(empty)=F_d([d])=0 and

    sum_(i=1)^k a_i=k(d-k)/d.                         (2.1)

Every permutation of a is in K_d because its largest k-coordinate
sum is (2.1). Singleton and complementary-singleton constraints give
|g_i|<=(d-1)/d for g in K_d. Thus K_d is nonempty and compact.

If w_(pi(1))>=...>=w_(pi(d)), summation by parts gives

    <w,g>
      =sum_(k<d)(w_(pi(k))-w_(pi(k+1)))
                           sum_(i<=k)g_(pi(i))
      <=sum_(k<d)(w_(pi(k))-w_(pi(k+1))) k(d-k)/d
      =sum_i a_i w_(pi(i)).                          (2.2)

The permutation g_(pi(i))=a_i attains this bound. It follows that

    K_d=conv{permutations of a},
    sigma_d(w):=max_(g in K_d)<w,g>
      =sum_i a_i w_(pi(i))=(1/d)sum_(i<j)|w_i-w_j|.   (2.3)

For the convex-hull equality, if a point g of K_d were outside the
hull, let v be its closest hull point. Differentiating squared
distance along segments gives <g-v,t-v><=0 for all hull points t.
The direction g-v would then strictly separate g from that hull,
contradicting (2.2) and its attainment. The last equality in (2.3)
counts the d+1-2i occurrences of the i-th sorted coordinate.

The body is centrally symmetric since -a is a permutation of a.
It contains a relative neighborhood of zero in E_d: for centered g
and a set of size k,

    |sum_(i in A)g_i|<=||g|| sqrt(k(d-k)/d).

Since k(d-k)>=d-1 for 1<=k<d, the ball in E_d of radius
sqrt((d-1)/d) satisfies every constraint. A strictly smaller radius
satisfies every proper constraint strictly. All norms here and below
are the original coordinate Euclidean norms.

## 3. One static smooth function and its whole minimum set

Define the single static function

    Phi_d(x)=max_(g in K_d){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.          (3.1)

Let P_d(x) be the unique closest point of x in K_d. Completing the
square identifies the maximizer in (3.1) as P_d(x). The closest-point
condition, proved by expanding squared distances along segments, is

    <x-P_d(x),g-P_d(x)><=0  for every g in K_d.        (3.2)

Adding this condition at x and x' gives

    <x-x',P_d(x)-P_d(x')>>=||P_d(x)-P_d(x')||^2.

Consequently P_d is 1-Lipschitz. Evaluating each maximum in (3.1)
at the other maximizer shows

    0<=Phi_d(x')-Phi_d(x)-<P_d(x),x'-x>
       <=<P_d(x')-P_d(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.

This proves differentiability and

    grad Phi_d(x)=P_d(x),
    Phi_d(x)=(||x||^2-||x-P_d(x)||^2)/2.             (3.3)

Convexity follows from the maximum representation. There is no
dimension factor in smoothness: on a neighborhood of zero in E_d,
P_d(x)=x, so its optimal global Lipschitz constant is exactly 1.

Writing bar(x)=d^(-1)sum_i x_i, the full minimum set is

    argmin Phi_d=R e,  min Phi_d=0,
    dist(x,argmin Phi_d)^2=||x-bar(x)e||^2.            (3.4)

Indeed Phi_d>=0 by g=0 and Phi_d(te)=0 because K_d is centered.
For nonzero z=x-bar(x)e, a sufficiently small epsilon in (0,2)
has epsilon z in K_d. Its value in (3.1) is
(epsilon-epsilon^2/2)||z||^2>0, excluding all other minimizers.
The distance assertion is orthogonal projection onto the line.

For any advertised L>0 use f=L Phi_d. Its actual GD with steps h_t/L
is x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t P_d(x_t). The body is not rescaled and h_t is
not changed. Initial normalization below divides by the exact radius
in (3.4); no fictitious unit-distance start is imposed.

## 4. Exact interval-average law, including sorting and all subset rows

For sorted coordinates y_1>=...>=y_d put z_i=y_i-a_i. For a contiguous
interval [p,q], let av_(p,q)(z) be its arithmetic mean. The exact
residual and projection in these coordinates are

    u_i=min_(1<=p<=i) max_(i<=q<=d) av_(p,q)(z),
    g_i=y_i-u_i.                                    (4.1)

Here is a full proof of the needed ordered projection assertion.
Minimize ||u-z||^2/2 on the decreasing cone u_1>=...>=u_d. Existence
and uniqueness follow from coercivity and strict convexity. Define
R_k=sum_(i<=k)(u_i-z_i), R_0=0. Perturbation by a constant vector,
increase of any prefix, and small decrease at a strict jump give

    R_d=0,  R_k>=0,  R_k(u_k-u_(k+1))=0.             (4.2)

Conversely, these conditions imply optimality: for any decreasing v,

    <u-z,v-u>
      =sum_(k<d) R_k[(v_k-v_(k+1))-(u_k-u_(k+1))]>=0.

Expansion of the squared objective now proves the converse.
On a maximal constant block [b,c] of u with level t, (4.2) implies
R_(b-1)=R_c=0, t=av_(b,c)(z), every prefix of that block has average
at most t, and every suffix has average at least t. For i in the
block, choosing p=b in (4.1) makes all its q-averages at most t,
with equality at q=c; later blocks have no larger levels. Conversely
for every p<=i, choosing q=c gives an average at least t, using the
suffix of the first encountered block and any intervening blocks.
This proves the min-max formula (4.1).

The vector g=y-u is centered and obeys

    sum_(i<=k)g_i=k(d-k)/d-R_k<=k(d-k)/d.             (4.3)

It is decreasing. Inside a constant block its differences are those
of y. At a block end c the suffix/prefix inequalities give
z_c>=u_c and z_(c+1)<=u_(c+1), whence
g_c>=a_c>a_(c+1)>=g_(c+1). Thus (4.3) implies every subset bound,
not just prefix bounds. Finally, for v in K_d,

    <u,v-g>=sum_(k<d)(u_k-u_(k+1))sum_(i<=k)(v_i-g_i)<=0:

every strict jump is at a saturated prefix of g by (4.2), and v
obeys the same body's upper bound. This is (3.2), proving that g
is the true Euclidean projection. Undoing the sorting permutation
proves the assertion for unsorted x. Equal coordinates cause no
ambiguity, since uniqueness fixes their equal projected coordinates.

For an arbitrary physical step, if pi_t maps sorted ranks to original
coordinate labels, the actual update is

    v_(t,i)=(1-h_t)y_(t,i)+h_t u_(t,i),
    y_(t+1,k)=v_(t,sigma_t(k)),
    pi_(t+1)(k)=pi_t(sigma_t(k)),                    (4.4)

where sigma_t sorts v_t, breaking ties by original labels. This is
only a state permutation; no physical step is reordered. The frozen
profile below admits a proof that pi_t stays the original lexicographic
order, with successively larger tie blocks. We establish that claim
before using it, rather than assuming that symmetry preserves ranks.

## 5. The one frozen profile and the unchanged physical word

For the principal result fix m>=3 and tau in [1/5,1/4]. Put

    d=2^m, n=2^m-1,
    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-tau/m,
    c=lambda(1+tau/m), alpha=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j),
    b_j=1+alpha c^j, S_j=sum H_j,  0<=j<m.           (5.1)

Every child uses these SAME enclosing (m,tau) parameters. The actual
word is H_m=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)). Define its actual prefix masses

    L_t=sum_(i<t)h_i,  0<=t<=n,  M=2S_m+1.           (5.2)

The symbol L_t is a prefix mass; L without subscript remains the
advertised smoothness. Concatenation proves that the first occurrence
of level j is physical step index 2^j-1, the preceding word is H_j,
and therefore

    L_(2^j)=S_j+b_j=:P_j,      0<=j<m.              (5.3)

For example this is the UPDATE COUNT 2^j, not the query just before
the bridge. Subsequent copies of any level retain every one of their
steps. The all-index formula is h_t=1+alpha c^(nu_2(t+1)), where
nu_2 counts factors of 2. This follows because adding 2^j to a
positive integer smaller than 2^j preserves its smaller valuation
in the right child. Every h_t is strictly greater than 1.

Label the d physical coordinates by epsilon=(epsilon_1,...,epsilon_m)
in {+1,-1}^m, with +1 preceding -1 in lexicographic order. This label
has rank

    i(epsilon)=1+sum_(k=1)^m ((1-epsilon_k)/2)2^(m-k).

The arithmetic rank vector in this fixed physical labeling is

    a(epsilon)=sum_(k=1)^m 2^(-k)epsilon_k.           (5.4)

The vectors epsilon_k, viewed as d-coordinate columns, are centered
and orthogonal: <epsilon_k,epsilon_l>=d if k=l and zero otherwise.
This follows by pairing labels that differ in one sign. We do not
replace the physical Euclidean metric by a metric on m coefficients.

For 2<=k<=m set

    T_k=2^(m-k+1),
    q_1(0)=M, q_k(0)=L_(T_k)=P_(m-k+1).

The ONE initial profile frozen in plan b4edb4877ec644b4 is

    x_0(epsilon)=sum_(k=1)^m 2^(-k)q_k(0)epsilon_k
       =(M/2)epsilon_1
         +sum_(k=2)^m 2^(-k)P_(m-k+1)epsilon_k.      (5.5)

There is no adjustable amplitude or numerical scan in (5.5). In
particular this is not the previous paired-rank profile. The extinction
times are T_m=2,T_(m-1)=4,...,T_2=2^(m-1), all specified directly
from the original physical hierarchy. The first mode survives the
whole word; the other modes have successively different lifetimes.

The exact trajectory statements in Sections 6--7 in fact hold for
all m>=2 and 0<tau<m. The interval in the principal result is needed
only for the uniform limiting comparison, not to assert non-core entry.

## 6. Actual ordered blocks and every nonlinear transition

For every query 0<=t<=n define

    q_1(t)=M-L_t,
    q_k(t)=L_(T_k)-L_t  if t<T_k,
    q_k(t)=0           if t>=T_k,           2<=k<=m.  (6.1)

Let r(t) be the number of positive q_k(t). Its formula is

    r(0)=m,
    r(t)=m-floor(log_2 t),                 1<=t<=n.  (6.2)

We claim that the actual iterate, gradient and residual are

    x_t(epsilon)=sum_(k<=r(t))2^(-k)q_k(t)epsilon_k,
    g_t(epsilon)=sum_(k<=r(t))2^(-k)epsilon_k,
    u_t(epsilon)=sum_(k<=r(t))2^(-k)(q_k(t)-1)epsilon_k.
                                                               (6.3)

We first prove the projection assertion for these displayed states,
then verify the physical recurrence. For an active k>=2, the prefix
mass still to be taken includes the last step at its fixed lifetime:

    q_k(t)>=h_(T_k-1)=b_(m-k+1)>1   whenever t<T_k.   (6.4)

Also q_1(t)>=S_m+1>1. All active coefficients are strictly ordered

    q_1(t)>q_2(t)>...>q_(r(t))(t)>1.                 (6.5)

For k>=2 their successive differences are positive differences of
the fixed prefix masses L_(T_k). The first difference is positive
because M>L_(T_2). Hence, for any active k,

    2^(-k)(q_k-1)>sum_(l=k+1)^r 2^(-l)(q_l-1),
    2^(-k)q_k    >sum_(l=k+1)^r 2^(-l)q_l.           (6.6)

For r=k the right side is zero. Otherwise use q_l<q_k and the
strict finite geometric bound sum_(l>k)^r 2^(-l)<2^(-k).

At the first differing active sign, (6.6) proves that the coordinate
with +1 is larger. Thus BOTH x_t and u_t are strictly lexicographic
in the first r signs, and constant on each block of labels with those
signs fixed. There are exactly 2^r consecutive maximal residual
blocks, each of size ell=2^(m-r). Within each block, g_t in (6.3)
is precisely the average of a: the last m-r signs in (5.4) average
to zero. The same is true of the average relation

    u_t|_[b,c]=av_(b,c)(x_t-a).

For a prefix of length s inside such a block, whose size is ell,
the residual R in (4.2) has the within-block value

    sum_(i=b)^(b+s-1)(a_i-av_(b,c)(a))=s(ell-s)/d.   (6.7)

This follows by summing the arithmetic sequence with gap 2/d.
It is nonnegative and returns to zero at s=ell. At every boundary
u_t jumps strictly by (6.6). Consequently (4.2) holds on the whole
ordered coordinate list. Section 4 now proves that the displayed
g_t is exactly P_d(x_t), satisfying every original subset constraint
and the full normal-cone condition. This is also an explicit solution
of every min-max in (4.1) at these queries.

The starting state is (5.5). Suppose (6.3) is the actual state at t<n.
The original step x_t-h_t g_t subtracts h_t from every active q_k.
For k>=2 still active after the step this yields
L_(T_k)-L_(t+1), exactly (6.1). If t=T_k-1, it yields
h_(T_k-1)-h_t=0 exactly. Already inactive modes stay zero. The first
mode becomes M-L_(t+1). These are precisely the next coefficients
in (6.1), whose true projection has just been proved. Induction proves
(6.3) for EVERY physical query and every physical update.

In particular, at the first occurrence of every level 1<=j<m,

    t=2^j-1: k=m-j+1 is active and q_k(t)=b_j,
    step h_t=b_j: q_k(t+1)=0,
    t+1=2^j: r drops from m-j+1 to m-j.               (6.8)

Each pair of adjacent old residual blocks differing in this last
active sign becomes one larger block. Its rank-gradient component
2^(-k)epsilon_k disappears. The other modes remain positive and
correctly ordered. There are m-1 such events at the original physical
update counts 2,4,...,2^(m-1), INCLUDING the largest root bridge.
After that bridge, the entire final H_(m-1) is retained and is described
by (6.1) with r=1; its constant remaining gradient is integrated over
every step, without replacing the earlier nonlinear history by a mass.

The original lexicographic permutation is valid throughout. New ties
are broken in that same label order; the proof (6.6), not symmetry
alone, excludes any unreported crossing or reversal. Residual block
counts are d,d/2,...,2 at the corresponding transition states. They
never merge to the one-block quadratic core. The gradients at these
states span the whole m-dimensional span of epsilon_1,...,epsilon_m,
since consecutive gradients differ by the nonzero orthogonal vector
2^(-k)epsilon_k. Thus this is a growing nonlinear trajectory, not one
constant-gradient exterior ray with a relabeled scalar amplitude.

Most directly, every query is genuinely outside K_d. For the top
half A={epsilon:epsilon_1=+1}, (6.3) gives

    sum_(epsilon in A)x_t(epsilon)=d q_1(t)/4>d/4=F_d(A).
                                                               (6.9)

The lower signs cancel on this set. This proves non-core status at
t=1 and throughout the final tail, including t=n, without a no-core
hypothesis. Although the final tail is one exterior mode, there is no
quadratic-core entry anywhere in the constructed trajectory.

## 7. Original metric, all ordered interpolation rows, and terminal loss

All x_t are centered. Thus zero is their closest minimizer on the
entire line (3.4), and the true initial squared radius is

    R_m^2=d [M^2/4+sum_(k=2)^m 4^(-k)P_(m-k+1)^2]
         =(d/4)(M^2+D_m),
    D_m=sum_(j=1)^(m-1)4^(j-m)(S_j+b_j)^2>0.         (7.1)

Every factor d comes from the actual Euclidean orthogonality in
Section 5. There is no uncharged dimension or coefficient-space
normalization. At any query the value is exactly

    Phi_d(x_t)=d sum_(k<=r(t))4^(-k)(q_k(t)-1/2).     (7.2)

This follows by substituting the true gradient in (3.1). At the
terminal point r(n)=1, q_1(n)=S_m+1, so

    x_n=(S_m+1)epsilon_1/2,
    g_n=epsilon_1/2,
    Phi_d(x_n)=d(2S_m+1)/8=d M/8.                   (7.3)

It follows that the actual normalized payoff of f=L Phi_d is

    V_(m,tau)=2f(x_n)/(L dist(x_0,argmin f)^2)
             =M/(M^2+D_m)
             =1/[M(1+E_m)],  E_m=D_m/M^2>0.         (7.4)

This also gives W_n(H_m)>=V_(m,tau) as a legal single-function lower
witness. The upper bounds on V below cannot be reversed into upper
bounds on W_n.

For clarity all original interpolation rows are paid directly. Put
F_i=Phi_d(x_i), G_i=g_i, Y_i=x_i-g_i and add the closest minimizer
sample x_star=G_star=F_star=0. For EVERY ordered pair of samples,

    Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<G_i,x_j-x_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2
        =<G_i-G_j,Y_j><=0.                          (7.5)

The identity follows by substitution of F_i=<x_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.
The inequality is (3.2) at j, since G_i belongs to K_d. It includes
all distant, incoming-terminal, outgoing-terminal and optimum pairs.
Their Gram matrices are PSD because these are actual vectors in R^d.
No pair or vector coordinate has been deleted.

The scalar unit-threshold Huber function H(u), equal to u^2/2 for
|u|<=1 and |u|-1/2 for |u|>=1, is a legal 1-smooth comparison on the
SAME word. Started at M=2S_m+1, it stays above 1 with state M-L_t.
Its exact true-distance terminal payoff is 1/M. Therefore

    V_(m,tau)/(1/M)=1/(1+E_m)<1                      (7.6)

for every m>=2 and 0<tau<m, not merely in the asymptotic diagnostic
interval. The disappearing modes contribute their complete original
squared radii to D_m and contribute zero terminal value. Orthogonality
prevents their initial costs from cancelling. This is the specific
loss mechanism of the frozen profile.

The same finite algebra explains the whole scope of this failure
mechanism, without selecting a second start: take m>=2, d=2^m, any
length-n word with all steps>1, and integer lifetimes
n>=T_2>...>T_m>=1. Write L_t for its true prefix masses and S=L_n.
For the profile Q epsilon_1/2+sum_(k=2)^m 2^(-k)L_(T_k)epsilon_k,
where Q>S+1, the proof of (6.3) applies with these prefix masses.
Writing D=sum_(k=2)^m 4^(1-k)L_(T_k)^2, its exact payoff is

    [2Q-(2S+1)]/[Q^2+D],

with D>0 the paid lower-mode radius contribution. It is strictly less
than 1/(2S+1), because the difference after positive cross multiplication
is (Q-(2S+1))^2+D>0. This scoped observation excludes rescuing this
extinction mechanism merely by increasing its surviving top amplitude.
It does not cover starts with surviving lower modes, overshoots, new
rank permutations, or general non-core points of the same body. The
selected profile remains exactly (5.5), with no repair or retuning.

## 8. Closed mass formulas and a quantitative growing-dimension limit

Put

    zeta=alpha/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)],  u=1-zeta.

For x=tau/m in (0,1),

    c(c-2)=1+(4+2sqrt(2))x+lambda^2 x^2>1>beta,

so 0<zeta<1 and u>0. The defining concatenation has
S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+alpha c^j. Direct substitution at j=0 and in this
identity proves, symbolically for every j,

    S_j=zeta c^j+u 2^j-1,
    P_j=S_j+b_j=zeta(c-1)c^j+u 2^j,
    M=2zeta c^m+2u 2^m-1.                          (8.1)

No recurrence has been executed. Let A=zeta(c-1). Summing the three
geometric series in the square of P_j gives the finite closed formula

    D_m=4^(-m){
         A^2 [(4c^2)^m-4c^2]/(4c^2-1)
       + 2Au [(8c)^m-8c]/(8c-1)
       + u^2 [16^m-16]/15 }.                        (8.2)

All j from 1 through m-1, including the root transition, appear in
this identity. It keeps every radius contribution; none has been
discarded as a small-coordinate or short-child error.

Here is explicit uniform control of its growing terms. On
tau in [1/5,1/4] and m>=3, c lies in the compact interval
[lambda,13lambda/12], zeta is bounded away from zero, and

    c=lambda+O(1/m), zeta=1+O(1/m), u=O(1/m),        (8.3)

with constants independent of tau. The displayed polynomial for
c(c-2) proves all of these estimates, including the positive lower
bound for zeta. Define

    delta_m=(u/zeta)(2/c)^m,
    epsilon_m=1/(2zeta c^m).

Then M=2zeta c^m(1+delta_m-epsilon_m). Dividing (8.2) by
4zeta^2 c^(2m), its three terms become respectively

    (c-1)^2/[4(4c^2-1)] * [1-(4c^2)^(1-m)],
    (c-1)u/[2zeta(8c-1)] * (2/c)^m * [1-(8c)^(1-m)],
    u^2/[60zeta^2] * (2/c)^(2m) * [1-16^(1-m)].      (8.4)

To obtain E_m, divide their sum by
(1+delta_m-epsilon_m)^2. This denominator tends uniformly to 1
and is bounded away from zero for all sufficiently large m. Every
coefficient in (8.4) is uniformly bounded. The second and third terms
have the indicated exponentially decaying factors; the omitted piece
of the first is also exponentially small. Thus

    E_m=eta+O(1/m),
    eta=(lambda-1)^2/[4(4lambda^2-1)]
       =1/[2(4lambda^2-1)]>0,                       (8.5)

uniformly on the tau interval. This proves in particular that the
paid radius of the nonlinear modes is a nonzero limiting fraction,
not an infinitesimal decoration of a scalar ray.

For completeness, log(1+x) lies between x-x^2/2 and x for x>=0;
this follows by integrating 1/(1+x) between 1-x and 1. Hence

    exp(tau-tau^2/(2m)) <= c^m/lambda^m <= exp(tau).

Together with (8.1)--(8.3), this gives, uniformly in tau,

    M/lambda^m=2exp(tau)+O(1/m),
    lambda^m V_(m,tau)
       =exp(-tau)/[2(1+eta)]+O(1/m).                (8.6)

There is one constant K, independent of tau and m, bounding the
absolute error in the second line by K/m for sufficiently large m.
The geometric errors above are bounded by a constant times 1/m;
this follows, for any fixed 0<r<1, from convergence of sum m r^m.
Thus this is a quantitative bound on all accumulated growing-family
terms, not a formal interchange of a growing sum and a limit.

Multiplying by the accepted convergent scalar ray in (1.1) proves
(A), uniformly on [1/5,1/4]. More explicitly its uniform error is
bounded by

    (62/25)K/m + K' |B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m-Z_1|,        (8.7)

for a fixed K', using the fully proved scalar envelope in the next
section. No quantitative rate for the accepted ray is assumed.

## 9. An elementary all-split scalar envelope and absolute deficit

This section is part of the same JOINT UNVERIFIED argument. Its
scalar bound is proved independently of saved numerical endpoints.
Set rho=log_2(lambda), p=rho/2 and kappa=62/25. One has p>5/8,
because lambda^4=17+12sqrt(2)>32 (use sqrt(2)>5/4).

First the following elementary power inequality holds:

    (25/31)x^(2p)+(1-x)^p<=1,
                0<=x<=1, p>=5/8.                   (9.1)

Increasing p can only decrease the left side, so consider

    psi(x)=(25/31)x^(5/4)+(1-x)^(5/8).

Its endpoint values are 1 and 25/31. An interior critical point obeys

    x^(1/4)(1-x)^(3/8)=31/50,
    x^2(1-x)^3=(31/50)^8.                           (9.2)

The function x^2(1-x)^3 decreases for x>=2/5. For x>=5/8,

    x^2(1-x)^3<=675/32768<21/1000
        <(147/1000)^2<(31/50)^8.                    (9.3)

The fixed rational comparisons use 675000<688128,
21000<21609, and
(31/50)^4=923521/6250000>147/1000 since 923521>918750.
Thus every interior critical point lies below 5/8.

At a critical point, the derivative equation makes

    psi(x)=(1-x/2)/(1-x)^(3/8)=:g(x).

The logarithmic derivative of g has the sign of 5x-2:

    g'(x)/g(x)=(5x-2)/[8(1-x)(2-x)].

Consequently its maximum on [0,5/8] is at an endpoint. Now g(0)=1
and g(5/8)<1, the latter being equivalent to

    11^8=214358881<226492416=27*2^23.

The continuous function psi attains a maximum either at an endpoint
or at such an interior critical point. This proves (9.1).
All these are displayed symbolic rational inequalities, not results
of an executed calculation or a numerical evaluation.

We also give the primitive power inequality rather than assume a
new recurrence fact. For positive a,b let A=a^(1/rho), D=b^(1/rho),
T=(A+D)^rho. Put E(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. The function
z -> log E(exp z) is convex: its second derivative is

    rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-2)
       *[(1+t)^rho-1-rho*t]/E(t)^2 >=0,  t=exp z,

because the bracket vanishes at zero and has positive derivative
for t>0. Convexity at opposite arguments gives

    E(t)E(1/t)>=E(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2.

It follows that (T-a)(T-b)>=2ab, or
T^2-(a+b)T-ab>=0. Since T is positive, it lies above the positive
root Omega(a,b). Zero arguments follow by continuity. Induction over
ALL original primitive splits therefore proves

    C_n<=(n+1)^rho.                                 (9.4)

We claim the corresponding dominant bound

    B_n<=kappa(n+1)^rho,              n>=0.          (9.5)

The case n=0 is 1<=kappa. In an arbitrary split at n>=1 put
A=k+1, D=n-k and N=A+D=n+1. By induction and (9.4), the two
arguments of G are at most A^rho and kappa D^rho. The function G
is increasing in both nonnegative arguments, directly from its formula.
Its value G(a,b) is the square of the positive root v of
v^2-sqrt(b)v-2a=0. Therefore it is at most T=kappa N^rho if

    2A^rho+kappa D^(rho/2)N^(rho/2)<=kappa N^rho.

On dividing by kappa N^rho, this is exactly (9.1) with x=A/N
and p=rho/2, since 2/kappa=25/31. It holds for EVERY split, so
taking their original maximum proves (9.5), without balancing or
deleting any split. In particular, (1.1) gives

    Z_1<=62/25.                                    (9.6)

It also gives an explicit finite all-depth upper law for the chosen
actual instance:

    B_(2^m-1)V_(m,tau)
       <=(62/25)lambda^m M/(M^2+D_m),               (9.7)

where M,D_m are the closed expressions (8.1)--(8.2). This is not
an upper bound for unrestricted W_n(H_m).

To obtain an absolute benchmark deficit, lambda<5/2 implies
eta>1/48. For every tau in [1/5,1/4], positivity of the exponential
series gives exp(tau)>=exp(1/5)>1+1/5+1/50=61/50. Thus

    Z_1 exp(-tau)/[2(1+eta)]
       <(31/25)(50/61)(48/49)
       =2976/2989<249/250.                          (9.8)

The last comparison is 744000<744261 after multiplication by
the positive denominators. Uniform convergence in Section 8 now
supplies a single finite integer M_0>=3 such that

    B_(2^m-1)V_(m,tau)<249/250
       for EVERY m>=M_0 and EVERY tau in [1/5,1/4].  (9.9)

No numerical value of M_0 is asserted. At tau=1/4, use
exp(1/4)>1+1/4+1/32=41/32 instead. The limit then satisfies

    Z_1 exp(-1/4)/[2(1+eta)]
       <(31/25)(32/41)(48/49)
       =47616/50225<19/20.                          (9.10)

The final comparison is 952320<954275. Hence at tau=1/4 the
actual finite scaled payoff is below 19/20 at every sufficiently
large depth. This sharper single-tau statement retains the SAME
frozen profile, body and physical word convention.

## 10. Exact failure scope and what remains open

The argument does NOT exclude other non-core starts of this SAME
arithmetic-rank body. In particular, starts with genuine rank crossings,

It does not optimize the all-word interval-average law, does not give

There was ONE selected profile and ZERO mathematical repairs. No

# Three nonlinear correlated laws: complete analytical screens

Status: JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Worker max2, invocation60,
under TASK.md, plan e570a4da874393de, master_guidance ca0fd834c90cd90a
and elaboration f5f79a115a09ff55. The original exact-profile problem is OPEN.
No mathematical program, numerical evaluation, arithmetic replay,
optimizer, solver, executed trajectory or recurrence, benchmark DP,
formal submission, installation, consult or subagent was used.

This is one allocated analytical round, after ended baseline59, with
ended ceiling60/attempted ceiling61. It does not renew itself.

## 1. What was screened, and what the conclusions mean

Three explicit laws leave the old aligned affine elapsed-clock class.

1. A parabolic longitudinal mean has a nonzero initial transverse
   gradient and a transverse sign reversal. A path of ORIGINAL rows
   from the initial optimum constraint through every physical step
   forces every legal radius1 payoff below 1/[1+(8/3)S] whenever
   max h_t <= S/2. This includes the unchanged tau=1/4 hierarchy.
   The terminal row alone is not an adequate screening test here.
2. Accumulated centered projective-incidence innovations have an exact
   real Gram, a parabolic longitudinal mean and an arbitrary initial
   transverse offset. The palindrome moment identity pays the complete
   terminal covariance charge. If the offset makes that charge negative,
   the first original two-row cycle forces a larger scalar payment.
   Under explicit conditions containing the assigned hierarchy, every
   legal radius1 payoff is strictly below the same-word Huber payoff.
3. One static nonorthogonal Huber ridge plus a quadratic background is
   globally legal, sharp1-smooth and has a unique minimum. It has actual
   queried gate changes, noncommuting Hessians and a terminal gradient
   with negative initial-radius component. A fully explicit three-step
   bridge beats the same-word Huber value. A different four-step word
   sends the entire fixed scale family to its minimum exactly. Zero
   padding therefore defeats that whole family at arbitrarily large n.

The first two are rejected by NECESSARY original-row inequalities before
asserting that their entire proposed data arrays are realizable. No
expected-feasibility or unsigned-Gram argument is used to claim a
function. The third is an actual static function, with every original
row, the terminal value, and the true closest radius proved below.
The first two upper bounds apply only to their declared origin-anchored,
radius1 law classes. They are not upper bounds on unrestricted W_n(h).
The third annihilator is a counterexample to that fixed witness family,
not a small-error upper bound on unrestricted W_n for its word.

Every new assertion is proved jointly here and remains unverified.
No unverified shared finding is a mathematical premise. Accepted
1252b8823bb5a290 supplies interpolation necessity/sufficiency and its
explicit conjugate construction. Accepted5fd396eba75651e7 is used ONLY
for its Section10 original benchmark comparison. Acceptedb98bb807d514e80d
is used for the original benchmark definitions and scalar single-cut
comparison. Accepted0e7e78056dc6258c was read to avoid returning to its
expected-orthogonality class; its upper bound is not applied to these laws.

## 2. The original word, all directed rows, and an explicit data convention

Normalize L=1 and take the optimum sample x_star=g_star=f_star=0.
For arbitrary finite h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) >=0 define

    S=sum_(t<n)h_t, P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, q_i=P_i/S.        (1)

For S>0, q_0=0, q_n=1, and q_(i+1)-q_i=h_i/S. Zero steps
repeat q and must repeat positions, gradients and values. All expressions
below are written finite sums; none was executed by a mathematical program.
For S=0 one can use the separate static fallback F(x)=||x||^2/2,
x_0=e0, with every iterate unchanged, unique minimum0 and payoff1.
That degenerate fallback repairs no failure on the positive-mass words.

For any displayed normalized gradient-vector law v_i and scale sigma>0,
use the complete original physical arrays

    g_i=sigma*v_i,
    D_i^nc=sum_(t<i)h_t*v_t,
    x_i=e0-sigma*D_i^nc,                 0<=i<=n.     (2)

These satisfy x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i, including every zero step.
The original signed interpolation residual, with its original orientation,
is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.       (3)

EVERY ordered distinct pair in {star,0,...,n} is retained. In particular,
no incoming terminal or remote queried-prefix row is discarded.

Here is an explicit common value convention for attempted laws1 and2:

    C_i^val=sum_(t=i)^(n-1) (h_t/2)*<v_t+v_(t+1),v_t>,
    f_i=tau_val+sigma^2*C_i^val, C_n^val=0.           (4)

For these values every finite ordered row is EXACTLY

    Q_ij/sigma^2 = C_i^val-C_j^val
                  +<v_i,D_i^nc-D_j^nc>+||v_i-v_j||^2/2. (5)

Both optimum directions are EXACTLY

    Q_(i,star)=tau_val-sigma*<v_i,e0>
        +sigma^2*(C_i^val+<v_i,D_i^nc>+||v_i||^2/2),
    Q_(star,i)=-tau_val+sigma^2*(||v_i||^2/2-C_i^val). (6)

Thus the full optimum-value interval, not only the terminal upper row, is

    lower_i=sigma^2*(||v_i||^2/2-C_i^val),
    upper_i=sigma*<v_i,e0>
         -sigma^2*(C_i^val+<v_i,D_i^nc>+||v_i||^2/2),
    max_i lower_i <= tau_val <= min_i upper_i.        (7)

If all finite rows(5) pass, an explicit maximal choice is
tau_val=min_i upper_i, provided it is at least max_i lower_i.
These formulas specify every position, gradient, value and original-row
cost, rather than supplying separately chosen values for different tests.
For any directed finite cycle, its exact cost is the sum of the displayed
terms in(5); the C_i^val terms telescope. For cycles involving star,
use(5),(6), where tau_val also telescopes. No unknown expectation enters.

Only if ALL those signs pass may the accepted interpolation construction
be invoked. Explicitly, with the zero jet included, put

    K(v)=max{||v||^2/2,
             max_(0<=i<=n)[<x_i,v>-f_i+||v-g_i||^2/2]},
    F(x)=sup_v[<x,v>-K(v)].                           (8)

Fact1252b8823bb5a290 proves that passing all rows realizes these exact
jets by ONE everywhere finite convex differentiable1-smooth F with
minimum0. The Gram is the Gram of the actual displayed Euclidean vectors.
If additionally tau_val>max_i lower_i, every nonzero-jet quadratic is
strictly below the zero-jet quadratic at v=0. Finitely many continuous
quadratics then leave K(v)=||v||^2/2 in a neighborhood of0. The
subdifferential of K at0 is {0}, so argmin F={0}. Consequently the true
closest initial distance is ||e0||=1, and smoothness1 is sharp locally.

Laws1 and2 require that strict condition for a claimed TRUE-radius1
realization. No existence of a passing array is assumed in their
performance screens. Their bounds actually allow ALL common values,
not merely(4). Thus changing(4) cannot repair the stated performance
failures. If a different realization has a closer minimum, the present
origin-radius upper bound is not asserted for that different normalization.

For advertised L>0 use L*F and physical steps h_i/L. The normalized
payoff is still 2f_n/dist(x0,argmin F)^2, exactly W=2R.

## 3. Law1: a parabolic mean and a transverse sign reversal

In R^2, with orthonormal e0,e1, choose the fixed coefficients

    delta_par=1/4, nu_par=27/8, b_par=9/10,
    v_i^par=(1-delta_par*q_i^2)e0
                         +sqrt(nu_par)*(q_i-b_par)e1. (9)

Use(2) for all positions. Written componentwise these are

    x_i=[1-sigma*P_i+sigma*delta_par*U_i]e0
                       -sigma*sqrt(nu_par)*(J_i-b_par*P_i)e1,
    U_i=sum_(t<i)h_t*q_t^2, J_i=sum_(t<i)h_t*q_t.     (10)

Use(4)--(7) for one explicit value array and its ALL-row/cycle costs.
The full gradient Gram and the initial-gradient correlations are

    <e0,g_i>=sigma*(1-q_i^2/4),
    <g_i,g_j>=sigma^2*[(1-q_i^2/4)(1-q_j^2/4)
                         +(27/8)(q_i-9/10)(q_j-9/10)]. (11)

This is a real PSD rank2 Gram, not independent directions. At i=0 the
transverse component is negative and nonzero. At i=n it is positive.
The longitudinal mean is quadratic in elapsed mass, so this is not
another scalar retuning of the affine-clock law. Initial gradient and
initial radius are not aligned.

### 3.1 The original optimum-to-terminal path pays the gain

Consider ANY common values which make these gradients and positions legal
with the origin optimum and x0=e0. The row Q_(0,star)<=0 gives

    f_0<=sigma-sigma^2*||v_0^par||^2/2.

For every physical t, the actual original row Q_(t+1,t)<=0 gives

    f_(t+1)<=f_t-h_t*sigma^2*<v_(t+1)^par,v_t^par>
                              -sigma^2*||v_(t+1)^par-v_t^par||^2/2.

Summing ALL these rows, including the terminal row at t=n-1, yields

    2f_n<=2sigma-D_path*sigma^2,
    D_path=||v_0^par||^2
       +2sum_t h_t*<v_(t+1)^par,v_t^par>
       +sum_t||v_(t+1)^par-v_t^par||^2.              (12)

This is the concrete replacement for the terminal-only screen. It uses
the initial optimum row, every directed physical predecessor row and
the full finite-amplitude vector law. No value re-selection avoids it.

### 3.2 Exact cross quadrature, without a trajectory evaluation

For a written partition interval [q,q+d] with 0<=q<=q+d<=1, define

    v(q)=(1-delta_par*q^2)e0+sqrt(nu_par)*(q-b_par)e1.

Polynomial integration gives the EXACT identity

    integral_q^(q+d)||v(t)||^2 dt -d*<v(q),v(q+d)>
       =d^3*[(delta_par+nu_par)/3
                      +delta_par^2*(q^2+q*d+d^2/5)]. (13)

For the transverse term, this is the equality
integral(q-b_par)^2-d(q-b_par)(q+d-b_par)=d^3/3.
For the longitudinal term, expanding 1-2delta_par*t^2+delta_par^2*t^4
gives delta_par*d^3/3+delta_par^2*d^3*(q^2+qd+d^2/5).
Thus(13) retains the entire error, not a Taylor remainder.

Since q+d<=1, q^2+qd+d^2/5<=1. Also

    integral_0^1||v(t)||^2dt
       =1-2delta_par/3+delta_par^2/5
                        +nu_par*(1/3-b_par+b_par^2)
       =4001/2400>5/3,
    (delta_par+nu_par)/3+delta_par^2=61/48<4/3.       (14)

Write d_t=h_t/S and d_max=max_t d_t. Because sum_t d_t=1,
sum_t d_t^3<=d_max^2. Summing(13) over the EXACT original partition,
with zero intervals contributing zero, proves

    (1/S)sum_t h_t*<v_t^par,v_(t+1)^par>
                    >5/3-(4/3)*d_max^2.             (15)

If max h_t<=S/2, the right side is at least4/3. Furthermore
||v_0^par||^2=1+nu_par*b_par^2>1 and the last sum in(12) is nonnegative.
Consequently

    D_path>1+(8/3)S,
    2f_n<=1/D_path<1/[1+(8/3)S]<1/(1+2S)=H_base(h). (16)

The middle inequality follows by completing the square in(12):
2sigma-D_path*sigma^2=1/D_path-D_path*(sigma-1/D_path)^2.
The bound is over ALL scales and ALL legal common values. In particular
it also holds for every successful strict-radius1 realization in(8).

### 3.3 Exact original benchmark comparison, at the actual horizon

Keep the assigned enclosing parameters, without substituting those of a child:

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=1-1/(4m),
    c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a_word=beta_word/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_word*c^j,H_j),
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, m>=1536.                         (17)

The word is a palindrome and its largest entry is the central
1+a_word*c^(m-1). It satisfies max h_t<S/2. To check the latter
without any mass computation, c<3 and m>=3 give

    2S_(m-1)>=2*(1+a_word*c^(m-2))
                         +4*(1+a_word*c^(m-3)),
    2S_(m-1)-(1+a_word*c^(m-1))
       >=5+a_word*c^(m-3)*(2c+4-c^2)>0.             (18)

Here 2c+4-c^2>0 on 1<c<3. Since S=2S_(m-1)+h_central,
the desired largest-step comparison follows.

The ORIGINAL benchmark is U_n^ZJ=1/B_n with ALL split indices retained.
Accepted5fd396eba75651e7, Section10, gives at THIS actual n

    B_n*H_base(H_m)<237/250,                         (19)

so every legal law1 radius1 payoff V satisfies the exact comparison

    V/U_n^ZJ=B_n*V
        < [B_n*H_base(H_m)]*(1+2S)/[1+(8/3)S]
        <237/250<1.                                 (20)

Law1 therefore cannot match the benchmark even on this original full
word. This rejects its fixed nonlinear coefficients; it excludes no
arbitrary nonlinear mean law or terminal longitudinal reversal.

## 4. Law2: accumulated real finite-field covariance with initial memory

### 4.1 The incidence design is a real simplex, with its metric proved

Choose a prime p_ff such that N_ff=p_ff^2+p_ff+1>=n+1, and put
d_ff=N_ff-1. Such a prime exists: any prime divisor of (n+1)!+1 is
greater than n+1. This is an existence argument, not a performed
factorial evaluation or prime search.

Over the field Z/p_ff Z, projective points are nonzero triples modulo
nonzero scalar multiples. Each class has p_ff-1 representatives, giving
N_ff=(p_ff^3-1)/(p_ff-1) points. A projective line is a nonzero linear
form modulo nonzero scalar multiples, with incidence defined by the
vanishing of that form. There are N_ff lines as well.

The kernel of one nonzero linear form has p_ff^2 elements. Removing zero
and passing to scalar classes gives p_ff+1 points per line. Two distinct
line forms are linearly independent; their common kernel has dimension1,
so their lines share exactly one projective point. These counts follow
by eliminating a coordinate with a nonzero coefficient, and eliminating
two coordinates for the pair. No external finite-geometry theorem is
being imported.

In REAL R^(N_ff), let 1_L be the incidence vector of line L and 1 the
all-ones vector. Put k_ff=p_ff+1 and

    u_L^ff=[1_L-(k_ff/N_ff)*1]
                     /sqrt(k_ff-k_ff^2/N_ff).        (21)

Their real inner products, by the proved counts, are exactly

    ||u_L^ff||^2=1,
    <u_L^ff,u_M^ff>=-1/d_ff for L!=M.               (22)

Indeed the distinct numerator inner product is
1-k_ff^2/N_ff=-p_ff/N_ff, while the squared denominator is
(p_ff+1)*p_ff^2/N_ff. Their quotient is -1/[p_ff(p_ff+1)].
Their Gram is therefore PSD because these are explicitly real vectors;
there is no conversion of a finite-field bilinear form into an assumed
Euclidean metric. For arbitrary real coefficients z_L the exact norm is

    ||sum_L z_L*u_L^ff||^2
      =[N_ff*sum_L z_L^2-(sum_L z_L)^2]/d_ff.        (23)

It is nonnegative also directly by Cauchy--Schwarz, extending a selected
subset's coefficients by zero to the N_ff lines.

Choose n distinct step lines and one further line, denoted u_t^ff
(0<=t<n) and u_extra^ff. Add a unit e0 orthogonal to R^(N_ff).

### 4.2 An explicit nonlinear all-history law

Choose sigma>0, nu_inc>0, 0<delta_inc<=1/2 and an ARBITRARY real
initial offset b_inc. Define

    z_i^inc=b_inc*u_extra^ff+(1/S)sum_(t<i)h_t*u_t^ff,
    v_i^inc=(1-delta_inc*q_i^2)e0+sqrt(nu_inc)*z_i^inc. (24)

Use ALL physical arrays(2) and the full common-value convention(4)--(7).
In coordinates the positions are

    x_i=[1-sigma*P_i+sigma*delta_inc*U_i]e0
                        -sigma*sqrt(nu_inc)*sum_(t<i)h_t*z_t^inc,
    U_i=sum_(t<i)h_t*q_t^2.                          (25)

Zero steps add no innovation, so repeated queries have identical gradients.
The covariance is time dependent and retains every earlier innovation.
It is not an independent fresh-gradient construction or an AR nearest-
neighbor assumption. The initial gradient has a transverse component
unless b_inc=0. Opposite temporal inner products are allowed.

Write E_i=sum_(t<i)h_t^2, and r_min=min(i,j). The exact covariance is

    <z_i^inc,z_j^inc>=b_inc^2
      -b_inc*(q_i+q_j)/d_ff
      +[N_ff*E_(r_min)/S^2-q_i*q_j]/d_ff.            (26)

The entire Gram of (x0,g0,...,gn), including all initial correlations,
is determined by(24)--(26), with

    <x0,g_i>=sigma*(1-delta_inc*q_i^2),
    <g_i,g_j>=sigma^2*[(1-delta_inc*q_i^2)
                       *(1-delta_inc*q_j^2)
                              +nu_inc*<z_i^inc,z_j^inc>]. (27)

The initial displacement is EXACTLY e0; a successful strict realization
of(8) has unique minimum0 and true radius1. No minimum is moved without
changing these law hypotheses. All pair/cycle costs are(5),(6) with
(24), not an expected sign or an unspecified combinatorial feasibility
claim.

### 4.3 Complete terminal charge, with every covariance term retained

Let A_inc=1-delta_inc>0 and set

    U=sum_t h_t*q_t^2<=S/3,
    T=sum_t h_t*P_t=(S^2-E)/2, E=sum_t h_t^2,
    eta=T/S, K_inc=1+S+eta, M=1+2S.

The bound on U is the left-endpoint inequality on each interval of the
nondecreasing function(P/S)^2, whose integral from0 to S is S/3.
The formula for T is obtained by expanding the square of sum h.

The original row Q_(n,star)<=0, for ANY legal common values, yields

    2f_n<=2sigma*A_inc-D_inc*sigma^2,
    D_inc=M*A_inc^2+2delta_inc*A_inc*(S-U)
                                   +nu_inc*C_inc(b_inc),
    C_inc(b)=||z_n^inc||^2+2sum_t h_t*<z_n^inc,z_t^inc>.
                                                            (28)

Direct use of(26) gives the complete initial-offset polynomial

    C_inc(b)=M*b^2-2K_inc*b/d_ff+C_inc(0),
    C_inc(0)=[N_ff*(E+2J_inc)/S^2-1-2eta]/d_ff,
    J_inc=sum_t h_t^2*sum_(r>t)h_r.                 (29)

No positive and negative cross term has been discarded. In particular
allowing initial transverse correlation can make C_inc(b) negative;
asserting nonnegativity for every b would be a wrong shortcut.

### 4.4 A palindrome identity pays the zero-offset part

For a palindrome h, reflection of the exact physical indices gives

    2J_inc=S*E-F3, F3=sum_t h_t^3.                 (30)

Proof: sum h_t^2 times prefix mass equals the suffix-mass sum by
reflection. Adding those sums gives sum h_t^2(S-h_t)=S E-F3.

Put p_t=h_t/S, e=sum p_t^2, f=sum p_t^3. For ANY nonnegative
p_t with sum p_t=1 the following exact identity holds:

    (n+1)e-nf-1
       =sum_(i<j)(p_i-p_j)^2*(1-p_i-p_j)>=0.       (31)

To verify it, sum(p_i-p_j)^2=n e-1 and
sum(p_i-p_j)^2(p_i+p_j)=n f-e. Subtract these two equalities.
Each last factor is nonnegative because all remaining p's are nonnegative.
Since N_ff>=n+1, e-f>=0 and N_ff e>=1, it follows that

    (N_ff+1)e-N_ff f-1>=0, N_ff E-S^2>=0.

Multiplying the numerator of C_inc(0) in(29) by S^2 and using(30)
gives exactly

    N_ff*E-S^2
      +S*[(N_ff+1)E-N_ff*F3/S-S^2]>=0.             (32)

Thus C_inc(0)>=0 for EVERY palindrome, including zero entries. This is
a proved coefficient inequality, not covariance domination by an
unverified temporal kernel.

### 4.5 The first original two-row cycle pays a negative initial-offset charge

Let h_lead be the first positive physical step. All earlier zero steps
leave q=0, z=b_inc*u_extra^ff and x=e0. Summing the TWO original rows
at this actual step cancels their values and gives exactly

    Q_(lead,lead+1)+Q_(lead+1,lead)
      =sigma^2*(h_lead/S)^2
          [delta_inc^2*(h_lead/S)^2
                    +nu_inc*(1-b_inc*S/d_ff)-delta_inc*h_lead]. (33)

The innovation has length h_lead/S, and its inner product with the
initial transverse offset is -b_inc/d_ff. This proves(33) directly,
including the initial correlation term instead of presuming it vanishes.
Legal original rows require the square bracket to be nonpositive.

Assume the explicit screen conditions

    h is a positive-mass palindrome, S>=2,
    0<h_lead<=2, d_ff>=4, S<=d_ff^2/4.              (34)

If C_inc(b_inc)>=0, (28) immediately gives D_inc>M*A_inc^2.
If C_inc(b_inc)<0, (29),(32) imply

    0<b_inc<2K_inc/(d_ff*M)<=2/d_ff,

because eta<=S/2 makes K_inc<=M. Consequently
1-b_inc*S/d_ff>=1-2S/d_ff^2>=1/2. The necessary original cycle(33)
then forces

    delta_inc*h_lead>=nu_inc/2,
    delta_inc>=nu_inc/4.                            (35)

Meanwhile completing the square in(29) gives

    C_inc(b_inc)>=-K_inc^2/(d_ff^2*M)>=-M/d_ff^2.

Since A_inc>=1/2, S-U>=2S/3 and S>=2, the COMPLETE correction in(28)
is bounded below, using(35), by

    2delta_inc*A_inc*(S-U)+nu_inc*C_inc(b_inc)
      >=nu_inc*S/6-nu_inc*M/d_ff^2
      >=nu_inc*S*(1/6-5/32)=nu_inc*S/96>0.          (36)

Here M=1+2S<=(5/2)S and d_ff>=4. Thus BOTH covariance phases give

    D_inc>M*A_inc^2,
    2f_n<=A_inc^2/D_inc<1/M=H_base(h).              (37)

Completion of the square in(28) proves the second line for every sigma.
This is a uniform screen over b_inc in ALL of R, every nu_inc>0 and
0<delta_inc<=1/2, and all legal common values. The coefficient payment
is not an amplitude or value tuning inside the old affine clock.

### 4.6 Conditions and exact benchmark on the original full hierarchy

For h=H_m in(17), h_lead=1+a_word<2 and the palindrome property holds.
We have a_word<=c-2: c>=lambda implies c(c-2)>=1>beta_word.
Induction on j then gives

    1+S_(j+1)=2(1+S_j)+a_word*c^j<=c^(j+1),
    S=S_m<c^m<3^m.                                 (38)

Also n=2^m-1>=2^(m-1), and for m>=10 the exact integer comparison
3^10<4^8, followed by multiplying the left/right by3/4 at each
increment, gives 3^m<4^(m-2)<=n^2/4. Thus
S<=n^2/4<=d_ff^2/4. The remaining conditions S>=2,d_ff>=4 hold
already for m>=3. In particular ALL of(34) hold for m>=1536.

At the actual n=2^m-1, use ONLY the accepted comparison(19):

    V_inc(H_m)/U_n^ZJ=B_n*V_inc(H_m)
                           <B_n*H_base(H_m)<237/250. (39)

No finite-field expectation, remote-cycle deletion or Gram-positivity
guess is a premise. Successful realizations would be static by(8), with
true radius1 under the declared strict condition; even such realizations
cannot improve the benchmark here. This closes ONLY law(24), its stated
coefficient range and its origin-anchored radius convention. It does not
exclude arbitrary signed incidence weights, nonlinear longitudinal
profiles, different initial anchors or terminal longitudinal reversals.

## 5. Law3: a fully legal changing-gate function with a terminal sign change

### 5.1 Static model, sharp smoothness and true radius

For delta_gate>0 define the symmetric Huber scalar

    Hub_delta(t)=t^2/2                    if |t|<=delta_gate,
                 delta_gate*|t|-delta_gate^2/2 otherwise,
    clip_delta(t)=max(-delta_gate,min(delta_gate,t)).

The ONE static function on R^2 is

    F_gate(x,y)=x^2/3+Hub_delta(x+y)/4.              (40)

Its gradient is

    grad F_gate(x,y)=(2x/3+clip_delta(x+y)/4,
                                      clip_delta(x+y)/4). (41)

The function is finite, differentiable and convex. It vanishes exactly
at x=0,x+y=0, so its unique minimum is(0,0). Start at
x0_gate=(0,2delta_gate), whose TRUE closest radius is2delta_gate.

Let e_x=(1,0), a_gate=(1,1), lambda_gate=2/3, kappa_gate=1/4.
The global Hessian upper matrix is

    M_gate=lambda_gate*e_x*e_x^T+kappa_gate*a_gate*a_gate^T
                       =[[11/12,1/4],[1/4,1/4]],
    I-M_gate=[[1/12,-1/4],[-1/4,3/4]]>=0.           (42)

The last matrix has nonnegative diagonal entries and determinant0.
For two points the derivative difference is obtained by integrating
matrices lambda_gate e_x e_x^T+theta kappa_gate a_gate a_gate^T,
with theta in[0,1], along the connecting segment. Each such matrix is
PSD and bounded by I, so grad F_gate is1-Lipschitz.
Near0 the Hessian is exactly M_gate, whose eigenvalues are1 and1/6:
its trace is7/6, its determinant1/6, and I-M_gate is singular.
Therefore1 is the SHARP global smoothness. No loose normalization is
used to create the finite gain.

### 5.2 Arbitrary words, complete data and ALL original cycles

For arbitrary h>=0 the actual full physical trajectory is defined by

    c_i=clip_delta(x_i+y_i),
    x_(i+1)=(1-2h_i/3)*x_i-(h_i/4)*c_i,
    y_(i+1)=y_i-(h_i/4)*c_i,
    g_i=(2x_i/3+c_i/4,c_i/4),
    f_i=x_i^2/3+Hub_delta(x_i+y_i)/4,               (43)

including the actual terminal g_n,f_n. This is the exact law of ONE
fixed function(40), not a different Hessian or oracle chosen at each
step. The equations are symbolic definitions, not executed recurrences.
Every zero step simply repeats the whole jet.

There is an exact ALL-pair cost formula. For any two samples i,j,
including the optimum jet, put p_i=x_i+y_i, d_x=x_j-x_i,
d_c=c_j-c_i, and

    B_Hub(i,j)=Hub_delta(p_j)-Hub_delta(p_i)-c_i*(p_j-p_i).

Direct substitution in the original orientation(3) gives

    Q_ij=-(lambda_gate/2)*d_x^2-kappa_gate*B_Hub(i,j)
             +||lambda_gate*d_x*e_x+kappa_gate*d_c*a_gate||^2/2. (44)

The scalar clip is nondecreasing1-Lipschitz. For p_j>p_i its Bregman
area is integral_(p_i)^(p_j)(clip_delta(t)-clip_delta(p_i))dt.
On the final subinterval of length d_c, monotonicity and Lipschitz1
bound that area below by the triangle area d_c^2/2. Reversing the
ordered endpoints uses the initial subinterval and gives the same bound.
Hence B_Hub(i,j)>=d_c^2/2 for every ordered pair.

Let A_gate have columns sqrt(lambda_gate)e_x and sqrt(kappa_gate)a_gate.
Because A_gate*A_gate^T=M_gate<=I, its operator norm is at most1.
Applying it to (sqrt(lambda_gate)d_x,sqrt(kappa_gate)d_c) gives

    ||lambda_gate*d_x*e_x+kappa_gate*d_c*a_gate||^2
                         <=lambda_gate*d_x^2+kappa_gate*d_c^2.

Thus(44) proves Q_ij<=0 for EVERY original directed pair and BOTH
optimum directions, with no finite-row omission. Summing(44) gives the
exact cost of any directed cycle and shows it is nonpositive.
The full Gram is automatically PSD from these actual R^2 vectors.
The true radius is already known from the unique minimum, not chosen
after examining a formal Gram.

### 5.3 A finite bridge which genuinely leaves the old sign restriction

Take h=(3/2,12,3/2), whose actual length is n=3. All coordinates and
gradients in the following table are divided by delta_gate; values are
divided by delta_gate^2.

| i | position (x_i,y_i)/delta_gate | (x_i+y_i)/delta_gate | g_i/delta_gate | f_i/delta_gate^2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | (0,2) | 2 | (1/4,1/4) | 3/8 |
| 1 | (-3/8,13/8) | 5/4 | (0,1/4) | 15/64 |
| 2 | (-3/8,-11/8) | -7/4 | (-1/2,-1/4) | 23/64 |
| 3 | (3/8,-1) | -5/8 | (3/32,-5/32) | 49/512 |

The exact substitutions in(41),(43) verify every entry. Queries0,1
are on the positive plateau, query2 on the negative plateau, and the
terminal is inside the quadratic region. The background Hessian
diag(2/3,0) and M_gate do not commute: their off-diagonal products in
the two orders are different. Thus the active Hessian law changes.

In particular

    <g_3,x0_gate>=-5delta_gate^2/16<0,
    <g_0,g_3>=-delta_gate^2/64<0.                    (45)

The initial gradient is not aligned with x0_gate. The terminal component
along the initial-radius direction is NEGATIVE. This is outside the
positive-terminal/aligned-gradient obstruction of round59.

Its exact normalized true-radius payoff is

    V_gate=2F_gate(x_3,y_3)/(4delta_gate^2)=49/1024,
    H_base(h)=1/(1+2*(3/2+12+3/2))=1/31,
    V_gate/H_base=1519/1024>1.                       (46)

This is finite nonzero amplitude, exact values and ALL original rows.
No expectation or formal negative matrix direction is being called a
lower witness.

For the ORIGINAL actual-n benchmark, the exact ratio is

    V_gate/U_3^ZJ=49B_3/1024.                        (47)

It is below1. Here is a symbolic bound using the original all-split
definitions from acceptedb98bb807d514e80d, without executing a DP.
Let Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2 and
G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2. Then C0=B0=1,
C1=lambda<5/2, B1=4. Monotonicity gives

    C2=Omega(1,lambda)<Omega(1,5/2)<9/2,
    B2=max{G(1,4),G(lambda,1)}<8.

The C2 bound follows from sqrt(89)<11. The B2 bounds follow from
sqrt(3)<2 and sqrt(21)<5. At horizon3 the THREE original split
expressions satisfy

    G(1,B2)<G(1,8)=6+4sqrt(2)<12,
    G(C1,B1)<G(5/2,4)=7+2sqrt(6)<12,
    G(C2,1)<G(9/2,1)=19/2+sqrt(37)/2<13.

Therefore B3<13 and the exact ratio(47) is <637/1024<1.
This is only a finite bridge beyond Huber. It is also below the accepted
same-word scalar single-cut witness: at the middle step P=T=3/2,
the scalar cut from factb98bb807d514e80d is121/25, which is larger
than49/1024. No superiority over the existing full witness portfolio
is claimed.

### 5.4 An exact arbitrary-horizon counterexample to this fixed law family

The legal word h_ann=(3/2,5,1,6) has actual length4. For EVERY
delta_gate>0 the SAME function family(40) and initialization yield
the following exact trajectory, divided by delta_gate:

| i | position | p_i | gradient |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | (0,2) | 2 | (1/4,1/4) |
| 1 | (-3/8,13/8) | 5/4 | (0,1/4) |
| 2 | (-3/8,3/8) | 0 | (-1/4,0) |
| 3 | (-1/8,3/8) | 1/4 | (-1/48,1/16) |
| 4 | (0,0) | 0 | (0,0) |

The first two steps bring the trajectory into the core. The remaining
steps1 and6 annihilate the two eigenmodes of M_gate; the displayed
intermediate point remains in the core, so this eigenmode explanation
is backed by the actual nonlinear gate checks, not just a matrix product.

The values at i=2,3,4, divided by delta_gate^2, are respectively
3/64,5/384,0. All original rows hold by(44). The true radius is still
2delta_gate and the terminal payoff is EXACTLY0.

For every n>=4 append n-4 zero steps. The actual iterate remains the
minimum, so the whole fixed scale family has

    sup_(delta_gate>0) V_gate(h_ann,0,...,0)=0,
    [sup_delta V_gate]/U_n^ZJ=0.                    (48)

The denominator is the original positive U_n^ZJ at THAT n. This is an
all-horizon family counterexample, not a computed finite trend.
It refutes using(40) with scale selection alone as the required all-word
adversary. It does not say W_n(h_ann,0,...,0)=0: other legal functions,
including the accepted scalar witnesses, have positive error there.
Varying the geometry, Hessian spectrum or initialization with h is
outside this fixed law family and remains open.

## 6. Original target interface: what remains unpaid

For every sufficiently large actual horizon n and EVERY predetermined
h>=0, the original affirmative target still requires a SINGLE legal
static function, finite dimension and attained minimum giving

    2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x0-x_star||^2)
                            >=(1-epsilon)/(2S_bullet(n)+1),

with a threshold in n independent of h. No statement above establishes
this. A negative answer still requires an actual full-class objective
UPPER bound with a fixed relative benchmark gap on unbounded horizons;
none is asserted here either.

The next decisive interface cannot merely be a covariance matrix whose
terminal row has the right sign. Laws1 and2 show exact original-row
payments that survive changes of scale and common values. Law3 shows
that genuine queried gate changes and terminal reversals are feasible,
but its two spectral modes are killed by a four-step word. A new law
would need word-dependent nonlinear memory/geometry which pays those
payments and avoids an annihilator, together with a uniform original
benchmark comparison. That final all-word comparison is an unpaid
THEOREM-LEVEL gap, not a technical lemma or a solved reduction.

No universal Huber optimality, covariance impossibility, unrestricted
W upper bound, resolution of(P), project finalization, revocation,
publication, shutdown or Goal completion is claimed.

text/hash persistence are separate. There is no new allocation or
automatic renewal after this assigned round.

## 8. Glossary

h,S,P_i,q_i,n are the full physical word, its mass, prefix masses,
normalized elapsed masses and ACTUAL horizon in(1). x_i,g_i,f_i,star
are the original sampled jets. Q_ij is(3). D_i^nc,C_i^val,tau_val,
lower_i,upper_i,K,F are the attempted-data and strict realization
quantities(2),(4),(7),(8). v_i^par,delta_par,nu_par,b_par,U_i,J_i,
D_path are law1 quantities(9)--(12). H_m,lambda,beta_word,c,a_word,m
are the SAME enclosing-m word(17). H_base=1/(1+2S). B_n,U_n^ZJ
are the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark denominator and its reciprocal.
p_ff,N_ff,d_ff,k_ff,u_L^ff,u_extra^ff,u_t^ff are the real incidence
construction(21)--(23). z_i^inc,v_i^inc,delta_inc,nu_inc,b_inc are
law2 quantities(24); A_inc,E_i,E,T,eta,K_inc,M,U,J_inc,F3,C_inc,D_inc
are its explicitly defined moment and payment quantities(28)--(32).
The p_t,e,f in(31) are local normalized step moments, not field elements
or objective values. F_gate,Hub_delta,clip_delta,delta_gate,x0_gate,
lambda_gate,kappa_gate,e_x,a_gate,M_gate,A_gate,B_Hub,V_gate,h_ann are
