# Three explicit nonlocal terminal transports and their original-space failures

JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT — UNVERIFIED. Worker max, invocation75 after
assigned ended baseline74, ended ceiling75, attempted ceiling76, cap1.
This manuscript is shared research evidence, not a fact. No formal gate,
mathematical program, arithmetic replay, executed trajectory/recurrence,
optimizer, installation, consult or subagent was used. Actual controller
completion and counters are not observed here.

The three laws change the terminal outgoing probability on EVERY original
finite prefix query, and add a completely specified coupled remote prefix
flow. They are constructed arrays, not selections from an unknown feasible
set. The uniform and physical-mass laws fail an old innovation direction.
The subsidized law passes the ENTIRE old two-dimensional innovation plane
and the actual root bridge/right-first gradient plane, but fails a larger
original dyadic innovation. Each failure is at the same physical enclosing
m, at every real objective level q. None is a W bound or a primal witness.

## 1. Target, actual word and accepted inputs

For a nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) is the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite-dimensional
Euclidean spaces, convex differentiable functions with L-Lipschitz
gradient and a minimizer x_star, L>0, x_0!=x_star, under
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). This is W=2R.
The original benchmark, with every integer split retained, is

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Fix ONE enclosing integer m>=1536, and throughout put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j),
    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j,
    d_j=a*c^j, D_j=2A_j-1, t_j=d_j/A_j, t_star=1/lambda.

All children use these SAME enclosing parameters. The actual diagnostic
word is h=H_m=H_(m,1/4); its physical entries are not altered or reordered.
Epsilon=0 will be a Silver calibration only. An identity on child H_j
always uses the original enclosing m, never epsilon=1/(4j).

Accepted inputs actually read are fact1252b8823bb5a290 in full (original
interpolation, every formal vector, and exact terminal-square theorem),
fact04219554e41d9750 Sections10–11 (primitive and objective conventions),
and fact5fd396eba75651e7 Section10 for ONLY

    B_(n_m)/D_m<237/250, m>=1536.

A full all-function upper W_(n_m)(H_m)<=zeta_m/D_m with
sup_m zeta_m<250/237 would therefore give a fixed improvement on
unbounded horizons. The restricted-family optimum in the last accepted
fact supplies no such upper. No original target is established here.

The full shared unverified1e4b5e4fdcb5a60f and f10a0fd97ba2b881,
including the scoped failures f251f43c220abd77,57162242c42d77eb,
309b078dc5f686e6, were read. They are awareness, not predecessors.
Every new formula reused from that regime is derived in THIS joint draft.
Guidancec8160224c76ffea9 and elaborationd53d7eabce096c9c govern this round.

## 2. Original full matrix and exact expenditure, jointly derived

Normalize smoothness1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Retain the independent
original formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), including terminal, bridge,
and any zero-step coordinates. Put

    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, Ppos_i=sum_(t<i)h_t,
    S=sum h, D=1+2S, h_n=0,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

The residual convention is exactly fact1252b8823bb5a290; every actual
legal transcript has Q_ij<=0. Let K_ij be the finite multipliers,
nu_i the i->star rows, xiCert_i the star->i rows, and K_ii=0.
Value cancellation is precisely

    sum_j K_ij-sum_j K_ji+nu_i-xiCert_i=2*1_(i=n).       (1)

After it, the entire certificate is

    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum K_ij Q_ij
                  +sum nu_i Q_(i,star)+sum xiCert_i Q_(star,i)
        =q||x_0||^2-sum nu_i<x_0,g_i>+Cgrad(g).        (2)

With out_i=nu_i+sum_j K_ij, collecting each diagonal and each ordered
position term yields

    Cgrad_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i sum_(v>i)K_iv,
    2Cgrad_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv
                           +h_i[nu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (3)

Indeed half the full incident mass at i is out_i-1_(i=n) by (1).
A forward position term contributes -h_i K_iv to its source diagonal.
For i<j the gradient-difference term is -(K_ij+K_ji); source i's
later destinations contribute -h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv, and all source j
destinations at or before i, together with its outgoing optimum row,
contribute the last term. This proves the complete matrix, including
the initial mixed row -nu/2 and all original off-diagonals.
For q>0, full PSD is equivalent to

    Cgrad-nu nu^T/(4q)>=0                              (4)

on the ENTIRE gradient space. A selected plane does not replace (4).

For completeness both optimum directions are priced before specializing
to the three laws. Let e be the all-ones gradient column, csum=Cgrad e,
X=sum xiCert_i, E=sum(1+Ppos_i)xiCert_i, and Hprice=D X-E.
Summing (1) gives sum nu_i=2+X. Multiplying it by Ppos_i and summing
gives sum K_ij(Ppos_i-Ppos_j)=2S-sum(nu_i-xiCert_i)Ppos_i.
Expansion at a common gradient then gives

    e^T Cgrad e=D+E,
    M(x_0=r,g=t e)=q r^2-(2+X)r t+(D+E)t^2.           (5)

The one-sided Huber function, zero for z<=0, z^2/2 on[0,1] and
z-1/2 above1, is convex and1-smooth. At x_0=D all samples lie above1:
g_i=1, x_i=D-Ppos_i, f_i=D-Ppos_i-1/2. Finite and outgoing optimum
residuals vanish; Q_(star,i)=-(2S-Ppos_i). At q=zeta/D this gives

    M(D,e)=(zeta-1)D-Hprice.                           (6)

Whole-word necessary prices are Hprice<=(zeta-1)D and
zeta>=D(2+X)^2/[4(D+E)]. These are single total allowances.
Completing the common-gradient/initial-point block, with v in e-perp,
gives its exact transverse charge. If

    DeltaCommon=q(D+E)-(2+X)^2/4>0,

the charge is

    Price(v)=[(D+E)(nu^T v)^2/4
       -(2+X)(nu^T v)(csum^T v)/2+q(csum^T v)^2]/DeltaCommon. (7)

This follows by inverting [[q,-(2+X)/2],[-(2+X)/2,D+E]] and
using the coupling (-nu^T v/2,csum^T v). In the singular case the
coupling must annihilate that block's kernel; no mode is discarded.

Our constructed laws have xiCert=0. They therefore have sum nu=2,
e^T Cgrad e=D, Hprice=0 and exact Huber slack(zeta-1)D. Set
dcontact=csum-D nu/2, so e^T dcontact=0. Equation(7) becomes

    Price(v)=(csum^T v)^2/D+(dcontact^T v)^2/[D(zeta-1)]. (8)

At the advertised zeta_0=101/100 the second term has coefficient100/D.
Equations(2)–(8) retain EVERY initial, common and transverse direction.
They are calculations in this joint UNVERIFIED draft, not accepted bricks.
Any negative direction at x_0=0 survives every inflation of q.

## 3. Fully explicit base flow; no PSD selection oracle

The base is an explicitly prescribed array. It is NOT asserted PSD on the
actual positive-epsilon word. A closed finite sum for all its coefficients
is supplied, together with identities establishing their signs and flow.

Finite summation of H_j gives

    v_h=a/(c-2),
    A_j=v_h c^j+(1-v_h)2^j,
    t_j=(c-2)/[1+(1/v_h-1)(2/c)^j].                   (9)

Here0<v_h<1 since c(c-2)>1>beta. The ratios increase to c-2.
In particular t_0,t_1<t_star<t_2, and for j>=2,

    0<t_j-t_star<c-lambda=lambda epsilon, t_j<1/2.     (10)

For the signs at j=1,2, direct physical substitution gives
lambda d_1-A_1=epsilon[-lambda+2/(lambda(1+epsilon))]<0 and
c(lambda d_2-A_2)=epsilon[(lambda+4)-(4lambda+2)epsilon
                                      -(5lambda+2)epsilon^2]>0.
The last bracket exceeds5 using12/5<lambda<5/2 and epsilon<=1/6144.

Define the complete primitive array Lambda^j on indices0..n_j.
At a join H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), with bridge p and terminal z,
embed the left array with weight1, the right with weight k_j, and add

    u_j h_i at i->p, v_j h_i at i->z for every right i<z,
    w_j at z->p, z_j at p->z,
    u_j=2lambda,
    v_j=[u_j d_j+2(A_j+d_j)]/A_j,
    k_j=1+(u_j+v_j)/2=2+lambda+(lambda+1)t_j,
    z_j=2A_j,
    w_j=4A_j+2d_j-u_j(A_j-1).                        (11)

Lambda^0=0. All unlisted rows are zero; repeated rows are summed.
This is also a closed finite coefficient formula: sum(11)'s four row
types over every dyadic internal node, multiplied by the product of
k_l over precisely those ancestors in whose RIGHT child the node lies.
Left ancestors contribute1. Thus each coefficient is a specified finite
sum of products of(9)–(11), with no unknown optimization or matrix choice.

Every coefficient is nonnegative. The first four scalar quantities are
positive. Also w_j=2lambda+2(d_j-A_j/lambda): j>=2 follows from(10),
j=0 has w_0=4+2a, and j=1 follows from A_1<lambda, giving w_1>2lambda-2.
The identities u_j+v_j=2(k_j-1) and
u_j(A_j-1)+w_j-z_j=2(A_j+d_j) give divergence -2h_i at each
nonterminal original query and2(A_(j+1)-1) at its terminal.

Put

    V_j=2sum_(i<n_j)h_i(f_i-f_(n_j))
       +sum_(i<n_j)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
       -A_j(A_j-1)||g_(n_j)||^2,
    Psrc_j=V_j+sum Lambda^j_iv Q_iv.

At one original join let r=sum_(i right,i<z)h_i g_i,
Y=r-(A_j-1)g_z and Z=g_p-g_z. Direct expansion yields

    Psrc_(j+1)=Psrc_j^left+k_j Psrc_j^right
       +(t_star-t_j)[(lambda+1)||Y||^2
          -2lambda A_j<Y,Z>+A_j^2(2+t_star+t_j)||Z||^2]. (12)

To audit all new terms, their four gradient polynomials before weighting
are, in(11)'s order,

    d_j<r,g_p>+||r||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2
                                      +(A_j-1)||g_p||^2/2;
    -||r||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2
                                      +(A_j-1)||g_z||^2/2-<r,g_z>;
    d_j<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2;
    -(d_j+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2-<g_p,r>-<g_p,g_z>.

Their r^2,r*g_p,r*g_z coefficients are(u_j-v_j)/2,
u_j d_j-z_j and w_j-v_j. Adding V_j and the child terms yields(12).
Its bracket is positive definite since its determinant divided by A_j^2
is(lambda+1)(2+t_star+t_j)-lambda^2>=lambda>0.
Equation(12) is an identity on ALL original gradients; at positive
epsilon it is not an induction that child matrices are PSD.

The full base objective array Kbase^j, mubase^j has empty base
Kbase^0=0, mubase^0_0=2, xiCert=0. At the same join write
A=A_j-1, bjoin=1+d_j, D=D_(j+1), D_c=D_j,
s_j=(A+bjoin)/(3A+2bjoin), theta_j=1-s_j. Put

    Kbase_(L_l,L_v)=Lambda^j_lv/D,
    Kbase_(R_i,R_v)=Kbase^j_iv,
    Kbase_(R_i,p)=s_j mubase^j_i,
    mubase_(L_l)=2h_l/D for l<n_j,
    mubase_p=2s_j-2A/D,
    mubase_(R_i)=theta_j mubase^j_i.                  (13)

All other finite rows and every incoming optimum row are zero.
Here L_l=l, p=L_(n_j), R_i=n_j+1+i, and R_(n_j) is the terminal.
For A=0, s_0=1/2; otherwise1/3<s_j<1/2. The bridge optimum row is
positive because(A+bjoin)D-A(3A+2bjoin)
=A^2+4A*bjoin+2bjoin^2+A+bjoin>0.
Primitive divergence is canceled by the left optimum rows; its terminal
divergence2A/D is canceled by the bridge optimum row and incoming right
mass2s_j. Each right node preserves flow since theta_j+s_j=1.
This proves(1), every final sign and sum mubase=2. No row enters the
unqueried actual terminal: this holds in the empty base and in every(13).

For an explicit unrolled original-index version at depth r, the right
spine child of depth l has offset o_l=2^r-2^l and bridge
p_l=n_r-2^(l-1), 1<=l<=r. Sum Lambda^(l-1)/D_l on its entire left
query block[o_l,p_l], and add s_(l-1)mubase^(l-1)_i at each original
right query i->p_l, INCLUDING the final query. These are every finite
row. Outgoing optimum rows on this left block are

    (2h_i/D_l) product_(t=l)^(r-1)theta_t for i<p_l,
    [2s_(l-1)-2(A_(l-1)-1)/D_l] product_(t=l)^(r-1)theta_t at p_l,

and at n_r they are2 product_(t=0)^(r-1)theta_t.
The same finite formula inside the child gives every mubase used in the
spine addition. All coefficients are thus closed finite products/sums.

Define astar_j=product_(t=0)^(j-1)theta_t, astar_0=1.
The ENTIRE outgoing terminal probability of this base is explicitly

    alpha_star^base=astar_r,
    alpha_(p_l)^base=s_(l-1)astar_(l-1), 1<=l<=r,
    alpha_i^base=0 at every other finite prefix index.  (14)

They sum to1 since s_j astar_j=astar_j-astar_(j+1).
There is no assumed optimum re-selection in(14).

Finally let r_R=sum_i mubase^j_i g_(R_i),
x_p=x_0-sum_(l<n_j)h_l g_(L_l),
Rchild_R=Cbase^j(g_R)-D_c||r_R||^2/4, and
kappa_j=D_c-D theta_j^2. The entire base identity is

    Mbase_(j+1)(1/D)=Psrc_j(g_L)/D+Rchild_R
       +||x_p+(A-D s_j)g_p-(D theta_j/2)r_R||^2/D
       +(kappa_j/4)||r_R-2g_p||^2.                   (15)

Here Cbase is the gradient matrix from(3) for(13). To check the
initial and every mixed term, the polynomial after source extraction is
||x_p||^2/D+2(A/D-s_j)<x_p,g_p>+(A^2/D+2s_j)||g_p||^2
-theta_j<x_p,r_R>+(bjoin-s_j)<g_p,r_R>+D_c||r_R||^2/4.
The scalar equality s_j(3A+2bjoin)=A+bjoin completes it to(15).
This also proves base contact Cbase e=D mubase/2: the primitive common
gradient derivatives vanish by(12), and the displayed squares vanish
at x_0=D,g_i=1 with the inherited child contact identity. Empty base
contact starts the induction. Neither Rchild_R nor kappa_j is presumed
nonnegative on the positive-epsilon word.

## 4. Constructed nonlocal terminal reroute and complete prefix flow

At actual depth r>=3 choose ONE of the explicit distributions below and
rhoRer=epsilon/4. For r<=2 set rhoRer=0. Write n=n_r, D=D_r,
alpha_i=alpha_i^base for i<n, astar=astar_r, Tmass=1-astar.
Let piRer_i be a probability on ALL finite original prefix indices0..n-1.
Define every FINAL row by

    alpha_star^new=astar,
    alpha_i^new=(1-rhoRer)alpha_i+rhoRer*Tmass*piRer_i,
    Knew_(n,i)=2alpha_i^new, nunew_n=2astar,
    Knew_(i,n)=0 for every i<n,
    Knew_(i,j)=Kbase_(i,j)+2rhoRer*piRer_i*alpha_j,
                         i,j<n, i!=j,
    nunew_i=mubase_i for i<n, xiCertnew_i=0.          (16)

All unlisted rows are zero. In particular the star is retained and has
no incoming direction in these laws, as explicitly chosen here.
Every row is nonnegative. The only decrement is rhoRer of each actual
original terminal finite edge, which is paid by that SAME edge.
No prefix row is decremented. The self-pair i=j is skipped; its path
keeps its old terminal edge rather than creating a diagonal residual.

The construction splits each old path n->j into n->i->j with probability
rhoRer*piRer_i. Prefix added divergence is exactly
2rhoRer(Tmass*piRer_i-alpha_i); the terminal incoming change at i is
the same quantity. Thus(1) holds at EVERY original finite node, and the
prefix flow of fact1252b8823bb5a290 is exactly out_i^pre-in_i^pre
=2alpha_i^new. Terminal outgoing mass is exactly2. This proves full
value flow without adopting a previous unverified flow identity.

For every original ordered triple, direct expansion gives

    Q_(n,i)+Q_(i,j)-Q_(n,j)=<g_n-g_i,y_i-y_j>,
    y_i=x_i-g_i.                                    (17)

The value terms cancel. The position terms are
<g_n-g_i,x_i-x_j>; the difference-square terms are
-<g_n-g_i,g_i-g_j>, proving(17) on the ENTIRE original formal space.
Consequently the full law is the explicit polynomial

    Mnew(q)=Mbase(q)+DeltaM,
    DeltaM=2rhoRer sum_(i,j<n)piRer_i alpha_j<g_n-g_i,y_i-y_j>.

Set ybar=sum_(j<n)alpha_j y_j and gbar=sum_(i<n)piRer_i g_i.
Expanding that finite sum, without assuming independence pays a square,

    DeltaM=2rhoRer[<g_n,Tmass sum_i piRer_i y_i-ybar>
                  -Tmass sum_i piRer_i<g_i,y_i>+<gbar,ybar>]. (18)

Equations(3),(13),(16) specify every coefficient, including every new
off-diagonal. Equation(18) is a bilinear identity, NOT a PSD claim.

The terminal-square fact applies to these concrete rows:

    V_new=x_n-astar*y_star-sum_i alpha_i^new y_i,
    R_new=q||x_0||^2-2sum_i alpha_i^new(f_i-||g_i||^2/2)
                 -||V_new||^2+sum_(i!=j in J_pre)lambda^pre_new_ij Q_ij,
    Mnew=||g_n-V_new||^2+R_new.                       (19)

Here y_star=0 and lambda^pre_new consists EXACTLY of(16)'s prefix K
and nu, with zero incoming optimum rows. This is the complete original
prefix remainder, not an oracle or a conditional-covariance certificate.
Its cumulative terminal probabilities w_t are the finite sums of(16),
and V_new=astar*x_0-sum_(t<n)(h_t w_t-alpha_t^new)g_t as in the
accepted fact. All original gradients remain independent.

Initial mixed coefficients nu are unchanged. The contact defect is
nonzero in general and paid, not suppressed. Define
Pbar=sum_(j<n)alpha_j Ppos_j, Ppi=sum_i piRer_i Ppos_i. Differentiating
(18) at a common gradient, where g_n-g_i=0 and y_i-y_j=Ppos_j-Ppos_i,
gives the ENTIRE changed defect

    dcontact_i=rhoRer*piRer_i(Tmass*Ppos_i-Pbar), i<n,
    dcontact_n=rhoRer(Pbar-Tmass*Ppi).                 (20)

The components sum to zero. Base contact was proved after(15), so(20)
is the full defect, not just one contribution. Substitute csum=D nu/2
+dcontact into(8): every transverse vector pays the exact square(20),
with coefficient1/[D(zeta-1)], in addition to the complete common
charge. Huber and common mixed prices are unchanged: Hprice=0 and the
common block is [[zeta/D,-1],[-1,D]], with determinant zeta-1.
The initial-point Schur charge for every original gradient is still
nu nu^T/(4q), but its gradient matrix is now the FULL(18).
No renewable local objective allowance is used.

These formulas are polynomial identities even with zero physical steps;
they impose no equality on repeated formal gradients. Empty r=0 is the
accepted direct certificate lambda_(0,star)=2, M=||x_0-g_0||^2.
No bound is claimed for a different word or a compressed horizon.

## 5. Three concrete laws and full small/Silver calibrations

For a nonempty depth-r word, define the following CLOSED distributions:

    Law A: piRer_i=1/n_r, Z_A=n_r.

    Law B: piRer_i=h_i/S_r, Z_B=S_r.

    Law C: Lmask=65536,
           vmask_i=h_i+Lmask*1_(i congruent3 modulo4),
           Z_C=S_r+Lmask*floor(n_r/4),
           piRer_i=vmask_i/Z_C.                     (21)

For r>=2, floor(n_r/4)=2^(r-2)-1. All three distributions sum to1.
The actual H_r has positive steps, so all three give strictly positive
new alpha_i at EVERY finite prefix query when r>=3. Their star probability
is precisely astar_r. Law C fixes its mask and coefficient once; it does
not solve for a feasible distribution, use a running budget, or retune
the old finite cycles or adjacent mixture. Every original prefix edge
is declared by(16), including remote edges from each i to EVERY nonzero
base terminal destination p_l. The construction has actual remote support.

At r<=2, rhoRer=0, so the complete actual H2 calibration is the base,
with ALL original initial mixed terms. Put b=1+a; then7/5<b<sqrt(2)<10/7.
At H1, (11) and(13) give

    Psrc_1=(2-b^2)||g_0-g_1||^2,
    Mbase_1(1/D_1)=||x_0-D_1(g_0+g_1)/2||^2/D_1
                              +(3-2b)||g_0-g_1||^2/4. (22)

Both remainders are PSD. At H2 the physical bridge is2-epsilon,
D_1>19/5, D_2<75/7, and
theta_1=(2b+2-epsilon)/(3b+4-2epsilon)<59/100.
The last ratio increases in b and decreases in the physical bridge;
use b<10/7 and2-epsilon>199/100 to prove the bound by cross multiplication.
Then D_2 theta_1^2<15/4 and kappa_1>1/20.
The full(15), its entire H1 Schur remainder from(22), and Psrc_1 prove
full H2 PSD. These are original-vector calculations, not quotient tests.

At epsilon=0 every rhoRer is zero. Also t_j=t_star and Psrc_j=0 by(12).
Writing u_star=1/lambda, A=A_j-1 and bjoin=1+u_star(A+1), expansion gives

    (3A+2bjoin)^2*kappa_j
      =2(1-u_star)A^3+(6-8u_star)A^2
       +(1+u_star)(8u_star-2)A+(1+u_star)^2(1-2u_star)>0. (23)

Each coefficient is positive since1/4<u_star<1/2. Starting with the
empty square, (15) and base contact give full Silver PSD by induction:
the ENTIRE child Schur remainder is PSD once its full matrix is PSD.
This use of child PSD is confined to the proved epsilon=0 calibration.
No positive-epsilon child PSD is assumed anywhere below.

## 6. Original old H4 plane and exact all-depth base restriction

Write Wloc=A_2+d_2, Uloc=A_2+d_3, rloc=Uloc/Wloc,
Edef=(lambda d_2-A_2)(A_2+d_2/lambda)>0 and delta3=t_3-t_star>0.
Choose the one-dimensional ORIGINAL formal-vector test

    g_0,...,g_3=u, g_4,...,g_7=v0,
    g_8,...,g_11=w, g_12,...,g_15=0,
    Wloc(u+w)+Uloc*v0=0.                            (24)

Let N be this two-dimensional zero-displacement plane; it chooses values
of independent test vectors and identifies no formal coordinates.
Equation(12) at its three actual joins gives EXACTLY

    P_N=-Edef(u-v0)^2-k_3 Edef*w^2
       -delta3[(lambda+1)Wloc^2*w^2
          -2lambda A_3 Wloc*w*v0+A_3^2(2+t_star+t_3)*v0^2]<0 (25)

at every nonzero point of N. The left H3 has Y=0,Z=u-v0;
the right H3 contributes -Edef*w^2. The final bracket is positive
definite by(12), so strict negativity follows also when either part vanishes.

Place(24) in the FIRST actual H4 of H_m, set x_0=0 and EVERY other
original gradient, INCLUDING g_n and the root bridge, to zero. Its zero
terminal and displacement make every later source Y,Z zero on the all-left
embedding, whose weight is1. The final(15) has zero boundary and right
variables. Hence, for every m>=5 and every real q,

    D_m Mbase_m(q)|N=P_N.                            (26)

Every base terminal destination p_l lies after this active block when
m>=6. Its gradient and position are zero. Therefore ybar=0 on N, and
(18) gives the exact whole-word restriction for EACH distribution

    D_m Mnew(q)|N=P_N+(2rhoRer*Tmass*D_m/Z)*Q_weight,
    Q_weight=sum_(i=0)^11 weight_i(g_i^2-g_i*x_i),     (27)

where weight_i is1,h_i, or vmask_i, and Z is the corresponding(21).
No child allowance or omitted terminal term appears in(27).

For physical weights and any zero-displacement test,

    sum h_i(g_i^2-g_i*x_i)=sum h_i(1-h_i/2)g_i^2      (28)

at x_0=0. Indeed sum h_i g_i=0, so
sum_(t<i)h_i h_t g_i g_t=-(sum h_i^2 g_i^2)/2.
This is an exact identity in the selected original vector, not a numerical
trajectory or an assumption about legal sampled values.

## 7. Laws A and B: early exact failures

Use u=1,v0=0,w=-1 in(24). It has zero displacement and every retained
optimum, terminal and inherited initial contribution already included.
Within each active four-query block the local prefixes are
0,b,b+2-epsilon,2b+2-epsilon, with total physical displacement Wloc.
For Law A this gives

    Q_uniform=8+2[4b+2(2-epsilon)]-4Wloc=4(1-d_2)<0. (29)

For Law B, (28) gives

    Q_physical=2Wloc-[2b^2+(2-epsilon)^2+(1+d_2)^2]<0. (30)

For example Wloc<826/100, b>7/5, 2-epsilon>199/100, d_2>12/5
give Q_physical<1652/100-98/25-39601/10000-289/25<-29/10.
The elementary parameter bounds are proved in Section9 below.
Combining(25),(27),(29),(30), BOTH complete laws fail on this original
direction for every real q and every actual m>=1536. All signs and
value flows remain valid; the gradient matrix does not.

For Law A the negative added curvature is exactly
(8rhoRer*Tmass*D_m/n_m)(1-d_2) after D_m normalization.
For Law B it is exactly(2rhoRer*Tmass*D_m/S_m)Q_physical.
Thus the actual whole-word cost is displayed, without reassigning it to
a new local allowance. Section10 also supplies the separate actual root
screen: Law A loses the right-first diagonal; Law B passes that two-query
plane but its independent early failure(30) persists.

## 8. Quantitative growth and base root entries without child PSD

We need explicit original-coordinate root entries to screen Law C before
the long lift. No child PSD estimate is used. From c(c-2)
=1+2(lambda+1)epsilon+lambda^2 epsilon^2 and lambda<5/2,

    v_h=(1-epsilon)/[c(c-2)]>1-9epsilon>255/256.       (31)

For the first inequality compare with1+7epsilon+7epsilon^2;
(1-9epsilon)(1+7epsilon+7epsilon^2)
=1-2epsilon-56epsilon^2-63epsilon^3<1-epsilon.
For every j>=1533, the third binomial term gives

    A_j/2^j>(255/256)(6/5)^j
       >(255/256) j(j-1)(j-2)/750>4000000.           (32)

Here j,j-1,j-2>1500, and1500^3/750=4500000.
In particular S_m>1000000*2^m. These are symbolic inequalities, not
an executed recurrence, scalar replay, or benchmark computation.

Let pterm_j denote Psrc_j with only its terminal gradient equal1.
Substituting Y=-(A_j-1),Z=-1 into(12) gives

    pterm_(j+1)=k_j pterm_j+(t_star-t_j)F_j,
    F_j=A_j^2(1+t_j)-2A_j+(lambda+1), pterm_0=0.      (33)

It can be read as the CLOSED finite sum
pterm_j=sum_(l<j)(t_star-t_l)F_l product_(v=l+1)^(j-1)k_v.
No value of that recurrence is executed. Since F_j=(A_j-1)^2+t_j A_j^2
+lambda>0, pterm_1,pterm_2>=0. For j>=2,

    k_j-(2+t_j)^2=(t_star-t_j)(1+t_j)<0,
    0<F_j/A_(j+1)^2<=(1+t_j)/(2+t_j)^2<=1/4.          (34)

The middle bound uses A_j>(lambda+1)/2. Therefore the normalized value
can decrease by at most lambda epsilon/4 at each later level: if it is
negative, multiplication by k_j/(2+t_j)^2 in(0,1) makes it larger;
if positive, the product remains positive. This proves for j<=m-1

    pterm_j>=-j lambda epsilon*A_j^2/4>-5A_j^2/32.    (35)

This is a scalar diagonal LOWER estimate from an exact identity, not a
claim that the source or any child is PSD.

Set j=m-1, Bmass=A_j, p=n_j and qright=p+1. The physical root bridge
is h_p=1+d_j; the right-first step is b=1+a. Define astarch=astar_j,
ap=s_j astarch, T_R=1-astarch, Tmass=ap+T_R=1-theta_j astarch.
The base terminal destinations consist of p with mass ap and right
destinations with total T_R. None is qright at these depths.
Since theta_l<=2/3, astarch<1/100 (use j>=12 and (2/3)^12<1/100).

Formula(3), with the fully expanded base(13), gives EXACTLY

    Cbase_pp=2s_j+[(Bmass-1)^2+pterm_j]/D_m,
    Cbase_qright,qright=2/D_j,
    Cbase_p,qright=b(h_p-s_j)/D_j.                   (36)

For the first entry the outgoing primitive mass at its terminal is
A_j^2-1+pterm_j, as follows from the terminal diagonal in V_j and(3).
The root right-first diagonal and mixed term follow from its inherited
child row, split optimum weight theta_j*mubase_0, backward weight
s_j*mubase_0 and mubase_0=2b/D_j. All inherited prefixes are retained.

By(10), d_j in((2/5)Bmass,Bmass/2), D_m<5Bmass,
and Bmass>=2^j>1250. Equations(35),(36) then give

    Cbase_pp>Bmass/6,
    Cbase_qright,qright>1/Bmass,
    7/25<Cbase_p,qright<2/5.                         (37)

For the first, ((27/32)Bmass^2-2Bmass+1)/(5Bmass)>Bmass/6
when Bmass>1250. For the mixed upper use b<10/7 and h_p<1+Bmass/2;
the lower follows from b>7/5 and d_j>(2/5)Bmass.
Thus the whole root two-query GRADIENT plane, with x_0=g_n=0 and every
other gradient zero, is positive definite already for the prescribed
base, even though its other directions are indefinite.

## 9. Law C passes the entire old N plane at the actual word

The fixed subsidy is large enough to pay this screen, with no allowance
renewed at higher levels. Let normN=u^2+v0^2+w^2 on(24).
On a zero-displacement N vector, define Q_mask using unit weights only
at original queries3,7,11; query15 has zero gradient. Since
x_3=-(A_2-1)u, x_7=-Wloc*u-(A_2-1)v0,
x_11=-Wloc*u-Uloc*v0-(A_2-1)w,

    Q_mask=A_2(u^2+v0^2+w^2)+Wloc*u(v0+w)+Uloc*v0*w.

Using u=-w-rloc*v0,

    Q_mask=(A_2-d_2)w^2+(2A_2 rloc-Wloc)w*v0
                                +(A_2 rloc^2-d_3)v0^2. (38)

We prove a uniform lower bound, rather than checking one lucky point.
The actual epsilon<=1/6144 implies

    582/100<A_2<584/100, 241/100<d_2<242/100,
    582/100<d_3<584/100, 141/100<rloc<142/100.          (39)

For explicit verification use707/500<sqrt(2)<283/200 and
b=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)] to get
5827/1000<A_2=3+2b-epsilon<583/100.
Also d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2) lies in(2413/1000,2415/1000), and
d_3=lambda^2(1-epsilon^2)(1+epsilon) lies in(5827/1000,584/100).
These sharper intervals give
rloc>(2*5827/1000)/(583/100+2415/1000)>141/100 and
rloc<(583/100+584/100)/(5827/1000+2413/1000)<142/100.

Subtract normN/8= w^2/4+rloc*w*v0/4+(rloc^2+1)v0^2/8
from(38). Its w^2 coefficient exceeds63/20; its v0^2 coefficient
exceeds53/10; the absolute value of its cross coefficient is less81/10.
These follow by direct insertion of(39):

    A_2-d_2-1/4>5.82-2.42-.25=3.15,
    A_2 rloc^2-d_3-(rloc^2+1)/8
       >5.82*(1.41)^2-5.84-[(1.42)^2+1]/8>5.3,
    0<2A_2 rloc-Wloc-rloc/4<8.1.

The determinant is positive because
(63/20)(53/10)=3339/200>6561/400=(81/10)^2/4.
Thus the entire plane has

    Q_mask>normN/8.                                 (40)

The strict inequality in(40) is for nonzero N vectors; both sides are
zero at the zero vector. The strict bounds in(41) below have the same
nonzero-vector convention.

The physical part Q_physical from(28) is greater than -100normN:
each four-query block has steps below7, so the sum of its four negative
terms -h_i^2/2 exceeds -98; positive terms may be discarded.
The source base loss is also fully bounded. In(25), Edef<24epsilon,
k_3<7, delta3<(5/2)epsilon, Wloc<9, A_3<15,
lambda+1<4, 2+t_star+t_3<3. The absolute cross term is at most
lambda A_3 Wloc(w^2+v0^2)<338(w^2+v0^2).
Together these give

    P_N>-3000epsilon*normN,
    Q_vmask=Q_physical+65536Q_mask>8092normN.          (41)

By(32), Z_C=S_m+65536(2^(m-2)-1)<(33/32)S_m;
Tmass>99/100. Since D_m>2S_m and rhoRer=epsilon/4,

    2rhoRer*Tmass*D_m/Z_C
       >(epsilon/2)(99/100)(64/33)>(3/4)epsilon.       (42)

Combining(27),(41),(42), at EVERY nonzero original N vector,

    D_m M_C(q)|N>3069epsilon*normN>0.                (43)

This pays the WHOLE old two-dimensional screen with the actual global
normalization, not a reset child normalizer. It does not assert full PSD.
In particular the large fixed subsidy may create additional isolated
query defects; all those entries remain explicitly in(3),(16),(18).
The exact deeper negative direction below is valid regardless of those
additional screens. No such direction is hidden or priced as free.

## 10. Actual root bridge/right-first screens for all three laws

Let omega=piRer_p and eta=piRer_qright. In the original two-query test
g_p=u_root, g_qright=v_root, x_0=g_n=0, all other gradients zero,
y_p=-u_root, y_qright=-h_p*u_root-v_root. From the COMPLETE base terminal
distribution(14),
ybar=-(ap+T_R h_p)u_root-T_R b*v_root.
Substitution into(18) gives exactly

    DeltaC_pp=2rhoRer*omega*T_R(1-h_p),
    DeltaC_qright,qright=2rhoRer*eta*(Tmass-b*T_R),
    DeltaC_p,qright=rhoRer[eta*ap*(h_p-1)-omega*T_R*b]. (44)

These include every remote destination and every inherited position.
They are not adjacent-only approximations.

For Law A, omega=eta=1/n_m, and

    Tmass-b*T_R=1-b+(b-theta_j)astarch<-(b-1)/2.

Use astarch<1/100, b>7/5 and b<3/2 for this strict bound.
Its actual right-first diagonal therefore satisfies

    C_A,qright,qright<2/D_j-rhoRer*(b-1)/n_m<0.       (45)

Here D_j/n_m>96m: D_j>(3/2)A_j and n_m<2^(j+1) give
D_j/n_m>(3v_h/4)(6/5)^(m-1).
The third binomial term is greater than m^3/6000>300m for m>=1536,
so the last expression exceeds200m. Then
rhoRer*(b-1)D_j/n_m>(1/(16m))*(2/5)*96m=12/5>2.
The ORIGINAL assignment g_qright=1, all other vectors zero, rejects Law A
at every q. It is a root right-first defect separate from(29).

For Law B, omega=h_p/S_m, eta=b/S_m. For Law C,
omega=(h_p+65536)/Z_C, eta=b/Z_C, since p is3 modulo4 and qright is0.
In both cases Bmass>128*65536, S_m>2Bmass and h_p<1+Bmass/2 give
omega<13/50, eta<=b/S_m. Equations(37),(44) imply

    C_new,pp>Bmass*(1/6-rhoRer/3),
    C_new,qright,qright>(1-rhoRer)/Bmass,
    7/25-rhoRer/2<C_new,p,qright<2/5.                (46)

For the first, the decrement is2rhoRer*omega*T_R*d_j<rhoRer*Bmass/3.
For the second use Tmass-b*T_R>=-(b-1), b(b-1)<1, and S_m>2Bmass.
For the mixed entry the correction is negative since ap<1/100,
T_R>99/100 and eta*ap*d_j<omega*T_R*b, and its magnitude is less
rhoRer*(13/50)*(10/7)<rhoRer/2.
Because rhoRer<1/1000, the determinant exceeds

    (1/6-1/3000)*(999/1000)-4/25
       =(498501-480000)/3000000>0.                  (47)

Thus Laws B AND C pass the ENTIRE actual root two-query gradient plane,
including the old direction g_p=1,g_qright=-Bmass/25. This is precisely
a screen with x_0=g_n=0. It is not a claim about the larger terminal
Schur plane, initial mixed directions, singular modes or full PSD.
All those directions continue to be represented by(3),(18),(19),(20).

## 11. Law C: exact full-depth failure after both screens pass

Set j=m-3>=1533 and Kblock=2^j. In the FIRST original H_(j+2)=H_(m-1),
choose the following original one-dimensional formal vector:

    g_i=1 for0<=i<Kblock,
    g_i=0 forKblock<=i<2Kblock,
    g_i=-1 for2Kblock<=i<3Kblock,
    g_i=0 for3Kblock<=i<=n_m,
    x_0=0.                                         (48)

Both active blocks contain the physical H_j followed by its actual
bridge1+d_j, so each has displacement Lblock=A_j+d_j, with opposite
sign. The entire prefix displacement is zero, its terminal query is zero,
and EVERY subsequent original query has position and gradient zero.
No gradients are deleted, no child parameters are reset, and (48) need
not be a legal primal transcript to test PSD of the full matrix.

Define E_j=(t_j-t_star)A_j^2(lambda+t_j)>0.
For the first H_(j+1), the all-common H_j source remainder is zero by(12),
the right child is zero, and its join has Y=0,Z=1. Thus its source value
is -E_j. The third-block copy similarly has source -E_j.
The H_(j+2) join has Y=-Lblock,Z=0. Hence the EXACT source value is

    P_big=-(1+k_(j+1))E_j
                   -(t_(j+1)-t_star)(lambda+1)Lblock^2<0. (49)

At the final objective join (48) kills all boundary/right terms in(15).
Every base terminal destination p_l is at or beyond the zero terminal
of this first H_(m-1), so ybar=0. For EVERY real q, (18) gives

    D_m M_C(q;48)=P_big+(2rhoRer*Tmass*D_m/Z_C)*Q_big. (50)

This is the whole original-space action including ALL remote prefix
rows, the optimum, terminal square and initial mixed terms.
The physical part of Q_big, by(28), is

    Q_big,physical=2Lblock
                      -[sum_(i<n_j)(H_j)_i^2+(1+d_j)^2]. (51)

For the mask part, every active block has Kblock/4=2^(j-2) mask queries.
Write P_local_i for the physical prefix inside H_j at a mask query.
The paired contributions of its two opposite blocks are

    Q_big,mask=sum_(i congruent3 mod4,0<=i<Kblock)
                          [2+2P_local_i-Lblock]

## Status

Original exact-profile minimax target OPEN. No target-scale law was obtained even for the entire inherited H_m family. No full-class asymptotic upper certificate was obtained. All new mathematics is UNVERIFIED and is fully derived in PROOF_ATTEMPT.md.

For each enclosing m>=1536, eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt2)(1+eps), a=beta/c are held fixed throughout H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j). ## Line 1: a real 64-atom uniform match fails

Take equal-probability midpoint atoms of [-h0^2,h0^2], where h0=1+a. Their original variance is h0^4(1-64^-2)/3. A sup-norm comparison with the exactly folded uniform law proves that the first finite output has absolute coordinates below57/64. Every remaining prefix multiplier has magnitude at most1, so the entire remaining actual run stays in the quadratic core.

With P_m=beta^(2^m-1)c^-m, this law's payoff is <9P_m^2, and D_m times its payoff tends to0. Its first projection is genuinely nonvertex; subsequent core collapse explains the failure. This is only a bound on this specific law.

## Line 2: exact inverse laws and their optimized radius debt

For any finite mean-zero next lawY and h>1, d=h-1, select a_*>=max(d,maxY), Z=a_*-Y, and conditional right-branch probabilities omega with E[omega Z]=d. The two preimages are

    X_left=Y-h,  X_right=a_*-h+Z/d.

They have mean0 and actual thresholda_*-h; both map toY by the physical h-step. Their exact original variance payment is

    VarX=VarY+2h a_*-h^2+(d^-2-1)E[omega Z^2].

Backward atom splitting preserves all already-built future laws on one final finite probability space. Every old and terminal projection row is therefore automatic.

An explicit independent-branch law is obtained by starting from terminal±2 and taking a_*=maxY+d and omega=d/a_* at each reversed step. It has at most2^(n+1) positive-weight atoms and multiple active atoms with a strict threshold gap at every preterminal step. Its terminal2F is3. Its endpoints and variance obey

    L_prev=L+h,
    q_prev=(h q+L)/d,
    V_prev=[(q+1/d)/(q+d)]V+h^2 q/d.

This law fails quantitatively: for all inheritedm, its payoff is <(9/8)3^(-2^(m-1)), and D_m times the payoff tends to0. This exposes the radius cost of that policy only; it does not decide optimized inversion.

The allocation AND threshold optimization are closed analytically at one step. Fixed a_* uses a fractional knapsack: fill highestY for1<h<2 and lowestY for h>2, with at most one fractional atom. On each interval with cutoffz and fully selected setA,

    J'(a_*)=d+E[1_A(z-Y)].

For h>=2, the optimized variance is strictly increasing in a_*, so its minimum is at max(d,maxY). For1<h<2 it is convex piecewise affine, with slope

    h^2/d-(d^-2-1)E(Y-z)_+.

The minimum is attained at the lower endpoint or at a feasible upper-tail breakpointa_*=(d+E[Y1_A])/P(A). This is an exact finite selection rule, not a solver result.

At h=sqrt2 the slope-zero stop-loss level is1. If t isY's ACTUAL threshold, p=P(Y>t), and the real guardp(maxY-t)<=sqrt2 holds, choosing the upper tail with a_*=t+sqrt2/p attains

    VarX=VarY+2sqrt2 F(Y)+(2+sqrt2)E[gY^2].

Boundary mass atY=t can create multiple equally optimal thresholds. The guard is material. Perturbation from sqrt2 is NOT required: for every1<h<2, let gamma=(h-1)h/(2-h), choose the actual cutoffz with E(Y-z)_+=gamma and selected upper massp, and set a_*=z+(h-1+gamma)/p. Under the exact guardp(maxY-z)<=h-1+gamma, the attained minimum is

             +[h^2/(2-h)]||P_K(Y/gamma)||^2.

The scaling appears only in this identity; physical steps and the original radius are unchanged. The guard bounds the maximum active excess divided by its conditional mean by2/h.

An exact finite tail/gap family was tested with the ACTUAL inherited leafgamma0=h0*a/(1-a)<1. Terminal±2 first inverts to±r, r=2+h0. The level-1 bridge has d=beta and gamma_bridge>r, so its optimized cap inversion makes three atoms

    w=-r-(1+beta), mass(r-beta)/(2r).

Their stop-loss atv is exactly1. The next leaf's gamma0 cutoff is strictly ABOVEv, with only the upper atom selected. There is therefore no boundary mass to vary at equal cost; this returns to a vertex step. More generally a cap bridge selecting only its minimum atom always regenerates this stop-loss-1 gap. Genuine nonvertex continuation needs multiple distinct selected lower values, or a different boundary-mass family. No propagation of their2/h excess-concentration guard through all inherited levels was proved. This is not a failure theorem for every optimized law.

## A precise obstruction after one-step optimization

For h=2 the globally minimal predecessor variance is

    VarY+4max(1,maxY)-4.

For Y=A with probability1/(A^2+1) and Y=-1/A otherwise, A>=1, both VarY and terminal2F equal1. The optimized predecessor variance is4A-3. Symmetric±1 has the same terminal variance and value but predecessor variance1. Attainment is explicit by splitting the negative atom with conditional right probability1/A at a_*=A.

Thus even optimized inversion cannot be controlled by terminal variance and value alone. This does NOT prove that the optimized policy fails H_m; it rules out a scalar induction that discards tail geometry.

## All-word edge cases and the exact remaining gap

The proof supplies actual inverse formulas for h=0 (identity), 0<h<1 (the unique monotone inverse using E(Y-a_*)_+=1-h), and h=1 (all maximum atoms receive their common least-variance preimage). Hence a completely specified greedy reverse law can be selected for any finite nonnegative word, starting from terminal ±2, with at most n+2 atoms and exact payoff 3/V0.

What is absent is the quantitative boundV0<=3B_n/(1-o(1)), uniformly over all words and all sufficiently large n; it is absent even for inheritedH_m. # R9 fixed-law nonvertex attempt — UNVERIFIED

Date: 2026-10-09. Allocation: grantff598f7965f7d469. One native analytical invocation; two substantive lines; mathematical programs, arrays, calculators, solvers and formal submissions: zero. No child agent, consult, worker/service operation, private worker file, truth-store mutation, publication, finalization, revocation or Goal change was used.

All mathematics in this document is an unverified direct-research draft. It has not entered the Danus fact graph. Current OPERATOR.md, the complete original PROBLEM.md, TASK.md and R8_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261009.md were read. A truncated combined display of PROBLEM.md was repaired by reading its remaining sections separately. The old memory note was used only to locate and preserve the original scope; current project instructions supersede its historical pause status.

## Verdict and original target

No target-scale finite law was obtained. The original problem remains OPEN. Line 1 supplies a fully specified finite nonvertex law but proves that its normalized payoff is negligible on the entire inherited family. Line 2 constructs actual finite laws backwards, solves their one-step branch/threshold variance optimization, and identifies the unresolved distributional estimate. Neither is an upper bound on the whole simplex family or on the full smooth-convex function class.

The target remains the uniform assertion

\[
 \forall\eta>0\ \exists N_\eta\ \forall n\ge N_\eta\ \forall h\in[0,\infty)^n:
 \quad \mathcal W_n(h)\ge(1-\eta)/B_n,
\]

where \(\mathcal W=2\mathcal R\), the denominator uses the ORIGINAL selected minimizer and initial point, and the performance is at the actual last iterate. The benchmark is the exact all-integer-split recursion

\[
C_0=B_0=1,\quad
C_n=\max_{k+l=n-1}\frac{C_k+C_l+\sqrt{C_k^2+6C_kC_l+C_l^2}}2,
\]
\[
B_n=\max_{k+l=n-1}\left(2C_k+\frac{B_l}{2}
 +\frac{\sqrt{B_l^2+8C_kB_l}}2\right).
\]

A negative answer still requires an asymptotic full-function-class upper certificate. No such certificate is given here.

For each enclosing \(m\ge1536\), fix

\[
\epsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad \beta=1-\epsilon,\quad
c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\epsilon),\quad a=\beta/c,
\]
\[
H_0=(),\quad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j),\quad
n_m=2^m-1,\quad S_m=\sum H_m,\quad D_m=1+2S_m.
\]

The accepted comparison supplied in the task is \(B_{n_m}/D_m<47/50\). Consequently a law with payoff asymptotic to \(1/D_m\) still misses the requested target. The inherited parameters above are kept fixed at every internal level.

## Common actual-function construction

Let a finite probability space have positive probabilities \(p_i\), and put

\[
E=\{X:\mathbb E X=0\},\qquad
K=\{g\in E:g_i\ge-1\},\qquad
F_K(X)=\max_{g\in K}\left(\mathbb E[Xg]-\tfrac12\mathbb E g^2\right).
\]

This is an ordinary Euclidean space under \(X_i\mapsto\sqrt{p_i}X_i\). The set \(K\) is compact: its defining conditions give \(-1\le g_i\le(1-p_i)/p_i\). It is convex, and zero is in its relative interior. The maximizer is the Euclidean projection \(P_KX\). Projection optimality gives

\[
\langle X-P_KX,k-P_KX\rangle\le0\quad(k\in K).
\]

Adding the inequalities for two inputs proves that the projection is 1-Lipschitz. Strong concavity in the maximization variable gives the gradient \(\nabla F_K=P_K\); hence \(F_K\) is globally convex and 1-smooth. It has unique minimum zero at zero: for nonzero \(X\), a sufficiently small positive multiple \(sX\) lies in \(K\), and \(F_K(X)\ge(s-s^2/2)\|X\|^2>0\).

The unique scalar \(t\) satisfying \(\mathbb E(X-t)_+=1\) gives

\[
g=(X-t)_+-1.
\]

Indeed this is the projection's clipped Lagrange-multiplier formula, and its mean is zero. The stop-loss function is continuous and strictly decreasing below the maximum atom, with range from infinity to zero. Moreover \(\mathbb E(X+1)_+\ge1\), so \(t\ge-1\). Writing \(U=(X-t)_+\), one has \(\mathbb EU=1\), \(\mathbb E[Xg]=t+\mathbb EU^2\), and therefore

\[
F_K(X)=t+\tfrac12+\tfrac12\mathbb EU^2
       =t+1+\tfrac12\mathbb Eg^2.                 \tag{1}
\]

Every constructed step below is exactly \(X'=X-hg\). A probability atom can be split into several atoms with identical current and future values: aggregate probabilities, thresholds, moments and future dynamics remain unchanged. Thus backward splitting specifies one finite probability space before the forward run. It does not change the function during that run. All old, cross and terminal projection rows follow from the one actual set \(K\); no selected subset of interpolation rows replaces them.

## Line 1: a finite matched-uniform law falls into the quadratic core

This line tests the uniform first-leaf match and closes its finite-dimensional transfer explicitly. It does not attempt to promote the match into an all-depth witness after the core collapse is seen.

Let \(h_0=1+a\), \(R=h_0^2\), and take exactly 64 equal-probability initial atoms

\[
X_{0,i}=R\left(\frac{2i-1}{64}-1\right),\qquad 1\le i\le64.       \tag{2}
\]

These are fixed before the run, have zero mean and variance

\[
V_0=\frac{R^2}{3}(1-64^{-2}).                                  \tag{3}
\]

For comparison only, a uniform continuous variable on \([-h_0^2,h_0^2]\) has first-step threshold \(t=h_0^2-2h_0\). Its inactive and active branches both map onto \([-ah_0,ah_0]\). Their constant densities add, so the first output is uniform on that interval.

This continuous helper is not the delivered function. Couple (2) to it by equal subintervals. The input error is at most \(R/64\) in sup norm. If two inputs differ by at most \(\delta\), their stop-loss roots differ by at most \(\delta\), and their projected gradients differ by at most \(2\delta\). Their outputs after the first step consequently differ by at most \((1+2h_0)R/64\).

For the inherited parameters, \(1/3<a<1/2\), \(h_0<3/2\), and \(R<9/4\). Thus the actual finite first output satisfies

\[
\|X_1\|_\infty <\frac34+\frac9{64}=\frac{57}{64}<1.             \tag{4}
\]

For completeness, \(c<5/2\) follows from \(\sqrt2<10/7\) and \(\epsilon\le1/6144\); then \(\beta>5/6\) gives \(a>1/3\). The upper bound \(a<1/2\) follows from \(c>2\) and \(\beta<1\).

Every nonempty scalar prefix product of the factors \(|1-h_t|\) in \(H_m\) is at most \(a\). To see this, the complete product in \(H_j\) is

\[
P_j=\beta^{2^j-1}c^{-j},\qquad P_j(ac^j)=a\beta^{2^j-1}\le a.   \tag{5}
\]

The recursive decomposition into the first child, bridge, and right-child prefix proves the claim by induction. Dividing a nonempty full prefix by the first factor \(a\) shows that every prefix of the remaining word has product at most one. Since (4) lies in the quadratic core \([-1,1]\), all subsequent actual gradients equal their inputs. No further face transition occurs. Hence

\[
X_{n_m}=\frac{\prod_t(1-h_t)}{1-h_0}\,X_1,
\quad
\frac{2F_K(X_{n_m})}{V_0}
=\frac{P_m^2}{a^2}\frac{\mathbb EX_1^2}{V_0}
<9P_m^2.                                                        \tag{6}
\]

Here \(V_0>1\) follows from \(h_0>4/3\) in (3), and \(\mathbb EX_1^2<1\) follows from (4). The first projection is genuinely nonvertex: a single active atom of probability \(1/64\) would require its excess above the threshold to be 64, while the whole initial support width is less than \(9/2\). Not all atoms are active, because the initial minimum is below \(-1\). The subsequent all-active motion is exactly what destroys this candidate.

The physical sum obeys

\[
S_m=\frac{a}{c-2}c^m+\left(1-\frac{a}{c-2}\right)2^m-1<c^m-1.  \tag{7}
\]

Indeed \(c(c-2)>1>\beta\), so \(0<a/(c-2)<1\). Equations (5)--(7) give

\[
D_m\frac{2F_K(X_{n_m})}{V_0}
<18\beta^{2(2^m-1)}c^{-m}\longrightarrow0.                       \tag{8}
\]

Thus this explicit 64-atom law fails the target throughout the inherited family. This is a statement about (2), not about every finite law.

## Line 2: actual inverse splitting and radius optimization

### Exact inverse for a step larger than one

Suppose a finite mean-zero next law \(Y\) is already fixed. Let \(h>1\), \(d=h-1\), \(q=\max Y\), and choose

\[
a_*\ge\max(d,q),\quad Z=a_*-Y\ge0,\quad t=a_*-h.
\]

The notation \(a_*\) here is a threshold parameter and is unrelated to the inherited constant \(a=\beta/c\). Choose conditional right-branch probabilities \(0\le\omega(Y)\le1\) such that

\[
\mathbb E[\omega Z]=d.                                         \tag{9}
\]

This is feasible because \(\mathbb EZ=a_*\ge d\). Split each atom into

\[
X_L=Y-h=t-Z,\qquad X_R=t+Z/d.                                  \tag{10}
\]

Use probabilities \(1-\omega\) and \(\omega\) within that atom. Then \(\mathbb EX=0\), \(\mathbb E(X-t)_+=1\), and both branches of the ACTUAL forward step map exactly to \(Y\). Zero branch masses can be omitted. The exact variance is

\[
V(X)=V(Y)+2ha_*-h^2+(d^{-2}-1)J,\quad
J=\mathbb E[\omega(a_*-Y)^2].                                  \tag{11}
\]

This follows by expanding (10), using (9), and using \(\mathbb EY=0\). All original-radius costs are present in (11).

### An explicit finite nonvertex law, with a closed quantitative failure

The following choice is useful for exposing the cost of independent branching; it is not asserted to optimize (11). Fix the terminal two-point law \(Y_n=\pm2\) with equal probabilities, so \(2F_K(Y_n)=3\). At each reversed physical step choose

\[
a_*=q+d,\qquad \omega(Y)=p=\frac{d}{q+d},                       \tag{12}
\]

independently of \(Y\). If the next-law support is \([-L,q]\), its predecessor has exact endpoints and variance

\[
L_{\rm prev}=L+h,\qquad q_{\rm prev}=\frac{hq+L}{d},
\]
\[
V_{\rm prev}=\frac{q+1/d}{q+d}V+\frac{h^2q}{d}.                \tag{13}
\]

Start these recurrences with \(L=q=2,V=4\), and use every physical step in reverse order. This specifies all atoms and weights before execution: atoms are indexed by a terminal sign and a binary branch word, with weight

\[
\frac12\prod_{t=0}^{n-1}p_t^{b_t}(1-p_t)^{1-b_t},
\]

and their initial values are the nested affine preimages (10). Thus there are at most \(2^{n+1}\) atoms, every weight is positive, and the dimension is finite for each actual word. No limiting distribution is involved.

At each query the threshold is \(q-1\). Every left preimage is strictly below it and every right preimage is at least one unit above it. There are multiple distinct active atoms, with the same fixed probability space throughout. This is genuine simultaneous face motion and does not invoke a wholesale switch without a gap.

Nevertheless the original radius is too large. From (13), each reverse step increases \(q\) by at least a factor \(1+1/d\). In \(H_m\), exactly \(K_m=2^{m-1}\) physical steps are leaves, with \(d=a\) and \(h_0=1+a\). Set \(\kappa=h_0/a>3\). Immediately before undoing the first physical leaf,

\[
q_1\ge2\kappa^{K_m-1},\qquad
V_0\ge\frac{h_0^2q_1}{a}\ge2h_0\kappa^{K_m}.
\]

Therefore this actual finite law has

\[
\frac{2F_K(X_{n_m})}{V_0}
\le\frac3{2h_0}\kappa^{-K_m}
<\frac98\,3^{-2^{m-1}},
\]
\[
D_m\frac{2F_K(X_{n_m})}{V_0}
<\frac94\left(\frac52\right)^m3^{-2^{m-1}}\longrightarrow0.      \tag{14}
\]

Unlike Line 1, the terminal payoff here is genuinely on a nonlinear part of the function, and every preceding step has multiple active atoms. The failure is the explicitly paid initial second moment, not an invalid interpolation row. It rules out only policy (12).

### Optimize the allocation, including the threshold

For fixed \(a_*\), (11) is a fractional-knapsack problem with resource \(\omega Z\). Its cost per resource unit is \(Z\). If \(0<d<1\), minimize \(J\) by filling the smallest positive \(Z\), equivalently the largest \(Y\), first. If \(d>1\), the coefficient in (11) is negative, so maximize \(J\) by filling the largest \(Z\), equivalently the smallest \(Y\), first. At most one atom needs a fractional split. Ties may be combined or resolved in the original atom order.

The threshold can also be optimized exactly. On an interval of \(a_*\) where the cutoff atom has value \(z\), let \(A\) be the set of fully selected atoms, with mass \(p_A\), first moment \(\mu_A\), and second moment \(\nu_A\). Directly eliminating the fractional mass gives

\[
J(a_*)=a_*\bigl(d-\mu_A+zp_A\bigr)+\nu_A-z\mu_A-zd,
\]
\[
J'(a_*)=d+\mathbb E[1_A(z-Y)].                                 \tag{15}
\]

For \(d>1\), \(A\) is a lower tail. Since its allocated deficit is at most \(d\),

\[
0\le\mathbb E[1_A(z-Y)]\le\mathbb E[1_A(a_*-Y)]\le d.
\]

The optimized value in (11) is consequently strictly increasing in \(a_*\), with piecewise derivative at least

\[
2h+2d(d^{-2}-1)=2+2/d>0.
\]

Its global minimum is at \(a_* =\max(d,q)\). The same endpoint is optimal for \(d=1\), when the coefficient of \(J\) vanishes.

For \(0<d<1\), \(A\) is an upper tail and (15) gives the variance derivative

\[
\frac{h^2}{d}-(d^{-2}-1)\mathbb E(Y-z)_+.                       \tag{16}
\]

As \(a_*\) increases, the greedy cutoff \(z\) can only move upward. Thus (16) is nondecreasing: the optimized predecessor variance is convex and piecewise affine in \(a_*\). Its slope is eventually \(h^2/d>0\). A minimum is attained at the endpoint \(\max(d,q)\) or at a feasible upper-tail breakpoint

\[
a_*=(d+\mathbb E[Y1_A])/\mathbb P(A),\qquad\mathbb P(A)>0.      \tag{17}
\]

This is a finite exact selection rule, not an instruction to run a solver. If several thresholds minimize the radius, choosing one requires retaining its resulting distribution for subsequent inverse steps. The same current radius does not make those distributions interchangeable.

### The sharp sqrt(2) relation and its actual guard

At \(h=\sqrt2\), \(d=\sqrt2-1\), the stop-loss level where (16) changes sign is

\[
\frac{hd}{1-d}=1.
\]

Let \(t_Y\) be the ACTUAL projection threshold of \(Y\), and let \(p=\mathbb P(Y>t_Y)\). Select those upper-tail atoms and put

\[
a_*=t_Y+h/p.
\]

Then (9) holds. This is feasible precisely when

\[
p(q-t_Y)\le h.                                                  \tag{18}
\]

The other bound \(a_*\ge d\) follows from \(t_Y\ge-1\) and \(p\le1\). With \(U=(Y-t_Y)_+\),

\[
J=(h-2)(a_*-t_Y)+\mathbb EU^2.
\]

Substitution into (11) gives the attained optimized variance identity

\[
V(X)=V(Y)+2\sqrt2 F_K(Y)+(2+\sqrt2)\mathbb E[g_Y^2].             \tag{19}
\]

Boundary mass at \(Y=t_Y\) can create an interval of equally optimal choices of \(p\) and \(a_*\). Guard (18) is necessary: it bounds the maximum active excess relative to its conditional mean. It need not hold for a general core law. Equation (19) is an attained one-step relation, not an all-depth construction.

### Exact inherited-leaf identity and a tested tail/gap family

One need not perturb (19). For ANY \(1<h<2\), put

\[
d=h-1,\qquad\gamma=\frac{dh}{2-h},\qquad
\mathbb E(Y-z)_+=\gamma.
\]

Select the upper tail above \(z\), with total selected mass \(p\), optionally including some mass at \(z\). Put \(a_*=z+(d+\gamma)/p\). The exact feasibility guard is

\[
p(q-z)\le d+\gamma.                                            \tag{19a}
\]

The other bound \(a_*\ge d\) follows from \(z\ge-\gamma\) and \(p\le1\). Writing \(U=(Y-z)_+\) gives

\[
J=(d-\gamma)(a_*-z)+\mathbb EU^2.
\]

Since \((d^{-2}-1)(d-\gamma)=-2h\), the coefficient of \(a_*\) in (11) cancels. When (19a) holds, the attained minimum is exactly

\[
V(X)=V(Y)+2h\gamma F_K(Y/\gamma)
       +\frac{h^2}{2-h}\|P_K(Y/\gamma)\|^2.                    \tag{19b}
\]

The appearances of \(Y/\gamma\) here are only evaluations in a variance identity. The actual physical step remains \(h\), with the original radius and the same function; no run is rescaled. Boundary mass at \(z\) can supply equal-cost choices of tail geometry and should not be dismissed as useless.

For the actual inherited leaf, \(\gamma_0=h_0a/(1-a)<1\), so (19b) applies with the actual parameter whenever its guard holds. The question is whether an exact finite tail family preserves that guard through the intervening bridges.

Here is an explicit test of the single upper atom plus separated lower atoms, allowing a boundary atom. Start backwards from terminal \(\pm R\), with \(R=2\). The first inverse leaf is the optimized symmetric law \(\pm r\), \(r=R+h_0\). To check this directly, the only threshold candidates for symmetric \(\pm R\), with \(R>d\), are \(a_*=R\) and \(a_*=R+2d\); their variance difference is \(2[R(d^{-1}-d)-h^2]\). Thus \(R>\gamma\) chooses the second candidate and gives \(\pm(R+h)\).

The next reversed physical step is the level-1 bridge \(h_b=1+\beta\), with \(d_b=\beta<1\). Its stop-loss level \(\gamma_b=\beta(1+\beta)/(1-\beta)\) exceeds \(r\): here \(\beta>5/6\), \((1-\beta)^{-1}\ge6144\), and \(r<7/2\). Also \(r>\beta\). The same candidate comparison therefore chooses \(a_*=r\), selecting only a fraction of the lower atom. Its exact three-atom output is

\[
u=r-h_b+2r/\beta,\quad v=r-h_b,\quad w=-r-h_b,
\]
\[
\mathbb P(u)=\beta/(2r),\quad
\mathbb P(v)=1/2,\quad
\mathbb P(w)=(r-\beta)/(2r).
\]

It has stop-loss EXACTLY one at the middle atom:

\[
\mathbb P(u)(u-v)=1.
\]

At the next actual inverse leaf, \(\gamma_0<1\), so its cutoff is

\[
z=v+(1-\gamma_0)\,2r/\beta>v.
\]

There is no atom at this cutoff. Only \(u\) is selected; (19a) holds, but there is no boundary mass to vary at equal cost. Its top preimage is \(u-h_0+h_0/\mathbb P(u)\), and every other atom is translated by \(-h_0\). The stop-loss at the translated middle atom becomes \(1+h_0\). Thus this concrete family re-enters a vertex trajectory at the actual leaf, rather than generating the sought simultaneous active-face freedom.

The same regeneration is exact at any later cap bridge that selects only the lowest atom. In particular, for a bridge \(h>2\), if the next law has maximum \(q>d\), minimum \(l\) of mass \(w_l\), and \(w_l(q-l)\ge d\), its greedy lower-tail cap inversion needs only that minimum atom. Its new unique upper atom has mass \(d/(q-l)\), sits \((q-l)/d\) above the largest left preimage, and again gives stop-loss one there. A following inherited leaf with \(\gamma_0<1\) selects only this upper atom. This is a vertex regime already excluded as a new mechanism by the task.

For genuinely nonvertex continuation, a bridge must instead select multiple distinct lower values, or a different family must arrange boundary mass at the actual \(\gamma_0\) cutoff. In the multiple-value case, (19a) requires the excess maximum divided by the conditional excess mean to be at most

\[
\frac{d+\gamma}{\gamma}=\frac2h.
\]

No bound propagating that concentration through all inherited bridges and physical copies was obtained. Nor was an admissible boundary-mass choice found that preserves it recursively while paying the initial variance. This is the precise missing guard for the tested extension; the one-atom calculation is not a proof that every optimized inverse law fails \(H_m\).

An unquantified substitution of (19) at the smaller inherited leaves would still be invalid. There are \(2^{m-1}\) leaves, and any alternative perturbative approach must retain that multiplicity. The exact identity (19b) avoids this perturbation but does not itself propagate its support guard.

### A genuine obstruction after full one-step optimization

Take \(h=2\). Equations (11) and the threshold optimization above give, for EVERY finite mean-zero next law,

\[
\min V(X)=V(Y)+4\max(1,\max Y)-4,                              \tag{20}
\]

where the minimum is over all allowed atom splits and thresholds in the exact inverse construction.

For any real \(A\ge1\), choose the terminal law

\[
Y=A\text{ with probability }\frac1{A^2+1},\qquad
Y=-1/A\text{ with probability }\frac{A^2}{A^2+1}.
\]

It has mean zero and variance one. Its minimum is at least \(-1\), so \(Y\in K\), \(g_Y=Y\), and \(2F_K(Y)=1\). Nevertheless (20) gives minimum predecessor variance \(4A-3\). For the symmetric law \(Y=\pm1\), the same terminal variance and objective give minimum predecessor variance one.

Attainment in the asymmetric example is explicit: set \(a_*=A\); assign right-branch probability \(1/A\) within the lower atom, and use either branch on the upper atom, where \(Z=0\). This satisfies (9). Hence the unbounded discrepancy persists after optimizing BOTH allocation and threshold; it is not an artifact of policy (12).

The conclusion is limited but decisive for a proposed scalar induction: terminal variance and terminal objective alone cannot control even one optimized inverse-radius payment. It does not prove failure of the optimized policy on \(H_m\), and it does not exclude a law family with additional tail control.

### Zero, unit, and subunit steps

The inverse construction can define a fixed finite law for every nonnegative word, including the missing edge cases. These extensions give realizability, not the target bound.

For \(h=0\), take \(X=Y\). For \(0<h<1\), let \(r=1-h\) and choose the unique \(a_*\) satisfying \(\mathbb E(Y-a_*)_+=r\). Set

\[
X=Y-h+\frac h r(Y-a_*)_+,\qquad t=a_*-h.
\]

The map has mean zero and is the actual inverse on each monotone branch. Also \(a_*\ge-r\), so \(t\ge-1\). Its variance is

\[
V(X)=V(Y)+2ha_*-h^2+
 \frac{h(2-h)}{(1-h)^2}\mathbb E(Y-a_*)_+^2.                    \tag{21}
\]

For \(h=1\), write \(a_* =\max Y\), \(p=\mathbb P(Y=a_*)>0\). Take \(X=Y-1\) off the maximum atoms and \(X=a_*-1+1/p\) on them, with threshold \(a_*-1\). Then

\[
V(X)=V(Y)+2a_*-1+1/p.                                         \tag{22}
\]

The common maximum preimage minimizes variance among the possible unit-step preimages by the elementary conditional second-moment inequality. Formulas (21) and (22) retain the same probabilities and original radius.

## What the optimized finite-law attempt does and does not select

One fully definite candidate for any fixed word is now available: begin with the terminal law \(\pm2\), use (21), (22), or the identity for steps at most one, and for steps above one minimize (11) by (15)--(17), choosing the least minimizing threshold and resolving any cutoff tie in atom order. A step above one splits at most one atom, so at most \(n+2\) atoms are needed. Reversing the entire known word once fixes its initial law and its function before the forward run. Its exact normalized payoff is \(3/V_0\).

The requested estimate for this candidate would be

\[
V_0\le\frac{3B_n}{1-o(1)}
\]

uniformly over all words, with a word-independent asymptotic threshold. No such estimate was obtained. Even on the inherited \(H_m\), no closed bound of the form \(V_0\le3B_{n_m}(1+o(1))\) was obtained. The formulas do not establish it. Greedy one-step variance minimization is not known to minimize the original variance across several inverse steps, because its choices change the tail geometry seen by earlier steps.

The missing payment is therefore concrete: control the changing upper support, the stop-loss cutoff and any threshold tie choices of the ACTUAL fixed law through every physical copy of each child, while keeping the accumulated original variance below the exact benchmark scale. Equation (20) proves that replacing those data by variance and objective loses arbitrarily much in general. The actual-parameter identity (19b) still requires propagation of guard (19a); the tested single-upper-atom family regenerates a vertex regime and does not supply that nonvertex propagation. Neither gap is routine or resolved here.

## Scope audit and close

- Every displayed lower-witness function is globally convex and 1-smooth in a finite-dimensional Euclidean space and has attained unique minimum zero. The finite transfer in Line 1 is explicit.
- Each original-radius denominator is the variance of the one initial law, with no restart, independent child law, stepsize rescaling, or new minimizer.
- Formula (14) concerns a specified nonoptimized independent-branch law. Formula (20) concerns one-step optimized inversion and obstructs a particular scalar induction. Neither bounds the supremum over all finite laws.
- The actual inherited parameters and all physical multiplicities are retained. The sqrt(2) identity is not silently applied to smaller inherited leaves.
- The inverse rules cover zero and arbitrary finite nonnegative steps, but no uniform performance theorem follows from their existence.
- There is no complete affirmative or negative original-target candidate to submit for formal review.

The one-invocation allocation is exhausted. Stop after saving these documents; no renewal or further mechanism is authorized by this report.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000846 -->
# R9: select one fixed finite law whose nonvertex dynamics give the target payoff

Fresh ONE gpt-6-astra/ultra analytical invocation, <=TWO substantive proof lines, mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions ZERO. Consult OFF. Parent owns ledger. Read current OPERATOR.md, COMPLETE original PROBLEM.md and research/R8_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261009.md. Human explicitly asks for direct original-target closure, not more equivalent formulations or fixed-horizon toys. All old grants are CLOSED.

## Original goal

Prove W_n(h)>=(1-o(1))/B_n uniformly over ALL sufficiently large n and EVERY predetermined nonnegative word, with W=2R, every finite-dimensional convex L-smooth function with attained minimum, original selected radius and actual last iterate; or give a certified full-class asymptotic separation. B_n is the exact original all-integer-split Zhang--Jiang profile, not a decimal asymptotic estimate. C0=B0=1, C_n=max Omega(C_k,C_l), B_n=max G(C_k,B_l), k+l=n-1 over every integer split; Omega=(x+y+sqrt(x*x+6*x*y+y*y))/2 and G=2x+y/2+sqrt(y*y+8*x*y)/2.

## Actual finite-dimensional model -- starting data, not the deliverable

Choose ONCE a finite probability space with M>=2 atoms and fixed positive probabilities p_i. Work in its mean-zero L2 subspace E={X:E X=0}, with norm squared E X^2. This is an ordinary Euclidean space of dimension M-1 under the isometry X_i ->sqrt(p_i)X_i. Let

    K={g:E g=0, g_i>=-1},
    F_K(X)=max_(g in K)(E[Xg]-E[g^2]/2).

Parent manual formulas to independently derive before use: K is a compact centered simplex with0 in its relative interior, F_K is globally convex1-smooth on E with unique minimum0, and the actual projection is

    g_i=(X_i-t)_+-1,   E[(X-t)_+]=1.

The threshold t is uniquely determined by the current ACTUAL law, with t>=-1. Every physical GD step is

    X_i' =X_i-h[(X_i-t)_+-1], E X'=0.

The same fixed probabilities and the same K are retained throughout the run. The exact original squared radius is E X0^2, and

    F_K(X)=t+1/2+(1/2)E[(X-t)_+^2]
          =(t+1)+(1/2)E[g^2].

Thus the normalized terminal payoff is [2t_n+1+E[(X_n-t_n)_+^2]]/E X0^2. Every old/cross projection row is automatic if this actual dynamics is used. Do not prescribe independent thresholds or replace each child by a newly optimized law. No time/stepsize rescaling or new initial radius is allowed.

This is the COMPLETE nonvertex evolution of this simplex family, not merely the R5 itinerary using one vertex per block. Simultaneously active atoms may create coupled face motion. The family is not assumed universal for all smooth convex functions. Deriving its projection formula or rewriting the problem as an optimization over laws is not target progress; the task is to choose a law and prove its quantitative terminal payoff.

## Decisive whole-family test, then arbitrary-word selection

For each enclosing m>=1536 use the SAME eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt2)(1+eps), a=beta/c in H0 empty and H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j). Set S_j=sum H_j, D_m=1+2S_m and n_m=2^m-1. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 gives B_n/D_m<47/50. R6_QUADRATIC_BASELINE_BOUND_20261009.md is an UNVERIFIED all-quadratic bound<=9/(16D_m). The specified noncrossing Huber witness is1/D_m. This portfolio misses the needed1/B_n; it is not all possible Huber crossings or all functions.

First construct an explicit finite initial law (M and weights may depend on the whole known word and on m) that gives a genuine nonlinear terminal payoff at the required1/B_n scale for the ENTIRE inherited-m family. Retain exact physical multiplicities and establish constants uniformly in m. If a continuum law helps derive the candidate, prove the quantitative finite-atom transfer for each actual word, with original norm and sufficient error at the target scale; an infinite-dimensional example alone is inadmissible. No numerical scan or finite m table is requested.

Then seek a proved way to select the law for arbitrary words, handling zero and arbitrarily large finite steps, with the exact B_n profile and a word-independent asymptotic threshold. Passing H_m alone is not the original solution. A complete finite-law construction plus a closed payoff estimate is the necessary deliverable; an unproved shape recurrence is not one.

Possible analytical tools include quantile/stop-loss transforms, finite-field or dyadic atom structures, and convex-order/second-moment inequalities. They are tools, not extra proof lines. A second substantive line may repair a specific quantitative failure of the first initial-law family, but it must still select a real fixed law and seek the same target estimate.

## Preserve the joint gaps if trying to glue atom groups

Splitting the initial probability space into two fixed groups can embed child laws, but both group means and the between-group variance are real parts of the initial radius. An inactive group has gradient-1, so its mean drifts even while its centered internal coordinates remain unchanged. On an active group of probability p, scaling the child centered coordinates by1/p can preserve the original physical child steps; derive the exact whole-history threshold guards before using this idea.

For any actual scalar coordinate law and a bridge b>1, the map X-b[(X-t)_+-1] has maximum at the threshold and is at most t+b. A wholesale switch of active groups therefore needs a REAL gap around that threshold; continuous density at the threshold can destroy the desired strict switch. Do not assume free child support/height menus, automatically dormant coordinates, or lossless gluing. If finite lacunary atoms are used, prove their threshold gaps on every physical query. Alternatively allow genuinely overlapping active groups and analyze the actual shared threshold without a fictional switch.

R5/R6 centered-simplex vertex paths, common-normal repairs and rightmost-spine rules have exact scoped limitations. R7 unit-positive-curvature scalar returns and R8 tangent-disk/area sectors also did not supply the target. None rules out this complete nonvertex finite-law family, but do not rename their excluded trajectories as a new mixture.

## Bounded close

Text/primary-source reads, manual reasoning, Markdown and hashes only. No mathematical code/calculators, old numerical arrays, new models/agents, paid consult, private worker memory, worker/service/truth-store changes, publication/finalization/revocation/shutdown or Goal mutation. Write complete self-contained PROOF_ATTEMPT.md and HANDOFF.md here, all new mathUNVERIFIED. If no target-scale law is obtained, state the concrete quantitative failure/gap rather than presenting this model as an equivalent solution. Then STOP; parent full-reads and closes. No automatic renewal.
