# Independent analytical audit of the frozen finite phase-cover candidate

**Status: COMPLETE INDEPENDENT UNVERIFIED AUDIT. No formal gate was called.**

Auditor: worker max3, project `gd-exact-profile-20260926`, assigned round 47.
The audited object is the COMPLETE final finding `0fc70796cd27f96d`, not a
planning snapshot:

`/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/shared_evidence/0fc70796cd27f96d.md`

Its identity was checked: 24,219 bytes, 633 lines, SHA256
`2a4a47e8ae949bb29e1173fea20576bced46e3376ab3f1b1b5ad6839cd021509`.
The entire object was read. Identity is not correctness. The source was not
edited or silently repaired.

**Verdict:** every mathematical interface listed below survives this analytical
audit in its stated conditional scope. I found no failing step, counterexample,
or missing hypothesis requiring a repair. This is an independent research
judgment supported by the detailed reasoning below, NOT verifier acceptance.
There is no new fact, no actual sufficient-capacity seed, and no solution of
the original unrestricted exact-profile problem.

The accepted inputs are `04219554e41d9750` and, for the already established
fixed-b=3 comparison only, `4c92d3fc4eb6153e`. The first fact's proof explicitly
contains the general-length seed condition and substitution in Sections 10--12;
its headline four-step consequence does not restrict those proved interfaces.
I read its relevant proof Sections 1--3, 7, and 9--12, and the second fact's
original seed/lift and scope Sections 6--7. Accepted finite endpoint data were
used only through those accepted statements. They were not recomputed.

## Claim-by-claim verdict table

Here PASS means that the displayed reasoning survived this UNVERIFIED audit.
It does not promote any new claim to the fact graph. FAIL would require an
exact contradiction; GAP would mean an unresolved necessary inference.

| Claim | Frozen section | Verdict | Audit evidence |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Original function class and W=2R normalization | 1 | PASS | A below |
| Every nonnegative seed step, every original off-diagonal sign, zero diagonal, exact flow, full PSD | 1 | PASS | A |
| General seed length, arbitrary PSD remainder, zero-step formal coordinates | 1--2 | PASS | A; accepted proof Sections 10--12 |
| Only basic-tree substitution; untouched dominant leaf; actual mass and length | 2 | PASS | A |
| Two-sided ray enclosure, enclosure width, and bounded physical offset | 2 | PASS | B |
| Integer-ray monotonicity and exact dyadic scaling | 3 | PASS | C |
| Upper telescoping product, orientation, and P_pc >= 1 | 4 | PASS | C |
| Positive margin and one fixed good phase in a finite list | 4 | PASS | C |
| Actual horizons, mass convergence, and uniformity over all tree ties | 5 | PASS | D |
| Certified finite phase selection, including rational outward enclosures | 6 | PASS | E |
| Ceiling cases, denominator positivity, and strict gap for every j >= J | 6 | PASS | F |
| Equivalence of a finite unanchored cover and A > (r+1)^rho | 7 | PASS | G |
| Anchored comparison and recovery of the accepted fixed-b=3 interface | 7 | PASS | G |
| Length five, A >= 49/5, 17 phases, gap 1/40000 | 8.1 | PASS | H |
| Length six, A >= 12, 11 phases, gap 1/80000 | 8.2 | PASS | I |
| Length five, A >= 197/20, nine anchored phases, gap 1/8000 | 8.3 | PASS | J |
| Complete theorem uses one selected phase on an unbounded physical sequence | 9 | PASS | D--F |
| No arbitrary-template inflation, exponent ceiling, seed existence, or target closure inferred | 10 | PASS | K |

There are no FAIL or GAP entries within the claimed conditional theorem.
The missing seed and the open original target are expressly outside what this
candidate claims to supply; their absence is not being relabeled as success.

## A. Original model, full seed, and actual construction

For a nonnegative length-n schedule h, W_n(h) is the supremum of

    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2)

over all finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, convex differentiable functions
with L-Lipschitz gradient, minimizers x_star, and x_0 != x_star, with L > 0 and
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i). This is exactly the accepted W=2R convention.

The scalar benchmark retains every integer split:

    Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,
    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,  lambda=1+sqrt(2),  rho=log_2(lambda).

For the actual seed, r >= 1 is its physical length, h_i >= 0 for 0 <= i < r,
s=sum h_i, A=1+s, and m=r+1. Lambda is an entire real (r+1)-square array with
Lambda_ii=0 and Lambda_ij >= 0 for all r(r+1) ordered distinct pairs. Let e
be the all-ones column, let T have diagonal one and T_it=h_t for t<i, with
zero entries above its diagonal, and set

    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
    D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)).

The required conditions are exactly

    L_flow^T e=omega,
    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >= 0 on R^(r+1).

These match accepted `04219554e41d9750`, proof Section 10, word for word in
mathematical scope. At L=1 its identity is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j) Lambda_ij Q_ij = <M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)
        +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_r||^2.

Here f_i and g_i are the value and gradient at x_i, Gram is their inner-product
matrix, and the matrix pairing is the trace pairing. Actual smooth convex
trajectories have Q_ij <= 0. Nonnegative multipliers and full PSD therefore
give V_h >= 0. The candidate needs only this sufficient implication.

No zero step deletes a formal gradient or identifies two coordinates. No
assumption requires h_i >= 1, symmetry of Lambda, prescribed support, rank,
product balance, or M_prim=0. The accepted composition retains arbitrary PSD
child remainders on the original indices. Thus these requirements are not
quietly narrowed when the seed is inserted into a tree.

A fully expanded maximizing B_n tree has n internal bridge nodes, n primitive
empty leaves, and one dominant empty leaf. Expanding each C child uses its
original recurrence; the maximizing B split is not assumed balanced. Replace
each primitive empty leaf by the length-r seed and leave the last dominant
leaf empty with mass one. For positive actual primitive capacities A_left,
A_right, the primitive bridge is 1+d_pp, where

    d_pp(A_left+A_right+d_pp)=A_left A_right,  d_pp>0.

For right dominant mass D_right=1+2sum(right), the dominant bridge is

    1+A_left/zeta,  zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A_left/D_right))/2.

These are precisely the physical joins of accepted Sections 11--12. All
capacities are at least one, so all displayed bridges exist and are positive.
The resulting word Hsub_n has mass Dsub_n=1+2sum Hsub_n and satisfies

    length(Hsub_n)=n+nr=mn,
    W_(mn)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n,
    (sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1))^2 <= Dsub_n <= A B_n.       (A1)

The square-root lower bound can be squared: its left side before squaring is
sqrt(A)(sqrt(B_n)-1)+1 >= 1. The loss sqrt(A)-1 pays for the one uninflated
dominant leaf. Treating that leaf as physically having mass A would give a
different and unjustified construction, but the candidate does not do so.
The equality in W uses the accepted full-function upper certificate and its
matching Huber example, not a restriction to a test family.

At n=0 the actual word remains empty, Dsub_0=1, and (A1) remains valid.
If every seed step is zero, A=1; the strict cover hypothesis below cannot hold.
This is a consequence of that hypothesis, not an excluded seed boundary.

## B. Accepted ray inputs and the width estimate

For each positive integer b, the accepted finite positive limit is

    Z_b=lim_(j->infinity) lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1).

Write, as in the candidate,

    L_pc(b,j)=lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1),
    U_pc(b,j)=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(b*2^j))+3/100)^2,
    E(b,j)=8sqrt(2)b^(rho-1)2^(-j)
            +(3/25)(b+1)^(rho/2)lambda^(-j/2)
            +(9/10000)lambda^(-j).

Accepted Sections 7 and 9 give L_pc <= Z_b <= U_pc. The candidate additionally
uses the width U_pc-L_pc <= E, not merely Z_b-L_pc <= E. This stronger-looking
usage is warranted by the actual accepted derivation. With M=b*2^j, subtracting
the endpoints gives exactly

    U_pc-L_pc
      =lambda^(-j)[B_M-B_(M-1)+(3/50)sqrt(B_M)+9/10000].

The accepted adjacent estimate bounds its first term by
8sqrt(2)b^(rho-1)2^(-j). The accepted bound B_M <= 4(M+1)^rho bounds its
second term by (3/25)(b+1)^(rho/2)lambda^(-j/2). This proves the stated width
bound without a new recurrence calculation.

For a fixed integer d >= 0 with a defined index, the same accepted proof gives

    0 <= Z_b-lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1-d)
       <= E(b,j)+8sqrt(2)d b^(rho-1)2^(-j).              (B1)

Also accepted are 1<rho<4/3, B_n>=C_n, and
C_(b*2^j-1)=lambda^j C_(b-1), with C_(b-1)>=1. Consequently
L_pc(b,j)>=1 and Z_b>=1. These facts justify the denominators and the
absolute-to-relative error conversion used later.

## C. Product direction and a fixed good index

The admissible split k=0 gives B_(n+1)>=G(1,B_n)>B_n for n>=0. Thus if
positive integers b<=c, the finite terms at b*2^j-1 and c*2^j-1 are ordered;
taking their established limits proves Z_b<=Z_c. Strictness of these limits
is not needed. For an integer ell>=0, changing the limiting index yields

    Z_(2^ell b)=lambda^ell Z_b.                         (C1)

This proof remains entirely on integer b. It supplies no real-base phase
function and does not need continuity or density of phases.

Let b0,d,K be positive integers, ell>=0 an integer, and C_pc>0 a proved upper
bound for Z_d/Z_b0. Suppose m^K b0<=2^ell d and put

    b_i=b0 m^i  (0<=i<=K),  P_pc=lambda^ell C_pc.

The entire product has the required upper orientation:

    product_(i=0)^(K-1) Z_(m b_i)/Z_b_i
      =Z_(m^K b0)/Z_b0
      <=Z_(2^ell d)/Z_b0
      =lambda^ell Z_d/Z_b0 <= P_pc.                    (C2)

The first ratio on the right is at least one by monotonicity. Hence P_pc>=1,
and A^K>P_pc implies A>1. Let 1<a<=A and gamma>0 satisfy

    a^K>P_pc,  K gamma<=1-P_pc/a^K.

Since P_pc/a^K is strictly between zero and one, gamma<1. Bernoulli's
inequality gives (1-gamma)^K>=1-K gamma>=P_pc/a^K. The induction is valid
on [0,1]: multiplication by 1-gamma preserves order, and the correction at
the next step is K gamma^2>=0. If every

    q_i(a)=Z_(m b_i)/(a Z_b_i)

were greater than 1-gamma, their product would exceed (1-gamma)^K and hence
P_pc/a^K, contradicting (C2). A single fixed i therefore has

    Z_(m b_i)/(A Z_b_i)<=q_i(a)<=1-gamma.               (C3)

This includes K=1 and equality in the permitted gamma inequality. It is
about one member of a finite list of integer ratios. The power A^K has not
been interpreted as the capacity of a physically iterated arbitrary seed.

## D. Physical offsets and uniform limits

For each 0<=i<K and j>=0 define

    n_(i,j)=b_i 2^j-1,
    N_(i,j)=m n_(i,j)=m b_i 2^j-m,
    H_(i,j)=Hsub_(n_(i,j)).

For the numerator the accepted shifted ray is b=m b_i, d=m-1. Indeed
N_(i,j)=m b_i 2^j-1-(m-1). Its index is nonnegative even when n_(i,j)=0.
Equation (B1) thus gives lambda^(-j) B_(N_(i,j)) -> Z_(m b_i).

For the denominator, write c_A=sqrt(A)-1>=0. Equation (A1) implies

    0<=A Z_b_i-lambda^(-j)Dsub_(n_(i,j))
      <=A E(b_i,j)
        +2c_A sqrt(A Z_b_i)lambda^(-j/2).              (D1)

To check its direction, start with A(Z_b_i-L_pc(b_i,j)) and add the loss
between A B_n and Dsub_n; then discard the negative term -c_A^2 lambda^(-j).
Use lambda^(-j)B_n<=Z_b_i only inside the nonnegative square root. This
establishes (D1), and its left side follows from Dsub_n<=A B_n.

Consequently the exact ratio limit is

    B_(N_(i,j)) W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))
       -> Z_(m b_i)/(A Z_b_i).                         (D2)

All exponents of b in E and (B1) are positive. Monotonicity of Z_b and the
largest b_i therefore provide common errors tending to zero over the finite
list. The denominator limits are >=A>0, and (A1) is independent of tree
depth and all maximizing-split ties. This proves the asserted uniformity
without any limit over an infinite collection of phases.

For a fixed selected i, N_(i,j+1)-N_(i,j)=m b_i 2^j>0 and N_(i,j) tends to
infinity. The words at different horizons can be independently prescribed;
no prefix nesting or anytime property is assumed.

## E. Finite certified selection and arbitrary-real input scope

The candidate tests

    F_i(t)=U_pc(m b_i,t)/(a L_pc(b_i,t)).

Each denominator is positive, and the numerator upper bound divided by the
denominator lower bound gives F_i(t)>=q_i(a). The endpoint width in B tends
to zero, so F_i(t)->q_i(a). For the index in (C3), its limit is at most
1-gamma, strictly below 1-gamma/2. Some finite t therefore passes

    F_i(t)<1-gamma/2.

Selecting the first successful stage and the smallest successful index
freezes an index once. No choice is subsequently made as a function of j,
the objective, the dimension, or the realized trajectory.

For rational a and gamma the outward rational version is also valid. At
stage t>=1 approximate the lower L_pc and upper U_pc endpoints with absolute
error at most 2^(-t). A positive lower denominator can always be retained,
since L_pc>=1. The resulting rational quotient remains an upper bound for
q_i(a) and converges to it for each fixed i. Hence its strict rational test
also terminates. All finite benchmark values involve a finite number of
additions, products, positive square roots, and maxima. Rational bracketing
of each square root and maxima of lower/upper endpoints provide arbitrarily
accurate outward bounds. This proves an in-principle finite procedure; it
does not assert a feasible running time or report an executed search.

There is no oracle assumption for an arbitrary real seed. From A^K>P_pc>=1,
one can choose rational a strictly between P_pc^(1/K) and A, and then a
positive rational gamma no larger than (1-P_pc/a^K)/K. Their existence is
mathematical; certification must accompany the effective input. Once those
certified rational numbers are supplied, the selection uses only them and
the benchmark. The exact-real rule and the rational implementation need not
select the same index; every successful index has the property used below.

The proof establishes finite termination, not a practical stage count. The
explicit J in the next section controls the output horizon. The search
depth t and the output depth j serve different purposes; no assertion that
t<=J is needed or used.

## F. Explicit common horizon, ceilings, and strictness

Let b_max=b0 m^(K-1), u_j=lambda^(-j/2), and q=(2/3)^j. The exact elementary
bounds sqrt(2)>5/4, lambda>9/4, sqrt(lambda)>3/2, sqrt(2)<3/2, and
1<rho<4/3 yield, for b>=1 and j>=0,

    E(b,j)+2u_j
       <[12b+(3/25)(b+1)+9/10000+2]q
       <=[12+6/25+9/10000+2]b q <15b q.                (F1)

For example, b^(rho-1)<=b, (b+1)^(rho/2)<=b+1, and b+1<=2b.
Also 2^(-j), lambda^(-j), and lambda^(-j/2) are each <=q. Strictness
in (F1) persists even at j=0, since the coefficient bound for sqrt(2) is
strict. No decimal estimate of rho is required.

Set

    J=ceil(log(60 b_max/gamma)/log(3/2)).

The argument of the numerator logarithm exceeds 60, since gamma<1 and
b_max>=1. Thus J is positive. For every integer j>=J,
q<=(gamma/(60 b_max)), including the case where the logarithmic quotient
is an integer. Equation (F1) then gives, strictly,

    E(b_i,j)+2u_j<gamma/4.                             (F2)

The ceiling equality case loses no strictness because (F1) was strict.
An equivalent definition of J is the least nonnegative integer passing
(3/2)^J>=60 b_max/gamma, so the definition does not require a numerical log.

Put E=E(b_i,j). From Z_b_i>=1 and Z_b_i-L_pc<=E,

    B_(n_(i,j))>=lambda^j(Z_b_i-E)
                 >=lambda^j Z_b_i(1-E).

The latter direction follows from E(Z_b_i-1)>=0. Also
B_(n_(i,j))>=C_(n_(i,j))>=lambda^j, so

    ell_n=(1-A^(-1/2))/sqrt(B_n)<=u_j.

Here 0<=ell_n<=u_j<1 by (F2). Using (A1), and using the LOWER enclosure
L_pc(m b_i,j)<=Z_(m b_i) for the benchmark numerator, gives

    B_(N_(i,j)) W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))
      <= [Z_(m b_i)/(A Z_b_i)] / [(1-E)(1-u_j)^2].     (F3)

The physical numerator satisfies B_N<=B_(m b_i 2^j-1)<=lambda^j Z_(m b_i),
so the offset has not been replaced by an equality. Both denominator factors
in (F3) are positive. For 0<=E,u<1,

    (1-E)(1-u)^2=1-E-2u+2Eu+(1-E)u^2>=1-E-2u.

Thus (F2) makes the denominator strictly greater than 1-gamma/4. The fixed
selected index from E has actual ratio <1-gamma/2. Consequently

    B_(N_(i,j)) W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))
      <(1-gamma/2)/(1-gamma/4)<1-gamma/4               (F4)

for EVERY j>=J. The last step is exactly
(1-gamma/4)^2-(1-gamma/2)=gamma^2/16>0. Since B_N>0, (F4) is the claimed
strict inequality W_N<(1-gamma/4)U_N^ZJ in the original normalization.

## G. Finite exponent interface and anchored comparisons

With b0=d=1 and C_pc=1, the finite sufficient conditions become

    m^K<=2^ell,  A^K>lambda^ell.

They imply A^K>m^(K rho), because
lambda^ell=(2^ell)^rho>=m^(K rho); hence A>m^rho. Conversely, if A>m^rho,
choose K>=1 with K log(A/m^rho)>log(lambda), and set
ell=ceil(K log_2 m). Then

    m^K<=2^ell<2m^K,
    lambda^ell<lambda m^(K rho)<A^K.

The strict upper inequality remains true if K log_2 m is itself an integer.
Here m>=2 ensures ell>=0. This proves the claimed equivalence using a
finite integer cover, not an external exponent ceiling for seed capacity.

For a certified comparison Z_d/Z_b0<=C_pc, the same proof gives the anchored
interface A>(lambda^ell C_pc)^(1/K). At K=1, ell=0, b0=3 and d=3m, the
accepted constants Gamma5_c107 and Gamma6_c107 in `4c92d3fc4eb6153e` are valid
choices of C_pc for r=5 and r=6. Thus the capacity hypotheses of that fact
are recovered. Its sharper specialized finite-depth estimates are neither
weakened as facts nor claimed to follow from the coarser J here.

## H. Exact length-five, seventeen-phase corollary

The common radical comparisons can be checked before either corollary:

    lambda^4=17+12sqrt(2),
    lambda^5=41+29sqrt(2),
    lambda^10=3363+2378sqrt(2).

The conjugate in the last line is lambda^(-10)>0, giving
lambda^10<6726<6750. Since 17^2>2*12^2 and 99^2>2*70^2,

    lambda<29/12,  lambda<169/70,  lambda^4<34.         (H1)

For m=6, a=49/5, K=17 and ell=44, the cover is equivalent to
3^17<=2^27. The exact integers are 129140163<134217728.

For the capacity side, the factor checks are

    49^4-9220*5^4=2301>0,
    922^2=850084,  675^2=455625,
    200*850084-373*455625=68675>0,
    49*373^2-170*200^2=17321>0.

The second comparison gives (675/922)^4<(200/373)^2; therefore (H1) and
a^4>9220 imply

    lambda^44/a^17
      <170*675^4/(49*922^4)
      <6800000/6817321<1.

The margin needed for gamma=1/10000 is certified by

    17321*10000=173210000 >115894457=17*6817321.

Thus 17 gamma<1-lambda^44/a^17 even at A=a. The phase list and actual
horizons are b_i=6^i for 0<=i<=16 and N_(i,j)=6^(i+1)2^j-6. Substitution
in F gives exactly

    J5=ceil(log(600000*6^16)/log(3/2)),
    W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))<(1-1/40000)U_(N_(i,j))^ZJ

for the fixed selected i and every j>=J5. The capacity comparison to 99/10
has exact difference 1/10. The endpoint A=49/5 is included because the
capacity-power and margin comparisons above are strict.

## I. Exact length-six, eleven-phase corollary

For m=7, a=12, K=11 and ell=31,

    7^11=1977326743<2147483648=2^31.

The identity 6750=(125/32)12^3 and (H1) give

    lambda^31/12^11
      <6750^3*(169/70)/12^11
      =125^3*169/(32^3*70*12^2)
      =330078125/330301440<1.

The difference of denominator and numerator is 223315. For gamma=1/20000,

    223315*20000=4466300000>3633315840=11*330301440.

This proves the required gamma inequality at A=12 as well as for larger A.
The phases are b_i=7^i for 0<=i<=10, the true horizons are
N_(i,j)=7^(i+1)2^j-7, and the resulting formulae are exactly

    J6=ceil(log(1200000*7^10)/log(3/2)),
    W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))<(1-1/80000)U_(N_(i,j))^ZJ

for the selected i and every j>=J6. The capacity 12 is below 1201/100 by
exactly 1/100.

## J. Exact length-five, nine-phase anchored corollary

Use m=6, a=197/20, K=9, ell=21, b0=3, d=15, and C_pc=19299/2500. The
accepted comparison is Z_15/Z_3<C_pc; its direction is the upper bound
required in (C2). The cover reduces to 3^9<=5*2^12, namely 19683<20480.

The elementary bounds are

    197^3-950*20^3=45373>0,
    4*19299=77196<77500=31*2500.

Hence a^3>950 and C_pc<31/4. With (H1),

    lambda^21 C_pc/a^9
      <[6750^2*(29/12)*(31/4)]/950^3
      =899*135^2/(48*50*19^3)
      =16384275/16461600
      =218457/219488<1.

The factor reduction uses 6750=135*50 and 950=19*50. The displayed difference
is 77325 before division by 75, and 1031 after it. For gamma=1/2000,

    1031*2000=2062000>1975392=9*219488.

Thus the gamma condition holds strictly at the floor. There are nine phases
b_i=3*6^i, 0<=i<=8, with true horizons N_(i,j)=18*6^i*2^j-6. The common depth
is exactly J5a=ceil(log(360000*6^8)/log(3/2)), giving gap 1/8000 for every
j>=J5a on the one selected phase. The floor 197/20 is below 99/10 by 1/20
and above 49/5 by 1/20, as the candidate states.

At this same K=9, 2^23=8388608<10077696=6^9<16777216=2^24, so 24 is the
least unanchored exponent. Moreover C_pc<31/4<14<lambda^3, using
lambda^3=7+5sqrt(2) and sqrt(2)>7/5. Thus the anchored scalar product bound
lambda^21 C_pc is strictly smaller than lambda^24. These bounds apply to
different lists of rays; their comparison does not order the true optimal
phases, and the candidate makes no such inference.

## K. What survived, what was not checked, and final scope

The complete conditional theorem survived: an ACTUAL seed satisfying all
original full-certificate conditions and a strict finite cover has a finite
list of genuine basic-tree schedule families, a terminating certified rule
selecting one fixed index, and a strict relative improvement gamma/4 for
every output depth j>=J on that index's unbounded physical horizon sequence.
The sufficient interface A>(r+1)^rho and all three advertised exact rational
specializations survived. No correction of the frozen proof was needed.

The following were not performed or established:

* No actual qualifying seed or full multiplier array was constructed or
  checked. Conditional seeds may have arbitrary allowed PSD remainders and
  zero steps; existence is still a separate open requirement.
* No phase search, benchmark recurrence, floating-point evaluation, solver,
  parameter search, machine algebra, proof checker, or fact_submit was run.
  The integer and algebraic inequalities above were checked by exact textual
  reasoning. File hashes and persistence checks are administrative only.
* No accepted fact, its finite certificate, or its external literature input
  was reopened for a new formal verification. Their stated proved interfaces
  were read and used in scope. A mandatory narrow literature recall returned
  no needed additional premise; no external result was imported here.
* No particular numerical phase was identified, no efficiency bound for the
  finite selection procedure was claimed, and no global phase optimum or
  all-horizon/anytime guarantee was established.
* No capacity ceiling for general seeds, arbitrary-template inflation,
  physical construction of a capacity-A^K seed, or original exact-profile
  theorem follows from this audit.

This one assigned audit is complete. Its PASS judgments remain UNVERIFIED;
only a separately authorized formal gate can place a new fact in the graph.
The original target remains OPEN. No formal authorization or further worker
round is inferred from completion of this report.

# Handoff: boundary-clock transport and the fixed-fan obstruction

**JOINT UNVERIFIED. Original target OPEN.** Guidance `1eaef2e700c54cd4`; one fresh `gpt-6-astra` / `ultra` analytical invocation. Two complete laws exhausted; mathematical programs, arrays, solves and formal calls zero. Parent owns registration/closeout.

TASK SHA256: `a042d1e62a2b539ec5c4518d6ce447fc74cf6be384e1864fadd92d1250f91e38`.

## Fixed setting

For enclosing `m>=1536`, all children use `eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c, b=1+a`. Let `H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j)`, `D=1+2sum H_m`, `q=17/(16D)`. The prefix deletes only the final update, retaining query `l=2^m-2`.

The task-fixed source is `mu=2pi^(m)`, `nu=0`, where `u^(j)=H_j/S_j`, `alpha=1/(2c)`, `gamma=1/sqrt(c)`, `zeta=1-alpha-gamma`, and `pi^(j)=(alpha u^(j-1),zeta,gamma pi^(j-1))`, `pi^(1)=(1)`. The base fan is `lambda0_ij=1_(i=l)mu_j` for distinct queries, with minimizer rows `lambda_(i,star)=mu_i`, `lambda_(star,i)=0`.

Every original row remains. The complete full matrix is defined by `N_ij=-lambda_ij`, `N_ii=mu_i+sum_(j!=i)lambda_ij`, original chronological `L`, `B=sym(NL)`, and

`Kres=B-a^2 e_l e_l^T-mu mu^T/(4q)`.

No sharp contact, discarded gradient, diagonal remainder or primitive-energy shortcut is imposed. Actual last-step descent remains `2f_after<=2f_l-(1-a^2)||g_l||^2`.

## The two concrete laws

At every nonleaf dyadic node, choose left/right probabilities proportional to `h_i t_J(i)^s`, where `t_J(i)=1+sum_(j in J,j<i)h_j`. The last deleted update's `h_l=b` is only a proof weight, never a GD update in `L`. Add the exact circulation bridge→left→right→bridge, with coefficients `theta pL`, `theta pL pR`, `theta pR`, to the fixed fan. Every duplicate original row is added. This proves nonnegative coefficients and exact value flow.

- **Law 1:** `s=1`, `theta_v=mu_(r_v)^2/(4q)`. The complete query-zero outgoing mass is the sum over ALL leftmost ancestors. Every proper ancestor contributes more than `1/(160H)`, where `H=S_(m-1)`. Thus `Kres_00<-3/H<0` at all stated depths. The root order mismatch has changed, but accumulated traffic still fails.
- **Law 2:** `s=2`, `theta_v=[mu_(r_v)^2/(4q)-mu_(r_v)]_+`. The report proves `Z2(H_d)>S_d^3/4`, treats the inactive depth-two ancestor, and bounds every proper ancestor plus the root. This gives `out_0<1/(3H)` and `Kres_00>2/(125H)>0`. Nevertheless root saturation gives `Kres_rr=0`, while exact original mixed coefficients give `Kres_0r>1/1000`. The 2x2 principal minor is negative.

The second result is a full mixed-matrix obstruction after the summed first-query screen passes. No third repair was investigated.

## Stronger common necessary cut: the whole positive-addition class fails

Main proposed a common cut during this same call; it was independently checked and is proved in REPORT Section 8. It is not a third coefficient law.

Let `C_ij>=0` be ANY zero-query-divergence addition to the fixed fan, not necessarily dyadic. Set `N_C=diag(Ce)-C`, `B_C=sym(N_C L)`. At the global left set `I=[0,r)`, `z=1_I`, `H=sum_(i<r)h_i`, `R=h_r`, `t=L e`,

`z^T B_C z=-sum_(j outside I,i in I) C_ji(H-t_i)`,

`(B_C)_rr=sum_(i<r)C_ri-(R-1)sum_(j>r)C_rj`.

Since `H-t_i>=a`, these imply `z^T B_C z+a(B_C)_rr<=0`. The base contributes `sum_(i<r)mu_i t_i+a mu_r`. With `A=sum_(i<r)mu_i=1/c`, `u=mu_r=2zeta`, PSD would therefore require

`(A^2+a u^2)/(4q) <=sum_(i<r)mu_i t_i+a u`.

The fixed source gives right side `<5H/24+37/60`. Independently, `D>24H/5`, `A>2/5`, `a>2/5`, and `u>17/60>7/25` give left side `>27H/125`. Thus

`z^T Kres z+a Kres_rr <37/60-23H/3000 < -H/150 <0`.

This excludes every nonnegative balanced query addition to this same fan/source, for every `m>=1536`, whatever the landing weights or intensities. It includes both laws above and all allowed positive dyadic circulations. No further boundary-weight tuning inside that class can fix it.

## Exact escape boundary and scope

The common proof requires entrywise retention of the base fan `lambda0_(l,i)=mu_i`, fixed source `mu`, and `nu=0`. It does NOT exclude a reselected nonnegative original array whose difference from the fan has negative entries on some old fan edges, or a changed source/minimizer choice. With the same source, a successful certificate must reduce at least one old terminal-fan edge; simply adding another circulation is excluded.

This boundary is stronger than the predecessor's proportional-landing-only failure. All negative directions are formal Gram directions, not legal GD functions. No full-class upper bound, exact interpolable counterexample, or original-goal resolution is claimed. The two laws and common-cut proof are JOINT UNVERIFIED pending any separately authorized review.

REPORT contains the complete constants, all-ancestor accounting, full original coefficients, quadratic normalization proof and corrected `N^T` convention. No program or raw array was used. Parent closes the analytical ledger. **STOP; original target OPEN; no renewal.**

# Boundary-clock cycles: two exact failures and the common positive-addition obstruction

**JOINT UNVERIFIED. Original target OPEN.** One fresh `gpt-6-astra` / `ultra` analytical invocation under guidance `1eaef2e700c54cd4`. Two explicit laws, zero mathematical programs, array reads, solves or formal submissions. Parent owns registration and closeout.

The linear-clock law fails after summing the traffic from all ancestors of query zero. The quadratic-clock law with reduced intensities passes that complete first-query screen, but has a zero root Schur diagonal and a nonzero mixed entry, so the full matrix is indefinite.

A common cut, proposed by the parent during this invocation and independently checked here, gives the stronger result: with the fixed source and fixed terminal fan of this task, **no nonnegative zero-divergence addition on the query graph can yield PSD**. This includes arbitrary landing distributions and intensities in the allowed dyadic cycles. The two concrete laws and their distinct failure mechanisms are retained below. The stronger result concerns the fixed-base positive-addition class, not all original certificates, all source profiles, the weighted-prefix inequality itself, or the original minimax goal.

## 1. Original problem, coefficients, and the independently checked capacity

Fix one enclosing `m>=1536`, keeping the following parameters unchanged in every child:

\[
\varepsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad\beta=1-\varepsilon,\quad
c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\varepsilon),\quad a=\beta/c,\quad b=1+a,
\]
\[
H_0=(),\quad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j),\quad S_j=\sum H_j,
\quad D=1+2S_m,\quad q=\frac{17}{16D}.
\]

Write `h=H_m` and delete only the final update `h_l=b`. The retained queries are `0,...,l`, with `l=2^m-2`; the actual prefix steps are `k_i=h_i` for `i<l`. The desired sufficient estimate is

\[
2(f_l-f_\star)-(1-a^2)\|g_l\|^2\le q\|x_0-x_\star\|^2.
\tag{1.1}
\]

After translation, take the selected minimizer and its value to be zero, without restarting the trajectory or changing its initial radius. All distinct original rows remain

\[
Q_{ij}=f_i-f_j+\langle g_i,x_j-x_i\rangle
+\tfrac12\|g_i-g_j\|^2\le0,
\qquad x_i=x_0-\sum_{t<i}k_tg_t.
\]

Set `mu_i=lambda_(i,star)`, `nu_i=lambda_(star,i)`, `N_ij=-lambda_ij` off diagonal and `N_ii=mu_i+sum_(j!=i)lambda_ij`. Let `L` be lower triangular with diagonal one and `L_ij=k_j` for `j<i`. Exact value flow is `N e=mu`, `N^T e=2e_l+nu`. Writing `B=sym(NL)`, the complete original polynomial is

\[
\mathcal M=q\|x_0\|^2-\left\langle x_0,\sum_i\mu_i g_i\right\rangle
+g^T(B-a^2e_le_l^T)g,
\]

and its full Schur residual is

\[
\boxed{\mathcal K=B-a^2e_le_l^T-\frac{\mu\mu^T}{4q}.}
\tag{1.2}
\]

These are the full matrices on the original independent formal vectors, including all mixed terms. No quotient, rank, diagonal-residual, primitive-energy or sharp-Huber-contact restriction is used. Once the literal multipliers below are given, (1.2) defines every matrix entry without a completion oracle.

The lead's necessary first-query capacity is correct. If `out_0` sums only outgoing query rows, the first physical step gives

\[
B_{00}=\mu_0-a\,\mathrm{out}_0.
\]

PSD of (1.2) therefore forces

\[
0\le\mu_0\le4q,\qquad
\mathrm{out}_0\le \mathcal C_0:=\frac{\mu_0-\mu_0^2/(4q)}a\le\frac qa.
\tag{1.3}
\]

The last inequality is the maximum of the displayed concave quadratic at `mu_0=2q`. Value flow at the nonterminal first query gives `in_0=mu_0+out_0-nu_0`. Its upper bound is the maximum of

`(1+a)mu_0/a-mu_0^2/(4qa)`

on `[0,4q]`. The maximizer `2q(1+a)` lies in that interval because `0<a<1`, and the maximum is `q(1+a)^2/a`. Thus the lead's incoming bound also follows without contact or sparsity assumptions.

The actual last update has not been replaced: `x_after=x_l-bg_l` and smoothness gives `2f_after<=2f_l-(1-a^2)||g_l||^2`. A proof of (1.1) at all sufficiently large enclosing depths would have the conditional implication described in accepted `931b9653ce0f7201`. This report supplies no such proof.

## 2. Fixed hierarchical source and original base fan

For `j>=1`, let `u^(j)=H_j/S_j`, the probability vector proportional to the physical step weights. Define

\[
\alpha=\frac1{2c},\quad\gamma=\frac1{\sqrt c},\quad
\zeta=1-\alpha-\gamma,\quad
\pi^{(1)}=(1),\quad
\pi^{(j)}=(\alpha u^{(j-1)},\zeta,\gamma\pi^{(j-1)}).
\]

Both laws use the task's fixed source `mu=2pi^(m)`, `nu=0` and minimizer rows `lambda_(i,star)=mu_i`, `lambda_(star,i)=0`. This is a deliberately restricted construction, not a necessity for the relaxed target.

The fixed query fan is

\[
\lambda^0_{ij}=\mathbf1_{i=l}\mu_j\quad(i\ne j).
\tag{2.1}
\]

It contributes divergence `-mu_i` at `i<l` and `2-mu_l` at `l`; with the minimizer rows its value flow is exactly the target. Every source is positive.

At the global root, define

\[
r=2^{m-1}-1,\quad H=S_{m-1},\quad R=1+ac^{m-1},
\quad A=\sum_{i<r}\mu_i=\frac1c,\quad u=\mu_r=2\zeta.
\]

The entire root-left block is proportional to the physical clock weights:

\[
\mu_i=\frac{h_i}{cH}\quad(i<r),\qquad
\mu_0=\frac b{cH}.
\tag{2.2}
\]

The elementary parameter bounds needed in the proofs are

\[
\frac{12}{5}<c<\frac52,\qquad
\frac25<a<\frac37,\qquad
\frac75<b<\frac{10}{7},\qquad\beta>\frac{99}{100},
\quad\frac14<u<\frac25.
\tag{2.3}
\]

For `a>2/5`, use `epsilon<1/69`, `(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon)>34/35` and `1+sqrt(2)<17/7`. For its upper bound, `a<sqrt(2)-1<3/7`. The bounds on `c` follow from `epsilon<1/100` and `7/5<sqrt(2)<10/7`. Finally `alpha<5/24`, `gamma<2/3` give `zeta>1/8`, while `alpha>1/5`, `gamma>3/5` give `zeta<1/5`.

Put `v=a/(c-2)`. Direct substitution in the word mass recursion gives

\[
1+S_j=(1-v)2^j+vc^j,\qquad 0<v<1,\quad v>2a.
\tag{2.4}
\]

Here `v=beta/[c(c-2)]<1` and `c-2<1/2`. At any bridge of depth `j>=2`, write `R_j=1+ac^(j-1)` and `s_j=S_(j-1)`. Then

\[
\frac{R_j}{s_j}>\frac25,\qquad s_j>2R_j-3.
\]

At the enclosing root, `H>4096`, `R>3`, and hence

\[
R<H,\qquad D>4H,\qquad D<5H+4<\frac{81}{16}H,
\qquad \frac{17}{81H}<q<\frac{17}{64H}.
\tag{2.5}
\]

The root upper bound uses `R<(H+3)/2` and `H>64`. These formulas keep the same enclosing parameters at every scale.

## 3. The two complete clock-weighted laws

Use the actual dyadic intervals of the full word. For a nonleaf node `v`, let `r_v` be its bridge, and `L_v,R_v` its left and right query sets. For either block `J`, use only as coefficient weights the original positive `h_i` and the local clock

\[
t_J(i)=1+\sum_{j\in J,\,j<i}h_j,
\qquad Z_s(J)=\sum_{i\in J}h_i t_J(i)^s.
\]

The final deleted update's `h_l=b` remains a proof weight at its retained query; it is never reinserted into the GD prefix or `L`.

For the selected power `s`, define strictly positive probabilities

\[
p^L_v(i)=\frac{h_i t_{L_v}(i)^s}{Z_s(L_v)},\qquad
p^R_v(j)=\frac{h_j t_{R_v}(j)^s}{Z_s(R_v)}.
\]

To the fixed fan (2.1), at every nonleaf node add exactly

\[
\begin{array}{ll}
r_v\to i\in L_v:&\theta_v p^L_v(i),\\
i\in L_v\to j\in R_v:&\theta_v p^L_v(i)p^R_v(j),\\
j\in R_v\to r_v:&\theta_v p^R_v(j).
\end{array}
\tag{3.1}
\]

Contributions to a repeated original row are added. All unlisted query rows are zero, and the minimizer rows are fixed as in Section 2. Each cycle has zero divergence at every query: the bridge has both total masses `theta_v`, each left query both masses `theta_v p^L_v(i)`, and each right query both masses `theta_v p^R_v(j)`. Thus all coefficient signs and exact value flow are proved for both laws. Local clocks are coefficient labels, not restarted trajectories.

The two complete choices are

\[
\boxed{\text{Law 1: }s=1,\qquad\theta_v=\frac{\mu_{r_v}^2}{4q}.}
\tag{L1}
\]
\[
\boxed{\text{Law 2: }s=2,\qquad
\theta_v=\left[\frac{\mu_{r_v}^2}{4q}-\mu_{r_v}\right]_+.}
\tag{L2}
\]

Here `[x]_+=max(x,0)` specifies every node completely; no intensity is an optimization variable. The second law is a changed intensity as well as a changed landing rule. No further law was examined.

## 4. Law 1: the full ancestor sum violates query-zero PSD

The nodes containing query zero are exactly the leftmost ancestors of depths `j=2,...,m`; query zero is in each left child. Consequently its complete outgoing query mass is

\[
\mathrm{out}_0=\sum_{j=2}^{m}\theta_j\frac b{Z_1(H_{j-1})}.
\tag{4.1}
\]

No contribution from an ancestor is omitted in this identity. For every proper such ancestor `j<m`, (2.2) gives

\[
\mu_{r_j}=\frac{R_j}{cH},\qquad
\theta_j=\frac{R_j^2}{4qc^2H^2}.
\]

For a positive word of mass `s`, direct expansion gives the lead's exact normalization

\[
Z_1=s+\frac{s^2-\sum_i h_i^2}{2}.
\]

For `s=s_j>=b>7/5`, this implies `Z_1<(5/4)s_j^2`. Since `R_j/s_j>2/5`,

\[
\frac{R_j^2}{Z_1(H_{j-1})}>\frac{16}{125}.
\]

Therefore each of the `m-2` proper nonleaf ancestors contributes more than

\[
\frac{4b}{125qc^2H^2}
>\frac{112}{15625H}>\frac1{160H}.
\tag{4.2}
\]

Here we used `q<1/H`, `c<5/2`, and `b>7/5`. The root contribution in (4.1) is nonnegative and is retained in the law. Thus

\[
\mathrm{out}_0>\frac{m-2}{160H}.
\]

Since `mu_0-mu_0^2/(4q)<=q`, the actual full Schur diagonal satisfies

\[
\mathcal K_{00}
\le q-a\,\mathrm{out}_0
<\frac1H\left[\frac{17}{64}-\frac{m-2}{400}\right]
<-\frac3H<0
\tag{4.3}
\]

for every `m>=1536`. The failure is the accumulated load from many scales, not the predecessor's single-root order mismatch. This negative Schur diagonal yields a formal negative direction with only `g0` nonzero and `x0=mu_0 g0/(2q)`; no legal function is inferred.

## 5. A normalization bound for the second law

For every full child word `H_d` of mass `s`, we need

\[
\boxed{Z_2(H_d)>s^3/4.}
\tag{5.1}
\]

This is derived here, not imported from a generic energy theorem. Write `Q_r=sum_i h_i^r`. Because the word is palindromic, its paired clocks satisfy `t_i+t_reverse=s+2-h_i`. Telescoping successive cubes gives exactly

\[
Z_2=\frac{(1+s)^3-1}{3}-\frac{s+2}{2}Q_2+\frac16Q_3.
\tag{5.2}
\]

Counting the two copies of each child, or substituting in the finite square-mass recursion, gives

\[
Q_2=2s-(2^d-1)+a^2\frac{c^{2d}-2^d}{c^2-2}
<2s+\frac{(s+1)^2}{17}.
\]

For the last bound, use `s+1>=v c^d`, `v=a/(c-2)`, and `(c-2)^2/(c^2-2)<1/17` for `12/5<c<5/2`. Substitution in (5.2), retaining only the nonnegative `Q_3/6` as a lower-bound omission, gives

\[
Z_2>\frac{31}{102}s^3-\frac2{17}s^2-\frac{39}{34}s-\frac1{17}.
\tag{5.3}
\]

For `d>=3`, `s>=S_3=7+4a+2beta+beta c>12`. After division by `s^3`, the three subtracted terms in (5.3) are respectively less than `1/100,1/100,1/1000`. Since `31/102-21/1000>1/4`, (5.1) follows.

For `d=1`, `Z_2=b>b^3/4`. For `d=2`, write `d_0=1+beta`, so `s=2b+d_0<5` and

\[
Z_2=b+d_0(1+b)^2+b(1+b+d_0)^2
>\frac{847}{25}>\frac{125}{4}>s^3/4.
\]

The lower bound uses `b>7/5`, `d_0>7/4`, `1+b>12/5`, and `1+b+d_0>4`. This completes all cases without any numerical recurrence evaluation.

## 6. Law 2: every query-zero ancestor is controlled

Again the complete first-query mass is (4.1), now with `Z_2` and the clipped intensities of (L2). The depth-two proper ancestor is inactive: with `R_2=1+beta<2`,

\[
\frac{\mu_{r_2}}{4q}=\frac{4DR_2}{17cH}<\frac{135}{136}<1,
\]

by (2.5), so `theta_2=0`. For all other proper ancestors the bound

\[
0\le\theta_j\le\frac{R_j^2}{4qc^2H^2}
\]

holds whether or not the positive part is active.

For `j>=3`, `R_j/s_j<3/4`. At `j=3`, `4R_3<14` while `3s_3=3S_2>1437/100>14`. For `j>=4`, `R_j>9/2` and `s_j>2R_j-3>4R_j/3`. Combining with (5.1) yields

\[
\frac{R_j^2}{Z_2(H_{j-1})}<\frac9{4S_{j-1}}.
\]

The masses more than double, `S_(d+1)>2S_d`, and `S_2>91/20`. Thus

\[
\sum_{j=3}^{m-1}\frac9{4S_{j-1}}
<\frac94\frac2{S_2}<1.
\]

The total contribution of all proper ancestors is consequently less than

\[
\frac b{4qc^2H^2}<\frac3{10H}.
\tag{6.1}
\]

The last comparison uses `b<10/7`, `q>17/(81H)`, and `c>12/5`. The full-root contribution is also included: since `u<2/5`,

\[
\theta_m\frac b{Z_2(H)}
\le\frac{u^2}{4q}\frac{4b}{H^3}
<\frac2{H^2}<\frac1{30H}.
\tag{6.2}
\]

Equations (6.1)-(6.2), together with the inactive depth-two node, bound the entire ancestor sum:

\[
\mathrm{out}_0<\frac1{3H}.
\]

The actual capacity is larger. Bounds (2.3)-(2.5) give

\[
\frac{\mu_0}{4q}=\frac{4bD}{17cH}<\frac{675}{952}<\frac57,
\qquad\mu_0>\frac{14}{25H},\quad a<\frac37.
\]

Therefore

\[
\mathcal C_0=\frac{\mu_0}{a}\left(1-\frac{\mu_0}{4q}\right)
>\frac{28}{75H},
\qquad
\boxed{\mathcal K_{00}=a(\mathcal C_0-\mathrm{out}_0)>\frac2{125H}>0.}
\tag{6.3}
\]

Thus the quadratic-clock change repairs the FULL first-query ancestor load, not just its leading order or one root term.

## 7. Law 2 still fails a full mixed block

At the full root, the bridge `r` belongs to no proper child interval. Its circulation sends only to earlier labels. Since `u>1/4>4q`, the root's positive part is active and

\[
\theta_m=\frac{u^2}{4q}-u,\qquad
B_{rr}=u+\theta_m,\qquad\mathcal K_{rr}=0.
\tag{7.1}
\]

This zero is produced by the explicit intensity law, not by a sharp-Huber contact assumption.

Let `p_0=b/Z_2(H)` be its actual left landing weight at query zero. Retaining the original mixed coefficients gives

\[
(NL)_{r0}=bu+a\theta_m p_0,
\qquad (NL)_{0r}=-R\theta_m p_0.
\]

The first equality uses the original first column `(1,b,...,b)^T` and `N e=mu`. For the second, no proper ancestor of query zero reaches beyond the root-left block; the only outgoing rows from zero to indices greater than `r` are the full-root left-to-right rows. No row from zero to the root bridge exists in these cycles. These are exact support identities accounting for all ancestors.

Consequently the full Schur entry is

\[
2\mathcal K_{0r}
=bu\left(1-\frac{8D}{17cH}\right)
-(R-a)\theta_m p_0.
\tag{7.2}
\]

The first term is greater than `bu/136>7/2720>1/400`, since `8D/(17cH)<135/136`. The second term is positive but less than

\[
\frac{Rbu^2}{qH^3}<\frac2H<\frac1{2000}.
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{\mathcal K_{0r}>\frac1{1000},\qquad\mathcal K_{rr}=0.}
\tag{7.3}
\]

The principal minor on original gradient labels `{0,r}` has determinant `-K_0r^2<-1/1000000`. This excludes full PSD despite (6.3). Neither gradient was eliminated, and no mixed term was dropped. The negative direction is formal Gram data, not a legal function trajectory.

## 8. Stronger common obstruction: no positive circulation addition can work

The parent proposed this common cut during the invocation. The following independent derivation strengthens the scope without introducing a third law or another mathematical program.

Retain the exact source, minimizer rows and base fan (2.1). Let `C_ij>=0` be ANY query-row addition satisfying zero query divergence,

\[
\sum_jC_{ij}=\sum_jC_{ji},\qquad \lambda_{ij}=\lambda^0_{ij}+C_{ij}.
\tag{8.1}
\]

All dyadic cycles allowed by the task, with arbitrary nonnegative intensities and arbitrary probability weights, belong to this class. Let `N_C=diag(Ce)-C`, `B_C=sym(N_C L)`. Then `N_C e=N_C^T e=0`.

Take the global left set `I=[0,r)`, `z=1_I`, and original clock `t=L e`. Its incoming and outgoing `C` masses agree. Also `(Lz)_i=t_i` on `I` and equals `H` outside. Expanding row/column sums therefore gives

\[
z^TB_Cz=-\sum_{j\notin I,\,i\in I}C_{ji}(H-t_i).
\tag{8.2}
\]

The original root diagonal is

\[
(B_C)_{rr}=\sum_{i<r}C_{ri}-(R-1)\sum_{j>r}C_{rj}.
\]

Since `H-t_i>=a`, combining these exact identities yields

\[
\boxed{z^TB_Cz+a(B_C)_{rr}\le0.}
\tag{8.3}
\]

In particular, this conclusion does not use a clock-power landing rule. The fan alone has

\[
z^TB_0z=\sum_{i<r}\mu_i t_i,\qquad (B_0)_{rr}=u.
\]

Full Schur PSD would imply `z^TBz>=A^2/(4q)` and `B_rr>=u^2/(4q)`. Thus every construction in (8.1) would necessarily satisfy

\[
\boxed{\frac{A^2+a u^2}{4q}\le\sum_{i<r}\mu_i t_i+a u.}
\tag{8.4}
\]

For the task's fixed left source, the right side is at most

\[
\frac AH\sum_{i<r}h_i t_i+a u
\le A\left(1+\frac H2\right)+a u
<\frac{5H}{24}+\frac{37}{60}.
\tag{8.5}
\]

The left side is strictly larger. First `R/H>a>2/5`, so `D>24H/5`. Moreover `gamma<13/20` follows from `c>12/5` and `5/12<169/400`; together with `alpha<5/24`, it gives `u=2(1-alpha-gamma)>17/60>7/25`. Using also `A>2/5` and `a>2/5`,

\[
\frac{A^2+a u^2}{4q}
>\frac{96H}{85}\left(\frac4{25}+\frac{98}{3125}\right)
>\frac{27H}{125}.
\tag{8.6}
\]

The final rational comparison is `57408/265625>57375/265625=27/125`. For `H>4096`, (8.5)-(8.6) contradict (8.4). Equivalently the complete residual obeys the uniform negative combined test

\[
\boxed{z^T\mathcal Kz+a\mathcal K_{rr}
<\frac{37}{60}-\frac{23H}{3000}<-\frac H{150}<0.}
\tag{8.7}
\]

The terminal penalty vanishes on both tested columns because `z_l=0` and `r<l`. A PSD matrix would make both terms nonnegative. Thus the ENTIRE fixed-base positive-circulation class is excluded, beyond the two chosen clock laws.

## 9. Exact scope and the changed next interface

The stronger obstruction depends on retaining the fixed source `mu=2pi^(m)`, zero incoming minimizer weights, and every original terminal fan edge `lambda^0_(l,i)=mu_i` as an entrywise lower bound. It does not exclude an arbitrary exact-flow nonnegative array with that source: the difference from the fan may have negative entries on the fan's existing edges. It also does not exclude changed source/minimizer choices.

Consequently another landing measure or another intensity inside the present positive-addition mechanism cannot evade (8.7). A successful construction with this same source would need to reselect the query rows so that at least one old terminal-fan edge is reduced below its fixed value. That possibility was not investigated; it is outside the two completed laws. The earlier specific query-zero explanation remains correct, but the common cut is the stronger conclusion for this task's whole construction class.

All negative tests are formal full-matrix obstructions. They are not interpolation-feasible GD witnesses and do not refute (1.1) or the original full last-iterate objective bound. The original minimax target remains OPEN. The conditional benchmark separation cannot be invoked without a genuine full-function all-depth upper certificate.

## 10. Evidence and bounded closeout

Read the current operator profile, original problem, complete predecessor report/handoff, and the supplied boundary-transport lead. The predecessor's precise scope was retained; its outputs remain JOINT UNVERIFIED rather than accepted facts. The lead's first-query bounds and normalizer identity were independently checked in Sections 1 and 4. The common parent-proposed cut was independently derived in Section 8.

# Fresh analytical allocation: boundary-suppressed dyadic return transport

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. Original target OPEN. ONE NEW native gpt-6-astra/ultra invocation, consult OFF, up to TWO explicit laws in ONE mechanism. Mathematical programs, array loads, solvers, scans and formal submissions ZERO. Parent owns this analytical ledger. ## Completed predecessor and precise reopening condition

Main FULL-read the final research/prefix_source_profile_search_20261008_r3/REPORT.md (SHA7c573bc6ea94b80a4fe652d75d78f4429f4d27af00094a8dd93a1554bfbf8be1) and HANDOFF.md (SHA95c8c0d508424ff3ae2b422ccd1448792eb00cab2463ff93977cd639ca55c49d). Read both. They are JOINT UNVERIFIED, not accepted facts. That allocation is CLOSED: one extraction,20accounted seconds,1.2740811170006054actual,zero solves/formal,then two completed laws.

Its new hierarchical source passes the old first-source and full-root cuts. Symmetric product circulations fail the root Schur diagonal. # R4 handoff: original-flow reselection with full corridor payments

**JOINT UNVERIFIED. Original target OPEN.** Guidance `d776a946dfa9f071`; one fresh `gpt-6-astra` / `ultra` analytical call. Two complete laws exhausted, program/array/solve/formal counts zero. Parent owns registration and closeout.

TASK SHA256: `5c4c44ffcdf442f830a0d31451b92c40edc9f85d2d9d47e4438a0886842fad0d`.

## Fixed coordinates and source

All children keep the same enclosing `m>=1536` parameters `eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c, b=1+a`. The prefix deletes only the last update, retaining final query `l=2^m-2`. The target is `q=17/(16D)`, `D=1+2sum H_m`, for `2f_l-(1-a^2)||g_l||^2` at the original radius.

The source remains `mu=2pi^(m)`, `nu=0`, with the task's `alpha=1/(2c)`, `gamma=1/sqrt(c)`, `zeta=1-alpha-gamma`, `pi^(1)=(1)`, `pi^(j)=(alpha H_(j-1)/S_(j-1),zeta,gamma pi^(j-1))`. At the full root `k=2^(m-1)-1`, `H=S_(m-1)`, `e=k-1`, one has `A=sum_(i<k)mu_i=1/c`, `u=mu_k=2zeta`, and `mu_i=h_i/(cH)` inside the left block.

All final original multipliers are nonnegative and have exact value flow. The complete matrix is always the original `N,L,B=sym(NL)`, with `K=B-a^2 e_l e_l^T-mu mu^T/(4q)`; no gradient, mixed term or original row is removed.

## Necessary rerouting independently checked

For any PSD certificate with this source, the original root cut requires

`sum_(i<k)(H-t_i)(mu_i-lambda_(l,i))_+ >H/150`,

and hence `sum_(i<k)(mu_i-lambda_(l,i))_+ >1/150`. REPORT Section 2 derives this from the exact original cut and its uniform scalar deficit. It is necessary, not sufficient.

## Changed base and complete payments

The common absorbing relay has, at every nonleaf dyadic interval `I`, edges `e_I -> e_(I_L)` with mass `A_I=sum_(i in I_L)mu_i`, and `e_I -> r_I` with mass `mu_(r_I)`, recursively in both children. Duplicate rows are summed; singletons absorb their own source through their minimizer row. The exact subinterval query divergence is `M_I e_(e_I)-mu|_I`.

The old terminal fan is actually replaced: at the root, only `lambda_(l,e)=A` goes directly into the left block. Both final laws remove more than `1/3` of old terminal-left weight and more than `H/6` in the weighted removal test. They are not positive additions to the old fixed fan.

Define the explicit balanced adjacent corridor `Corr(s,t;T)`: for `s<=i<t`, add in BOTH directions of `(i,i+1)` the weight

`J_i=T product_(r=i+1)^(t-1)(h_r-1)`.

It adds `+T` at its terminal gradient diagonal, `-T product_(r=s)^(t-1)(h_r-1)` at its initial diagonal, and zero at every interior diagonal, with all adjacent mixed terms retained.

Both complete laws add the terminal corridor `Corr(0,l;T_star)`, where `T_star=a^2+mu_l^2/(4q)<6`. This makes `K_ll=2` exactly. At every nonleaf node use the fully defined intensity

`T_I=[mu_(r_I)^2/(4q)-mu_(r_I)]_+ +q`.

The two laws are:

1. Add `Corr(e_(I_L),r_I;T_I)`, a single adjacent pair at each node.
2. Add `Corr(min I,r_I;T_I)`, traversing the entire actual left block at each node.

The relay alone was audited as a base, not counted as a third law. All completions are nonnegative balanced additions to the NEW base. Both final arrays pass the actual aggregate root test `z^T Kz+aK_kk>H/17`, and both have the derived root margin `K_kk=q>0` and terminal margin `K_ll=2`.

## Decisive exact failures

**Law 1:** Every first-query contribution is included. Only the depth-two node pair and the global terminal corridor touch query zero, giving `K_00>1/(100H)>0`. But the last root-left receiver has exactly `B_ee=A-a T_root<-D/340<0`. Proper local pairs do not touch that receiver, and the global terminal corridor cancels at its interior diagonal. This is a full original-matrix negative direction.

**Law 2:** The full first diagonal retains all leftmost-ancestor losses and the global terminal loss:

`K_00=mu_0-mu_0^2/(4q)-sum_(j=2)^m T_j P_(j-1)-T_star P_pre`.

The exact product is `P_j=beta^(2^j-1)c^(-j)`, `P_pre=P_m/a`. The actual depth-four node `[0,15)` alone has `T_4 P_3>3/(10H)`, forcing `K_00<-11/(320H)<0`. No ancestor cost or mixed term was discarded from the matrix definition.

These formal negative directions need not satisfy smooth-convex interpolation and are not GD function counterexamples. The proofs do not exclude arbitrary original-flow reselection or the fixed source itself.

## Exact algebra retained for the direct-proof priority

The complete original-row corridor identity is

`sum_(i=s)^(t-1) J_i(Q_(i,i+1)+Q_(i+1,i))`

`=T||g_t||^2-T product_(r=s)^(t-1)(h_r-1)||g_s||^2`

` +sum_(i=s)^(t-1)J_i(h_i-2)<g_i,g_(i+1)>`.

This retains all signed mixed terms and can be inserted literally into an independently developed all-depth argument. It is not a positive energy or a convergence proof by itself. The exact absorbing-relay divergence identity above is also available as algebra, not an upper bound. No claim that either identity closes the Zhang–Jiang comparison is made.

Following the latest steering, no further cosmetic routing variant, equivalent program, finite toy experiment or third law is proposed. REPORT gives the complete coefficients, all-depth constants, checks of the original terminal and ancestor traffic, and both scoped failures. Original target **OPEN**. Parent closes this analytical ledger. **STOP; no renewal.**

# R4: absorbing relays with explicit diagonal completions

**JOINT UNVERIFIED. Original target OPEN.** One fresh `gpt-6-astra` / `ultra` analytical invocation under guidance `d776a946dfa9f071`. Two complete laws were fixed and checked; mathematical programs, array reads, solves and formal calls were zero. The acyclic relay below is a common base, not a third candidate. Parent owns registration and closeout.

Both constructions genuinely reselect the old terminal fan. They remove more than `1/3` of its terminal-to-root-left weight and pass the actual root aggregate test with margin greater than `H/17`. They also explicitly pay the final retained query's Schur diagonal and the physical bridges. Nevertheless:

- Law 1 passes the first-query capacity but produces `B_ee<-D/340` at the last query of the global left block.
- Law 2 moves that payment along adjacent original rows; a fixed depth-four ancestor then produces `K_00<-11/(320H)`.

These are failures of the two stated full coefficient arrays, not exclusions of arbitrary reselection, not legal-function counterexamples, and not a resolution of the minimax target. The latest steering is retained: finish these two laws and preserve exact algebra relevant to a direct proof, without opening another routing variant.

## 1. Fixed objective, source and complete original matrix

For enclosing `m>=1536`, keep in every child

\[
\varepsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad\beta=1-\varepsilon,\quad
c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\varepsilon),\quad a=\beta/c,\quad b=1+a,
\]
\[
H_0=(),\quad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j),\quad S_j=\sum H_j,
\quad h=H_m,\quad D=1+2S_m,\quad q=\frac{17}{16D}.
\]

Only the last update `h_l=b` is removed, where `l=2^m-2`. Queries `0,...,l` remain. In normalized smoothness one, with the selected minimum translated to zero, the sufficient target is

\[
2f_l-(1-a^2)\|g_l\|^2\le q\|x_0\|^2.
\tag{1.1}
\]

All original ordered rows on `star,0,...,l` remain

\[
Q_{ij}=f_i-f_j+\langle g_i,x_j-x_i\rangle
+\tfrac12\|g_i-g_j\|^2\le0,
\quad x_i=x_0-\sum_{t<i}h_tg_t.
\]

The last formula uses only indices `t<l`; no removed update is reinserted. The actual last step still satisfies `2f_after<=2f_l-(1-a^2)||g_l||^2`. Thus an eventual full-class all-depth proof of (1.1), in the original `W=2R` normalization, would have the conditional benchmark implication of accepted `931b9653ce0f7201`. It remains unproved here.

Use the task-fixed source

\[
u^{(j)}=H_j/S_j,\quad\alpha=1/(2c),\quad\gamma=1/\sqrt c,
\quad\zeta=1-\alpha-\gamma,
\]
\[
\pi^{(1)}=(1),\quad\pi^{(j)}=(\alpha u^{(j-1)},\zeta,\gamma\pi^{(j-1)}),
\qquad\mu=2\pi^{(m)},\qquad\nu=0.
\tag{1.2}
\]

Every `mu_i` is positive and their sum is two. Fix `lambda_(i,star)=mu_i`, `lambda_(star,i)=0`. The chosen zero incoming-star profile is a construction restriction; with the same `sum mu=2`, nonnegative exact-flow incoming-star rows would in any event have total zero.

For the final literal query coefficients, let `L` be lower triangular and put

\[
N_{ij}=-\lambda_{ij}\ (i\ne j),\quad
N_{ii}=\mu_i+\sum_{j\ne i}\lambda_{ij},\quad
L_{ii}=1,\ L_{ij}=h_j\ (j<i),\quad B=\operatorname{sym}(NL).
\]

Exact flow is `N e=mu`, `N^T e=2e_l`. The full original polynomial is

\[
q\|x_0\|^2-2f_l+(1-a^2)\|g_l\|^2+\sum\lambda_{ij}Q_{ij}
=q\|x_0\|^2-\left\langle x_0,\sum_i\mu_i g_i\right\rangle
+g^T(B-a^2e_le_l^T)g.
\]

Completing the original initial-radius square gives

\[
\boxed{\mathcal K=B-a^2e_le_l^T-\frac{\mu\mu^T}{4q}.}
\tag{1.3}
\]

The multiplier rules below and this formula define every entry of the full matrix. No rank restriction, diagonal remainder, gradient elimination, sharp-Huber contact, or unspecified completion oracle is used.

## 2. Scalar bounds and independent check of necessary rerouting

At the global root write

\[
k=2^{m-1}-1,\quad H=S_{m-1},\quad R=h_k=1+ac^{m-1},
\quad e=k-1,\quad z=\mathbf1_{[0,k)},
\]
\[
A=\sum_{i<k}\mu_i=1/c,\qquad u=\mu_k=2\zeta,
\qquad\mu_i=h_i/(cH)\quad(i<k).
\]

In particular `mu_0=mu_e=b/(cH)`. The elementary bounds, all with the same enclosing parameters, are

\[
12/5<c<5/2,\quad2/5<a<3/7,\quad7/5<b<10/7,
\quad\beta>99/100,\quad1/4<u<2/5.
\tag{2.1}
\]

They follow from `7/5<sqrt(2)<10/7`, `epsilon<1/100`, and `(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon)>34/35`. More precisely, `gamma<13/20` and `alpha<5/24` give `u>17/60>7/25`.

With `v=a/(c-2)`, direct substitution in the exact mass recursion gives

\[
1+S_j=(1-v)2^j+vc^j,\qquad0<v<1,\quad v>2a.
\]

Thus `H>4096`, `R/H>a>2/5`, `H>2R-3`, and

\[
\frac{24H}{5}<D<5H+4<\frac{81H}{16},\qquad
\frac{17}{81H}<q<\frac{17}{64H}.
\tag{2.2}
\]

Also `A<5/12`. These facts follow algebraically; no recurrence was executed.

Here independently verify the parent rerouting corollary. Write `t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j` and `w_i=H-t_i` for `i<k`, so `a<=w_i<H`. Exact row and column flow gives

\[
\begin{aligned}
HA+au-z^TBz-aB_{kk}
&=\sum_{j>k,i<k}w_i\lambda_{ji}
+\sum_{i<k}(w_i-a)\lambda_{ki}\\
&\quad+a(R-1)\sum_{j>k}\lambda_{kj}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{2.3}
\]

Every term is nonnegative. Isolating the actual terminal rows in the first sum yields

\[
z^TBz+aB_{kk}
\le\sum_{i<k}\mu_i t_i+au
+\sum_{i<k}w_i(\mu_i-\lambda_{li})_+.
\tag{2.4}
\]

The comparison gap is strict. Exact clock summation gives

\[
\sum_{i<k}\mu_i t_i+au<5H/24+37/60,
\]

while (2.1)-(2.2) and `u>7/25` give

\[
\frac{A^2+au^2}{4q}
>\frac{96H}{85}\left(\frac4{25}+\frac{98}{3125}\right)>27H/125.
\]

Their difference is less than `37/60-23H/3000<-H/150`. Full PSD would make `z^TBz+aB_kk>=(A^2+au^2)/(4q)`. Combining with (2.4) proves exactly

\[
\boxed{\sum_{i<k}w_i(\mu_i-\lambda_{li})_+>H/150,
\qquad\sum_{i<k}(\mu_i-\lambda_{li})_+>1/150.}
\tag{2.5}
\]

This is a necessary reselection amount, not a sufficient construction. All final coefficients must still be nonnegative.

## 3. The genuinely changed absorbing-relay base

For every original dyadic interval `I`, let `e_I` be its last query, `r_I` its bridge, and `I_L,I_R` its left/right children. Write `M_I=sum_(i in I)mu_i` and `A_I=sum_(i in I_L)mu_i`. At every nonleaf interval add the two query rows

\[
e_I\longrightarrow e_{I_L}:A_I,
\qquad e_I\longrightarrow r_I:\mu_{r_I}.
\tag{3.1}
\]

Apply the same rule to both children, noting that the right child's last query is the same `e_I`. Singletons add no query edge. Coincident rows are summed; all other query coefficients are zero. This defines the relay `lambda^rel` explicitly on the original labels. Every edge points to an earlier query and every coefficient is nonnegative.

The exact divergence on a subinterval is

\[
\operatorname{div}(\lambda^{\rm rel}|_I)=M_I e_{e_I}-\mu|_I.
\tag{3.2}
\]

For a singleton this is zero. At a nonleaf, the two new edges contribute `(A_I+mu_(r_I))e_(e_I)-A_I e_(e_L)-mu_(r_I)e_(r_I)`. Adding the left and right induction identities cancels the deliveries and gives (3.2). At the full root `M_I=2`; adding the minimizer rows therefore gives exactly the target flow. No oracle or numerical flow solve is involved.

In this base, a nonleaf bridge absorbs only its own source and has no outgoing query row, so its diagonal is `B^rel_(r_I,r_I)=mu_(r_I)`. Indeed nonleaf bridge indices are odd, whereas nontrivial block-terminal indices are even. A block terminal absorbs its whole delivered mass; at the global terminal `B^rel_ll=2`, and at the last root-left query `B^rel_ee=A`. These observations explain why the base alone is insufficient and why explicit completions are necessary. The base-only DAG is not counted as one of the two completed laws.

The original terminal-to-root-left entries are now

\[
\lambda^{\rm rel}_{l,e}=A,\qquad\lambda^{\rm rel}_{l,i}=0\quad(i<k,\ i\ne e).
\tag{3.3}
\]

They are not the old fan plus a nonnegative addition. In particular `A>mu_e`, and the removed positive fan weight is `A-mu_e>1/3`. Using `w_e=a` and the clock identity, its weighted removed amount is

\[
\sum_{i<k}w_i(\mu_i-\lambda^{\rm rel}_{li})_+
>A(H/2-1)-a\mu_e
>H/5-13/20>H/6.
\tag{3.4}
\]

Thus this base changes the hypothesis responsible for the R3 obstruction by a quantitatively nonzero amount.

## 4. Exact original-row corridor identity

This identity specifies the full completion matrices, including their mixed terms. Put `d_i=h_i-1>0` for actual prefix steps. For original query endpoints `s<t` and intensity `T>=0`, define

\[
J_i=T\prod_{r=i+1}^{t-1}d_r\quad(s\le i<t),
\]

and add `J_i` to BOTH original query rows `(i,i+1)` and `(i+1,i)`. Empty products are one. This is a nonnegative balanced query circulation, denoted `Corr(s,t;T)`.

The exact row-pair identity under the actual GD substitution is

\[
Q_{i,i+1}+Q_{i+1,i}
=-d_i\|g_i\|^2+(d_i-1)\langle g_i,g_{i+1}\rangle+\|g_{i+1}\|^2.
\]

Because `J_(i-1)=d_i J_i`, its complete sum telescopes to

\[
\boxed{
\sum_{i=s}^{t-1}J_i(Q_{i,i+1}+Q_{i+1,i})
=T\|g_t\|^2-T\!\prod_{r=s}^{t-1}d_r\|g_s\|^2
+\sum_{i=s}^{t-1}J_i(d_i-1)\langle g_i,g_{i+1}\rangle.
}
\tag{4.1}
\]

The corridor adds `+T` to its terminal gradient diagonal, `-T product d_r` to its initial diagonal, and zero to every interior diagonal. It also adds the literal adjacent mixed coefficients displayed in (4.1); they are not discarded or assigned a sign. This identity is exact original-row algebra, not by itself a monotone energy or a convergence bound.

For the actual child words, let

\[
P_j=\prod_{i\in H_j}(h_i-1).
\]

The literal recursion `P_(j+1)=P_j^2 ac^j`, `P_0=1`, proves

\[
P_j=\beta^{2^j-1}c^{-j},\qquad
P_{\rm pre}=P_m/a.
\tag{4.2}
\]

The second formula deletes precisely the last update factor `a`; no extra gradient step is added.

## 5. Common terminal completion and the two full laws

The source at the final retained query is `mu_l=2gamma^(m-1)`, hence `mu_l^2=4c^{-(m-1)}`. Define the fully explicit terminal intensity

\[
T_\star=a^2+\frac{\mu_l^2}{4q},
\tag{5.1}
\]

and add `Corr(0,l;T_star)` to the relay. Since `D<2c^m`, this intensity obeys `0<T_star<6`. It makes the final retained query's full Schur diagonal exactly

\[
\mathcal K_{ll}=2+T_\star-a^2-\frac{\mu_l^2}{4q}=2.
\tag{5.2}
\]

It leaves every other interior diagonal unchanged, while its initial diagonal cost is `T_star P_pre`.

At each nonleaf dyadic node define one further explicit intensity

\[
T_I=\left[\frac{\mu_{r_I}^2}{4q}-\mu_{r_I}\right]_++q.
\tag{5.3}
\]

The positive part defines every case, including inactive deficit branches; the final `+q` supplies a rederived strict bridge margin rather than presuming one. Both complete laws retain (3.1), the terminal corridor (5.1), and the fixed minimizer rows. Their only difference is the node completion:

\[
\boxed{\text{Law 1: add }\operatorname{Corr}(e_{I_L},r_I;T_I)\text{ for every nonleaf }I.}
\tag{L1}
\]

Here `e_(I_L)=r_I-1`, so this is a single balanced adjacent pair.

\[
\boxed{\text{Law 2: add }\operatorname{Corr}(\min I,r_I;T_I)\text{ for every nonleaf }I.}
\tag{L2}
\]

This second corridor traverses the entire actual left child before reaching its physical bridge. All coincident original coefficients are summed; all remaining query rows are zero. Equations (3.1), (4.1), and (5.1)-(5.3) give every coefficient and every full matrix entry. Each circulation is balanced and nonnegative, so exact value flow and all final multiplier signs hold for both laws.

No local node completion touches the last query `l`: every nonleaf bridge is earlier than its node's last query. Therefore (5.2) holds in the final array of both laws. At the global bridge, the deficit in (5.3) is active because `u>1/4>4q`, giving

\[
T_{\rm root}=u^2/(4q)-u+q,\qquad\mathcal K_{kk}=q>0.
\tag{5.4}
\]

The terminal corridor cancels at that interior diagonal. In Law 1 no other node pair touches `k`; in Law 2 other node corridors either avoid it or have it as an interior query with zero diagonal contribution. Thus the displayed positive margin is derived from the actual coefficients.

## 6. Both laws pass the reselected aggregate cut

Neither completion changes (3.3): the only new query row out of the global terminal is the terminal corridor's edge to `l-1`, which lies in the global right block. Hence (3.4) and its removed-mass bound remain true.

For the relay base, the only incoming root-left edge from outside is `(l,e)` of weight `A`. Since `w_e=a`,

\[
z^TB^{\rm rel}z=(H-a)A,\qquad B^{\rm rel}_{kk}=u.
\]

Using `A<5/12`, `a<1/2`, `u<2/5` and `D<81H/16` gives

\[
\frac{A^2+au^2}{4q}
<\frac{81H}{68}\frac{913}{3600}<H/3.
\]

Thus the relay's complete root test is

\[
z^T\mathcal K^{\rm rel}z+a\mathcal K^{\rm rel}_{kk}
>H/15-5/24>H/16.
\tag{6.1}
\]

Each local root node completion in either law contributes `-a T_root` to `z^TBz` and `+T_root` to `B_kk`, so it cancels in this combined test. Other local completions lie wholly within the left or right child and contribute zero to it.

The terminal corridor leaves the bridge diagonal unchanged. Its current across the left/root boundary is

\[
J_e=T_\star\prod_{r=k}^{l-1}d_r
=T_\star\beta^{2^{m-1}-1}<6,
\]

by (4.2). Hence it costs only `a J_e<3` in (6.1). Both complete laws therefore satisfy

\[
\boxed{z^T\mathcal Kz+a\mathcal K_{kk}>H/16-3>H/17>0.}
\tag{6.2}
\]

This checks the actual aggregate after all completions. It is a passed necessary test, not PSD.

## 7. Law 1: first query passes, but the late left receiver fails

Only one local node pair reaches query zero: the leftmost depth-two pair `(0,1)`. For its bridge `R_2=1+beta<2`, (2.2) gives

\[
\frac{\mu_1}{4q}=\frac{4DR_2}{17cH}<135/136<1,
\]

so its intensity is exactly `T_2=q`. The entire additional outgoing mass at zero is consequently

\[
\mathrm{out}_0=q+T_\star P_{\rm pre}/a.
\tag{7.1}
\]

This includes the common terminal corridor and accounts for every ancestor: no other local pair has query zero as an endpoint.
